# Parity ledger

xfx is an unofficial behavioral port of
[`vercel-labs/fx`](https://github.com/vercel-labs/fx), pinned to
`580a0c5da9386317251968c09c1cee69e763487a`. This file is the product's truth
about what it can actually do.

Read it this way: **a surface that is not `implemented` here is absent from the
binary.** It is not a hidden flag, a silent no-op, or a stub that returns
success. Deferred rows exist so this document can be honest about the gap
between xfx and upstream, not so the gap can be advertised as a feature.

## Status values

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `implemented` | Complete for the documented contract, with a green acceptance test. |
| `partial` | Present and useful, but narrower than upstream. The row states the limit. |
| `deferred` | Absent from the binary. Not in help, not in a tool schema, not a stub. |

`scripts/check-no-stubs.sh` reconciles this file against the binary in **both**
directions, for commands, tools, and shell slash commands:

- every surface the binary advertises has an `implemented` row here;
- every `implemented` row names a surface the binary really advertises, so
  "implemented" cannot be claimed for something that does not exist;
- no name from a `deferred` row -- including the names listed inside a grouped
  row, such as `delete_file` or `/login` -- is advertised anywhere; and
- no surface name appears in two rows.

`tests/parity.rs` proves the same reconciliation against the *running* binary
rather than against the source text: the parser's own subcommand list, the tool
schemas as they are serialized into a Gateway request, and the rendered help
pages.

## Commands

Upstream's command union is `src/core/cli/cli_surface.zig:58-84`.

| Surface | Kind | Status | Notes and upstream evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `status` | command | implemented | `[--json]`. Model, provider, credential source, permission mode, sandbox, workspace, history turns, step limit. `cli_surface.zig:69`, `output_contracts.zig:489-540`. |
| `doctor` | command | implemented | `[--json]`. Aggregate counts plus `{name,status,detail}` checks: `workspace`, `config`, `provider`, `auth`, `permissions`, `sessions`, `startup`. The `provider` check appears only when the configured provider cannot run -- an unreadable `backend` value, or `backend: llmux` with no usable `llmux_url` -- and **fails**, because every turn on such a machine refuses; it names `xfx setup llmux` or quotes the unreadable value. A provider that works is reported by the snapshot's `provider`/`provider_url` fields instead. The `auth` check asks each provider its own question: the Gateway fails without a bearer credential, llmux passes without one, because a loopback request is accepted keyless. No network I/O: `doctor` stays a command that is always safe to run, and never probes the daemon. The `sessions` check reports how many sessions are recorded, how many directories could not be trusted, and how many staged manifests an interrupted write left behind -- a report, never a repair. `cli_surface.zig:73`, `output_contracts.zig:1209-1285`. |
| `help` | command | implemented | `help`, `--help`, `-h`. Lists only implemented commands. `cli_surface.zig:60`. |
| `ask` | command | implemented | `[--auto\|--yolo] [--json] [--no-save] [--add-dir <PATH>]... [--resume <last\|ID>\|--resume-id <ID>] <prompt>`. A bounded multi-step Gateway turn: ordered assistant text, tool calls executed locally under a permission authority, then exactly one terminal event. Ctrl-C cancels the turn and kills any running command's process group; a second Ctrl-C exits 130. `--no-save`, `--add-dir`, the resume flags, and the permission modes have their own rows. `cli_surface.zig:61`. |
| `interactive` | command | implemented | What a bare `xfx` runs. A line-oriented append shell on the terminal's own canonical mode: it never enters raw mode or the alternate screen, so scrollback is preserved and there is no terminal state to restore. It refuses to start without a terminal on both stdin and stdout, and without a place to record the conversation. Each prompt is one ordinary turn -- same provider, registry, permission authority, and session store as `ask` -- and it owns exactly the seven `slash` rows below. Ctrl-C stops a running turn and a second one exits 130; at the prompt it clears the line, and twice in a row leaves. It has no name to type: the parser reaches it by a bare invocation, which `src/cli.rs` declares as `ADVERTISED_ENTRYPOINTS`. `cli_surface.zig:59`, `app_entry_runtime.zig:224`. `XFX_TUI=1` runs the partial TUI in its place; the shell described here is what every other invocation gets. `xfx --resume <last\|ID>` opens the TUI on a saved session (its own row, session resume); the line shell refuses the flag rather than opening a fresh conversation under it. |
| `session` | command | implemented | `<last\|ID>\|--id <ID> [--json]`. Replays one session's log through its published boundary and cross-checks it against the manifest, then renders bounded turns. Read-only: it creates no profile state, and a session it cannot trust is a named refusal rather than a partial read. With no usable `HOME` -- unset, empty or relative (the `profile home` row) -- it reads nothing and says `xfx session: HOME is not set` on stderr, exit 1, upstream's line with this product's name (`writeLookupFailure`, `src/core/cli/cli_surface.zig:2923-2927@481104fd`; `openFromEnv`, `src/core/session/session_adapter.zig:211-213@481104fd`); under `--json` too, where upstream writes its JSON failure (`:2867-2869`), as every `session` failure here is text on stderr. `cli_surface.zig:76`. |
| `sessions` | command | implemented | `[--json] [--all] [--limit N]`. Newest first with a total order, scoped to the current workspace unless `--all`, bounded at 20 by default and 200 at most. A session directory that cannot be trusted is counted in `skipped_invalid` rather than failing the listing. Read-only. With no usable `HOME` it lists nothing and says `xfx sessions: HOME is not set` on stderr, exit 1, as `session` does; before fx v0.0.16's rule it read a store rooted at a relative `.xfx` -- the workspace's own project directory -- and listed what was there. `cli_surface.zig:77`. Upstream's `sessions convert [--json]`, new in v0.0.16, converts every session saved in fx's previous (v1) store into its v2 one (`src/builtins/commands.zig:192`, `:198@481104fd`). It is not advertised: xfx's store is its own and has one format (`MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` and `STORAGE_FORMAT`, `src/session/store.rs:74`, `:78`, any other refused, `:1364-1375`), so there is no older format to convert, and `xfx sessions convert` is refused as an unexpected argument. The v1-store switches 0.0.16 removed -- `--sessions-v2` (and `FX_SESSIONS_V2`), `session migrate` and `ask --continue-recovery` (`commands.zig:32`, `:53`, `:57`, `:196`, `:202@c1db9191`; gone at `481104fd`) -- were never in xfx. |
| `resume` | command | deferred | Upstream's standalone `resume_session` command. xfx resumes through `ask --resume`/`--resume-id`, a bare `xfx --resume <last\|ID>` (the TUI on a saved session) and the TUI's `/resume` picker, so the subcommand name is not advertised. `cli_surface.zig:78`. |
| `acp` | command | deferred | Agent Client Protocol server. Post-v0.1. `cli_surface.zig:62`. |
| `pr` | command | deferred | GitHub pull-request workflow. Post-v0.1. `cli_surface.zig:63`. |
| `issue` | command | deferred | GitHub issue workflow. Post-v0.1. `cli_surface.zig:64`. |
| `login` | command | deferred | Vercel OAuth. xfx reads environment credentials only. `cli_surface.zig:65`. |
| `logout` | command | deferred | Requires stored credentials, which xfx does not keep. `cli_surface.zig:66`. |
| `setup` | command | implemented | `<gateway\|llmux> [--url URL] [--json]`. Selects the provider a turn will talk to and records it. This is xfx's provider-switching surface, in the same place upstream put it when it removed `/provider` in 0.0.5. `llmux` additionally discovers and proves a daemon: discovers it (explicit `--url`, else the url a previous setup recorded, else `http://127.0.0.1:3456`, else the `proxy.port` in llmux's own config -- never a scan, never off this machine; holds `--url` to the same loopback-service policy and refuses a remote or pathed one before any network I/O), proves it is llmux (`GET /` answers exactly `llmux` **and** `GET /models` answers a non-empty catalog, each read through a bounded stream), keeps the profile file's own model when the catalog has it and otherwise takes the catalog's first entry -- either only when it is a model xfx would have written (`model_id_problem`; an entry that is not is passed over, and a catalog with none is refused with nothing written) -- the decision is about the layer being written, not the fully resolved value -- and merges `backend`/`llmux_url`/`model` into `~/.xfx/settings.json` through a staged `0600` file and a rename -- preserving every unrelated key, and refusing rather than replacing settings it could not parse. It writes `provider` and `models[<target>]` alongside `backend` and `model`, so a v0.1.0 binary reading the same profile still resolves the endpoint the operator chose. When a higher layer (`XFX_MODEL`, or an exact-workspace entry) will still outrank what was written, it says so on stderr and in `overridden_by`. It sends **no completion request**: the ping and the catalog are the whole receipt. It reads exactly one field of llmux's config, `proxy.port`, and never any credential. `gateway` performs **no network I/O**: it has no daemon to probe and reads no catalog, so it records the selection, keeps the profile's model for that provider, and names the credential the environment supplies. **It is not credential onboarding**: no key is read from a prompt and none is written, and a target with no resolvable credential is recorded with a warning naming the two variables rather than refused, because the profile is machine state and the environment is shell state. Upstream's interactive Gateway onboarding remains absent. `cli_surface.zig:67`. |
| `permissions` | command | deferred | Permission rule management UI. `cli_surface.zig:70`. |
| `models` | command | deferred | Model catalog. `cli_surface.zig:71`. |
| `provider` | command | deferred | Provider switching, including Codex. xfx implements provider *switching* as `xfx setup <provider>`, which is where upstream moved it in 0.0.5; the standalone `provider` command name is not advertised. `cli_surface.zig:72`. |
| `background` | command | deferred | Background and durable work. `cli_surface.zig:74`. |
| `teams` | command | deferred | Vercel team selection; needs `login`. `cli_surface.zig:75`. |
| `credits` | command | deferred | Billing surface; needs `login` (see § Upstream command aliases). Alias: `balance`, upstream's second spelling of the same command at both pins (`.aliases = &.{"balance"}`, `src/builtins/commands.zig:219@481104fd`, `:237@c1db9191`; the help row reads `credits\|balance`, `:308@481104fd`). `cli_surface.zig:79`. |
| `usage` | command | deferred | Usage reporting; needs `login`. `cli_surface.zig:80`. |
| `upgrade` | command | deferred | Self-updater and release channels (see § Upstream command aliases). Alias: `update`, which fx v0.0.16 added as a second spelling of the same command (`c61fc618`; `.aliases = &.{"update"}`, `src/builtins/commands.zig:242@481104fd`, parsed as `upgrade` with the same `--channel <stable\|dev>` and `--json`, `src/core/cli/cli_surface.zig:3664-3667@481104fd`). There `fx update --help` prints upgrade's own page -- `fx upgrade`, `Upgrade 𝒇x on the selected release channel`, `Usage: fx upgrade [--channel <stable\|dev>] [--json]` -- and exits 0, and the top-level help's row reads `upgrade\|update` (`commands.zig:320@481104fd`; `fx-p5-cli-ledger-update-help-40x120-h00-update-help`, `-h02-help-update`); at 0.0.13 `fx update` was `fx: unknown subcommand: update`, exit 1. xfx has no self-updater, so `xfx update` takes the parser's unknown-command path like any name it has never heard of. `cli_surface.zig:81`. |
| `replay` | command | deferred | Golden terminal replay; needs the full-screen UI. `cli_surface.zig:82`. |
| `workspace` | command | deferred | Additional-root management. `cli_surface.zig:83`. |

## Interactive shell commands

Upstream's slash palette is the ~40 entries in `src/builtins/commands.zig:414-457`.
xfx's shell owns the thirty-two below and nothing else; `scripts/check-no-stubs.sh`
reconciles them against `SLASH_COMMANDS` in `src/interactive.rs`, in both
directions.

**An alias is not a command of its own, and it does not get a row of its own.**
`SLASH_REGISTRY`, beside that list, carries each command's aliases as metadata
on it, and the same script reconciles those aliases against the notes of the
row of the command they name -- both ways, so a name the shell answers with no
row is a failure and a row claiming an alias the shell does not answer is a
failure too. There are four today, `/exit` on `/quit`, `/setup` on
`/provider`, `/img` on `/image` and `/balance` on `/credits` (v0.0.16 dropped
/cost, `commands.zig:454@481104fd`), and they are why the canonical count is
thirty-two while the number of names the parser accepts is thirty-six.

| Surface | Kind | Status | Notes and upstream evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/help` | slash | implemented | Two front ends, one registry. **The TUI** opens upstream's help catalog in the band under the composer and writes nothing to the document: `Commands N`, an `All` tab and one tab per upstream category (the active one bracketed, packed behind a dim `…` when the row is too narrow), a blank row, then one row per command -- the name padded to the widest name in the filtered list plus four, and the summary. Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) wrap, PgUp and PgDn move a page of the composer's row cap and wrap too (`routeHelpMenuMove`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:449-466`), Tab and Shift-Tab change the tab, the composer is a search (every whitespace-separated word must appear, case-insensitively, in the name, an alias, the summary or the category; the completion menu does not open while the catalog is up), Escape closes it and empties the search, and C-u empties the search and keeps it. Enter runs a command typed out in full as typed (`/version`, `/exit`); otherwise it puts the marked command in the composer -- with a trailing space and nothing run when the command takes an argument, run at once when it does not. A tab with no xfx command on it says `Commands 0` and `No commands found.` rather than listing anything. Upstream also searches a separate usage string that xfx's registry does not carry; xfx's summaries hold the argument grammar instead. **The line-oriented shell** lists every canonical command with one line each, then says that anything else is a prompt. Both are generated from `SLASH_REGISTRY` in its own order, grouped by category in the catalog, so neither can advertise a name the parser does not answer. `commands.zig:463-499`, `help_menu_presentation.zig`, `command_specs.zig:191-251`, `:429-473`, `app_input_runtime.zig:2732-2762`. |
| `/new` | slash | implemented | Ends the current session and starts a fresh identity on the next prompt. The old session's writer lock, its "always" grants, and its read proofs are all released with it, because each was sold as being about that session. In the TUI a running turn is cancelled first, with whatever was queued behind it, rather than waited for; the document on the screen is handed to the terminal's own scrollback -- erased band, then the document's rows scrolled off the top -- and the welcome line is painted on row 1 with the band under it, with no notice and the window title back to `xfx v<version> \| <workspace>`. The line shell says one sentence and erases nothing. `commands.zig:417`, `app_session_runtime.zig:1698-1708`. **What the new session goes on in** is what was in force: after a `/resume`, the resumed session's provider, model, effort and Fast, as upstream's `/new` resets none of them (`newSession` -> `applyIdleLiveSessionTransition`, `app_session_runtime.zig:1524-1526`, `:1788-1833@481104fd`), and its first save records those (`/model`). **Recorded divergence:** upstream seeds the new session's log from the workspace's preferences instead (`beginFreshV2Session`, `app_session_runtime.zig:1481-1487@481104fd`; `:1653-1667@c1db9191`), which a resume does not move, so its log can name an effort and a Fast its turns are not sent with; xfx's log names the ones they are. Owner: the dispatcher. Reason: the log must match what is sent. Expires `date:2026-12-04`. |
| `/clear` | slash | implemented | The same operation as `/new`, under upstream's other name for it: upstream's `/clear`, `/new` and `/reset` all reach `resetSessionWithBackgroundPolicy` and its policy is discarded before anything reads it, so the conversation is dropped and the next prompt opens a new session. **Not** an erase: the scrollback keeps everything, as upstream's does. `commands.zig:464`, `app_commands.zig:667-680`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2001`. |
| `/reset` | slash | implemented | The same operation as `/new` and `/clear`, under upstream's third name for it; it opens a new session id rather than keeping one and clearing its history, as upstream's does. `commands.zig:466`, `app_commands.zig:672-675`. |
| `/resume` | slash | implemented | **The TUI** opens upstream's session picker in the band under the composer: `Sessions N  [Current workspace]  All workspaces` (the active scope bracketed), a blank row, then one row per saved session, newest first -- the title (a `/rename`, else the first prompt) padded to the widest title in the filtered list, four columns, then `<workspace> · <age> · N turn(s)` in fixed columns with the age (`now`, `Nm`, `Nh`, `Nd`) right-aligned; the metadata is dropped rather than squeeze the title under twelve columns, and a title too wide for its column loses its middle. The session this one is in is not listed, nor a session with neither a turn nor a title. **A session with no turn and a title is listed at `1 turn`**, as fx v0.0.16 lists every session it keeps at `@max(turns, 1)` (`summaryOf`, `src/core/session/session_adapter.zig:4632@481104fd`): the sessions xfx keeps with no turn are a `/rename` before the first prompt and a `/fork` before it, so a zero-turn fork reads `<title> (fork)  … · 1 turn` on both (`fx-p5-effort-fork-fork-empty-40x120-resume`). A fork is listed as any session is, by its title, which ends ` (fork)`, and Enter on it says `* session resumed: …`, not the fork's notice. Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) **stop** at the ends, PgUp and PgDn move a page of the composer's row cap and stop there too (`routeComposerVertical`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:209-227`), Tab and Shift-Tab switch the scope, the composer is a search (a case-insensitive substring of the title or the workspace path; upstream also searches a preview of the conversation, which xfx's store does not keep), Escape closes it and empties the search, and Enter resumes the marked session. The store is read on the runtime thread: the picker opens on `Loading sessions…`, says `Unable to load sessions.` when the store cannot be read and `No sessions found.` when it has nothing, and Enter on nothing does nothing. It shows a page at a time, as upstream's does -- the screen's rows less seven, never fewer than ten (`resumePageLimitForRows`, `app_session_runtime.zig:1188-1193`) -- with `↓ Load more` under the last session while there are more (`resume_menu_presentation.zig:155-192`, `:417-436`): the mark reaching the last loaded session loads the next page, as Enter on the row does, and the header's count and the search cover the pages loaded; a scope change starts from one page. The runtime reads up to 200 sessions per scope at once, so the next page is there already and upstream's `↓ Loading more…` is never shown. A session another xfx holds is refused at once, without waiting on its lock, with upstream's red row under the header (`This session is open in another xfx. Close it there, then press enter to retry.`); any other failure is `Unable to resume this session.` -- upstream's third row, a session being updated, cannot happen here because the store reads only published bytes. On success the session that was open is closed (it is kept: xfx writes no session before a prompt or a `/rename`), the screen **and its scrollback** are reset (`ESC[H ESC[2J ESC[3J`, the first thing inside the synchronized update that carries the replay and the band, so the three land as one write and a terminal that takes none of it is owed all three again -- upstream's `appendFrameReset`, the first movement after its `?2026h`, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:611-616`, `:1311-1327` at `c1db919`), and the saved session is replayed from row 1 -- `* session resumed: <title>`, then each turn as it was shown live: the card, the answer through the markdown presenter, its tool group, and the dim `  Ns (↑in ↓out)` summary from the log's timestamps and recorded usage -- with no welcome line, and the window titled after the session. A turn replays in upstream's order: each step's text, then its calls and their results, then the closing answer. A replayed tool row says what the live one did -- `Exited 3`, `Denied file`, `Edited a.txt +1 / -1`, `No changes to …` -- from the outcome and line counts the log keeps beside each result, except that a failure loses its reason (`Failed missing.txt`), as upstream's replay does (`app_session_runtime.zig:5036-5042`); a log written before those were kept replays every call as done or failed. A call the turn's stop cut off is not replayed, nor a step none of whose calls came back (`execution_memory.zig:449-452`): a stopped turn ends on upstream's `■ Cancelled · What can xfx do differently?` and any other interrupted one on `✗ system: failed` (`:4796-4803`), except a turn stopped while its automatic compaction ran, which is its card alone, as upstream's (`cancellation_origin: compaction`; e2e `tui-compaction-activity.test.ts:481-488`). Ctrl-O after it shows each replayed call's arguments, its whole result and a change's diff, from the log. The provider and the model the session last recorded are restored -- the model whatever the profile chose, unless `XFX_MODEL` names one (`restoreRuntimePreferences`, `app_session_runtime.zig:6147-6151`); a log that recorded no provider, as every log of the 2026-10-06 preview, goes on with the configured provider, in its own model only when no setting chose one. **Opening a session writes nothing to it**, as upstream's resume appends nothing (`hydrateResumedSession`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2368-2420`): what it goes on under is written as one `preferences_changed` just before the first turn's prompt, where it differs from the log -- the launch's `permission_mode`, and for a log with no provider the provider -- which upstream does not write, because its preferences carry no mode and its `commitRuntimePreferences` (`:3469-3554`) runs only for a command that changes one; a `/model`, `/provider` or `/permissions` after the open writes its own frame at once, as in any session. Refused while a response is in hand -- a turn running or a prompt sent and not started, upstream's `stream.active` (`:2590`) -- with `* session: resume is unavailable until the response finishes`; a `/model` being answered does not refuse it. Takes nothing. **The line-oriented shell** has no picker and says where it is. `commands.zig:467`, `app_commands.zig:682-693`, `resume_menu_presentation.zig`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2249-2282`, `:2552-2661`, `:4595-4810`. |
| `/rename` | slash | implemented | `/rename <title>` gives the session a title of the user's own, which outranks the one its first prompt gives it in every listing, in the resume notice and in the window title. The title is trimmed of spaces, tabs and line breaks and refused in upstream's words when it is empty (`✗ usage: /rename <title>`, no topic), over 240 bytes (`✗ session: title is too long`) or holds a control character (`✗ session: title must be printable text`); otherwise the window takes it at once, a turn running or not, as upstream's does, and the runtime writes a durable `session_renamed` event and publishes it in the manifest -- at once when idle, the moment the running turn ends when not, and before a reset drops the session or a resume replaces it. A rename is said once, as upstream's: between turns the notice is what became of the write, `* session: renamed to "<title>"` or, when the store refuses it, only `! session: renamed for this process but not saved (…)`; while a turn runs `renamed to` is said at once, as upstream's is, and a store that then refuses the write it waited for adds the warning -- upstream writes at once under its session lock, so it never says both. A title typed while a reset is still unwinding is the new session's. A session's derived title is upstream's (`session_display_metadata.zig:33-147`): the first prompt that gives one -- not blank, not a slash command alone -- as its first non-blank line's first eight words, at most 240 bytes; a listing of a session whose manifest was written before that rule shows the title it published then until the session is written again. A rename outranks a title a model generated for the session (the generated-session-titles row), before it and after it -- one typed while a turn runs included, whose write waits for the turn: a generated title that comes back after it is dropped (`user_title_present`). A rename before the first prompt opens the session for it, so a session renamed and never asked anything is kept on disk -- as upstream keeps one -- and, like upstream's, is not named by the exit's resume line and is not listed by the picker, which lists sessions with a turn in them. The line-oriented shell writes the same event and prints `[shell] session renamed to "<title>"`. `commands.zig:469`, `app_commands.zig:3815-3858`, `app_session_runtime.zig:3790-3822`. |
| `/fork` | slash | implemented | fx v0.0.16's command (`vercel-labs/fx@481104fd`, commit `e4348723`; D58): upstream's row, "fork the session before a prompt", Session, after `/rename` (`commands.zig:449`). Takes nothing: `/fork 2` names no command (`command_router.zig:253-256`). **The TUI** asks the runtime what can be forked -- the conversation is the runtime's -- and says, under `session`: `nothing to fork yet` (neutral) when nothing has been asked, a fork before the first prompt included; `/fork is available once the current turn finishes` (neutral) while a response is in hand, `/resume`'s rule; and `/fork needs a saved session` (a warning) when the conversation's log has stopped taking what it is given (`openForkMenu`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2072-2096`). Upstream's SDK-host sentence has no xfx host to say it. Otherwise it opens the Fork menu in the band under the composer and writes nothing: no title; each prompt the conversation ended a turn for -- finished or stopped, oldest first, and only where the turn before it ended too, as the store will fork nowhere else -- in at most three rows two cells in, broken at its own line breaks and after the last blank that fits, the third row of a longer one cut a cell short and ended `…` (`steering_message_layout`, `render_input.zig:466-525`, ported at this release's rule for this menu: the waiting-prompt banner keeps its own); the selected prompt bold, the rest dim; at most eighteen rows, the window moving only as far as it must to keep the selected prompt whole (`fork_menu_presentation.zig`). It opens on the newest. Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) **stop** at the ends, PgUp and PgDn move a page and stop too; Escape closes it; any edit to the composer closes it and the edit stands, and one made before the runtime answered keeps it from opening (`dismissActiveMenusForComposerEdit`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1984-1990`); upstream's menu is open from the command on, so before the answer a recall, an Escape, a line sent, an undo, a redo, a select-all or any other surface opening (`C-p`'s catalog included) keeps it from opening too, and the panel `/status` and `/permissions` open and `/effort`'s menu close it, as they close each other (`closeInlineCommandMenusIfPresent`, `app_commands.zig:1174-1182`): the slot holds one surface. An undo, a redo or a select-all closes the open fork and `/effort` menus too, whether or not it changes the draft (`app_input_runtime.zig:332-391`). The key help is `↑↓ navigate     enter fork     esc close`, narrowing with the screen, and `press ctrl+c again to exit` while a Ctrl-C would leave (`input_presentation.zig:704-735`). Enter forks before the selected prompt (`forkBeforeSelectedPrompt`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2098-2127`): the runtime makes the fork with the session store (the `session fork` row) **while the conversation is still its own and its lock held**, and only then lets the conversation go -- its log closes and its lock is released, nothing written to it -- and installs the fork by `/resume`'s path; anything that fails before that leaves the conversation as it was and says `✗ session: Unable to fork the session.`. The screen and its scrollback are reset and the fork replayed from row 1, as a resume's is: `* session forked: <title>` (the fork's title, the source's marked ` (fork)`), then a plain line, no glyph and no colour, `original session xfx --resume <id>` -- this product's resume spelling, the id read again as a session id before it is written -- then the kept turns; the window is titled after the fork; and the prompt goes back into the composer, unsent, unless something was typed there since the Enter or it is past what the composer holds (`submitForkMenuSelection`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1667-1690`). The next block under the plain line opens with a blank row, the composer does not; a panel that takes the composer's rows -- `/status`, `/statusline`, `/usage`, `/workspace`, a `/permissions` rule's confirmation -- keeps a blank row of the band's above it while it is up, as upstream's modal footer asks its frame for one over a tail with no gap of its own (`top_gap_rows`, `surface_frame.zig:686@481104fd`; `idle_gap`, `frame_layout.zig:388-395`). The fork goes on as a resumed session does -- the provider and model its log recorded as of the cut, unless `XFX_MODEL` names one, and the effort and switches it recorded -- and **its permissions are its own**: the mode in force, never the one the log recorded, as a resume's; the grants its log holds and no other, so an "always" the source took back after the cut, or gave after it, is not the fork's at its next turn either; and the saved-session rules (the `/permissions` row) its own `permissions.json`, holding **each deny the source has remembered**, as the source holds it at the fork with its id, and **no allow**, read by the fork's first call on. **Divergence:** fx v0.0.16 keeps a session's rules as `set permissions` lines in its log (`setPermissions`, `src/core/session/session_adapter.zig:2205-2208@481104fd`), so its fork holds them as they stood at the cut, allows and denies, among the lines 2..S it copies (`openFork`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:1744-1747`; `copyFolded`, `:1837-1868@481104fd`), and restores them as a resume does (`restoreWithPermissionState`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2179@481104fd`). xfx keeps them beside the log, where only what they are now can be read, so its fork takes what never widens what it may do: every deny -- one remembered after the cut too, which fx's fork does not hold -- and no allow, since nothing shows one was in force at the cut: where fx's fork keeps an allow made before the cut, xfx's asks again (in `auto`, refuses what `auto` would not admit on its own). A deny revoked after the cut is not the fork's, as the source no longer holds it; fx's fork holds it. Without the carry a fork lost every remembered deny: in `auto` a write in the workspace or a read-only command it named ran, and in every mode but full access a read it named was read without a question (`tests/tui.rs`'s `a_remembered_deny_still_refuses_its_*_in_a_fork_in_*`). The file is written in the fork's stage with the rest -- owner-only, published with `link(2)`, read back -- so the fork is never seen without it; the source's is read not through a link, and one that cannot be read, or is a link, a folder or a FIFO, refuses the fork (`✗ session: Unable to fork the session.`), as a source whose store cannot be read refuses every call. No child process moves: the MCP servers are the runtime's and the shell tool's commands end with their calls. Its first request carries the kept turns and the prompt (`fx-p5-effort-fork-fork-40x120-bodies`). It is on disk from the fork, so the exit line names it even with no turn (D90, `tests/tla/session_manager/system-design.md:1536-1560`); `/status`'s `· saved` is that row's. **The line-oriented shell** has no menu and no draft to put a prompt back into, says where the menu is, and writes nothing. `app_commands.zig:455-466`, `app_session_runtime.zig:573-600`, `:3948-3958`. |

A space after a bare `/effort` opens it on its first row, as every edit puts the mark there; Tab on a bare `/effort` opens it on the level in use. | `/provider` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/provider`, with `/setup` as its alias, "choose the model provider and how it signs in", Account (`commands.zig:472`). **The TUI** answers it with upstream's provider picker, first column only: a bare `/provider` or `/setup` and a Return reseed the composer with `/provider ` and open the column under the argument (column 12; typed as `/setup ` it keeps that spelling and sits at column 9), one row per provider this build has (`gateway`, `llmux`), the marked row bold in the hint grey and the others dim, ` · current` in the dim grey on the active provider while a credential it accepts is configured -- the Gateway key or a recorded loopback llmux url, asked without I/O by the same two questions setup asks (`provider::credential_ready`) -- and a body of six rows whatever the count (`provider_picker_runtime.zig:56-156`, `picker_presentation.zig:785-825`). Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) wrap, PgUp and PgDn move a page of the composer's row cap and wrap too, typing filters live by case-insensitive substring (`no matching providers` when nothing matches, and a Return then is `✗ command: Unknown command. Try /help.`), Tab writes the marked row into the composer, Escape closes the column and keeps the text, Left moves the caret (there is no earlier column), and Right from the end of the text is Return -- kept only because a commit here opens no browser and starts no sign-in. Return on the provider already in force, while its credential is configured, says `* provider: Already using <name>.` and changes nothing -- no write, and the conversation is kept (`app_auth_runtime.zig:1159-1167`). Return on any other row commits: the runtime runs the setup transaction below, the composer is cleared without a history entry, and the band says `* provider: Preparing <name>.` and, once the configuration has been re-read, `* provider: Switched to <name> with <model>.` (`app_auth_runtime.zig:1239`, `:1383-1414`). While a turn is running or a prompt is waiting for one -- upstream's busy rule; a catalog load, a `/model` change or another switch in the queue does not count -- the command says `* provider: Provider switching is unavailable until active and queued work finishes.` and opens nothing, and a column opened by typing refuses its Return the same way and keeps the text (`app_auth_runtime.zig:30`, `:430-439`). `/setup llmux` and a Return still switch at once, column or no column. Upstream's later columns -- the sign-in method, the Vercel team, the key source, the masked key field -- and its Codex and Grok rows are fx sign-in, gated (zbrain open-loop #88), and absent; the sentence on the row still names how a provider signs in because it is upstream's. **The line-oriented shell** keeps `/provider <gateway|llmux>` (or `/setup …`) as a complete command and says how to use it when it is given none. Both run the same decision procedure and write the same bytes as the `setup` subcommand, because all three are `provider::setup::prepare` followed by `provider::setup::commit` -- one decision procedure, one writer. **That shared pair is all the `setup` subcommand and the line shell perform**: they prepare, commit and re-read the configuration, and they do **not** run the snapshot/classify/rollback transaction described next, which is the TUI worker's alone. In the TUI the whole of it is a transaction on the runtime thread: the profile's preimage is snapshotted first, every network and read step runs before anything is written, a cancellation may win **only** before the commit, a commit error is classified by rereading the raw bytes rather than by trusting the error value, the configuration is then re-read from the file that was written, and a reload failure rolls back only while the disk still holds exactly the document this transaction wrote -- bytes and Unix mode both. Bytes it did not write are never overwritten: a third state on disk means another writer owns the file, and the session ends rather than clobbering it. What the shell shows afterwards -- the model, the credential fact -- is what the reload resolved to rather than what the write intended, so a settings layer that outranks the profile is named instead of silently winning. A switch drops the bundle -- a connection to an endpoint no longer configured -- and **keeps the conversation**, as upstream's `/provider` keeps its session (`app_auth_runtime.zig:1377-1381`): its log, grants, read proofs, `/undo` journal, title and the meter's tokens go on; the switch is written into the log as `preferences_changed` with the provider and the model (`commitRuntimePreferences`, `app_session_runtime.zig:3539-3550`); and the new provider's catalog is read at once, in the background as at launch (the `/model` row), as upstream's switch adopts the target's (`:1377`), so the meter measures the same tokens against the new window. The next request carries every earlier turn in the new provider's wire, rebuilt from the log: text, tool calls, results (a refusal as `execution-denied` on the Gateway and `is_error` on llmux), images, steers and a compaction checkpoint all translate. A step's sealed reasoning -- llmux's signed `thinking` blocks -- goes only to the provider **and the model** that sealed it, so a `/model` change on llmux drops it too, and the rest of the step goes as it is: its text and its calls (upstream's `ProviderReplay.matches` and `projectProviderReplay`, `types.zig:1512-1515`, `:1548-1563`); upstream drops it without a word, xfx says so once per conversation (`! session: this session recorded reasoning on the anthropic_messages wire and is continuing on vercel_gateway, so that reasoning was not carried over` after a switch, `! session: this session recorded reasoning from <model> and is continuing with <model>, so that reasoning was not carried over` after a model change). A call id the wire cannot take -- the Gateway relays ids such as `functions.read:0`, which Anthropic's `tool_use` refuses -- is sent under upstream's alias, `fx_` and 40 hex of its SHA-256 and a step (`tool_call_ids.zig`), on both wires as upstream does; the log keeps the id that was minted. A resume in the TUI -- `--resume` or `/resume` -- continues on the provider the session last recorded, whatever the profile says by then, in the model it last ran under there unless `XFX_MODEL` names one, and replays every turn in that provider's wire (upstream's `restoreRuntimePreferences`, `app_session_runtime.zig:6147`); when that provider has nothing to authenticate with, the session stays on it, says `! auth: <the turn's refusal>` once under the replay and refuses the next prompt with the same words (`restoreSessionCredential`, `app_auth_runtime.zig:172-182`) -- on any resume, the provider the session recorded or the one in force, as upstream says it on any. A log that recorded no provider resumes on the configured one; `xfx ask --resume` and the line shell still resume on the configured provider. The line shell's `/provider <gateway|llmux>` keeps the conversation the same way and says `[shell] provider=<p> model=<m>`. `commands.zig:472`, `app_commands.zig:734-745`. |

The rules live in `~/.xfx/sessions/<id>/permissions.json` in upstream's shape (`{"schema_version":2,"next_generation":N,"rules":[{id,kind,canonical,display_identity,decision,generation}]}`, a non-UTF-8 key as `{"encoding":"base64","data":…}`), owner-only, changed under a `permissions.lock` as a **compare-and-swap on the rule's generation** against the file as it stands: a confirmation shown an older rule says `! permissions: saved-session permission rule changed before confirmation`, a full store (1024) `saved-session permission rule capacity reached`, a broken one is refused (`session_permission_state.zig`, `session_codec.zig:995-1091`, `input_approval_runtime.zig:255-327`). Every decision reads the file afresh: after a configured deny and before everything else, a remembered deny refuses and a remembered allow admits, in any mode but full access; a store that cannot be read refuses the call. **Before the first prompt** upstream's session is already open, so both forms are taken then too (`handlePermissionRuleManagement`, `app_commands.zig:598-750@481104fd`): the TUI holds a confirmed change in memory for the session its first save opens -- under the id `/status` names -- in force for that session from the moment it is confirmed, as upstream's goes into its state at once (`replacePermissionStateOwned`, `input_approval_runtime.zig:289-293@481104fd`), listed by the panel, and a revoke takes a held rule out and spends its generation, so it does not come back and its id is not reused. Yes` / `2. Yes, and don't ask again for this request` / `3. No`, Tab amending the first and the third (`approval_ui.zig:1842-1913`, `:1966-1972`) -- and the line shell asks `xfx wants to read` the file with its `y/a/n`.
