| `/undo` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/undo`, "undo the latest tracked file operation", Session, no argument (`commands.zig:481`); `/undo now` is an unknown command in the TUI. It takes back the newest change this conversation's own `write_file` or `edit_file` committed -- the file put back as it was, or deleted when the change created it -- and says so in one neutral notice, upstream's words with the absolute path spelled terminal-safe (`\x1b`, `\x0a`, a byte that is not UTF-8 as `\xNN`): `* undo: Restored <path>`, `* undo: Deleted <path> (was newly created)`, `* undo: Could not undo <path>`, `* undo: Nothing to undo.` (`commandUndoLast`, `app_commands.zig:1242-1290`). The line shell prints the same sentence as `[shell] undo: …`. **What is tracked** is upstream's (`ChangeTracker`, `change_tracker.zig:30-99`; `publishCommittedTracker`, `app_callbacks.zig:1125-1170`): a write or an edit that landed, newest last, at most 100 deep with the oldest dropped first -- not `create_folder`, not a command, not a change that was refused, failed or changed nothing -- and a change is taken off whether or not it could be reversed. xfx also drops the oldest past 64 MiB of preimage (upstream has no byte bound; a preimage may be a 10 MiB file), always keeping the newest. **In memory only**, as upstream's: nothing is written to the session log and the model is not told; `/new`, `/clear`, `/reset` and `/resume` leave every earlier change behind (`app_session_runtime.zig:1922`, `:1999`; upstream's resume test is answered `Nothing to undo.`, `tui-resume.test.ts:6029-6037`); a provider switch does not, because it keeps the conversation, as upstream's `/provider` keeps its session and its tracker (`runProviderCommand`, `app_auth_runtime.zig:387`). **No question is asked in any permission mode** -- the person typed it, and upstream's handler calls `undoLast` directly -- **but every structural rule of a write holds**: the target is located again from the conversation's scope (an authorized root, no `..`, nothing under `.git` or `.xfx`, no symbolic link on the way), read without following a link, and replaced by a staged file renamed into its own directory with its permission bits kept, or unlinked there; a directory on the path that has gone is not made again. **Stricter than upstream** in one rule, the read-proof rule every write here obeys: the file must still be what the change left there or what this session last read of it, so a file edited by someone else since, or a link standing where it was, is `Could not undo` and left as it is (upstream restores over whatever is there, `change_tracker.zig:53-81`, `:501-537`). A read proof is left alone, so the model's next edit of a file it has not read as it now is is refused until it reads it again. **In the TUI** it is done on the runtime thread: at once between turns; typed while a turn runs it is done the moment that turn is over (upstream's is done at once) **and takes back the change that was newest when it was typed** -- the band reads the journal's position at the keystroke, and a change the turn makes after it stays for the next `/undo`; if that later change touched the same file, the undo is `Could not undo` and the file is left as the later change made it. One typed after a `/new` the runtime has not reached yet is answered for the fresh conversation. Its answer is never dropped by a reset's drain. |
| `/mcp` | slash | implemented | The verbs (unit MCP-V) and the menu (unit MCP-M) are fx v0.0.16's, and so is a launch with no trust prompt (`ba0ea4a9`): a project server waits, unasked, for the menu or `approve`. Upstream's row: `/mcp [list\|show\|add\|remove\|login\|logout\|approve\|reject]`, "manage MCP servers and their logins", Extensions, after `/undo` (`src/builtins/commands.zig:463@481104fd`); xfx's registry has no help entry, so its row is the summary. **The TUI**: a bare `/mcp` opens fx v0.0.16's picker under the composer with the menus that share its slot closed (`commandHandleMcp`, `src/core/app/app_commands.zig:927-945@481104fd`; `list` prints its table, below), drawn as `src/ui/footer/mcp_host_menu_presentation.zig@481104fd` draws it and keyed as `src/core/mcp_host/menu.zig@481104fd` does (`src/tui/mcp_menu.rs`): `MCP servers  N` (`none yet` and fx's two `Add one with /mcp add …` rows, `n of N` filtered), the servers that work, then a blank and `Needs attention  n`, each group by name ignoring case, a row each with its status at its column and `profile` or `project` -- the source column at 64 columns and more, the name `clamp(w/4, 12, 22)` wide, a status that needs the person bright -- and `N config note(s): /mcp list shows it/them`; a server's screen `runs` or `http`, `file`, `status`, `protocol`, `error`, its choices with `/mcp VERB NAME` at column 16 (56 columns and more), then `Tools N` and a row per tool (name 20, its description's first line at column 24) or why there are none (`Loading tools…`, `Its tools show here once you approve it.`, `Its tools couldn't load: …`, `It has no tools.`, `No tools match.`), in twelve lines and `N more below`; read again four times a second. The composer holds the filter -- the list's, or on a server's screen its tools', by name or description -- and each screen keeps its own (`swapHostMenuFilter`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:643-653`). Keys are fx's (`app_input_runtime.zig:994-995`, `:1498-1513`, `:1752-1831`; `input_completion_runtime.zig:424-429`): ↑↓, `C-k` and `C-j` move; Enter, and → with the caret at the end of the filter, open or run; Esc, and ← with nothing typed, go back; Esc on the list closes it and clears the filter; Ctrl-C closes it from any screen, filter and all, and arms no exit; Ctrl-P opens nothing over it, and `$` no skills menu behind it. Remove and Reject ask on their row first -- `Remove X from FILE?   yes  no`, `Reject X? xfx won't start it in this project.   yes  no`, yes chosen as fx's is, ← yes and → no, ↑↓ leave the answer, Enter answers, Esc or any printable key (Backspace's DEL among them) is no -- and Approve on its own screen (below). Three rules fx's has not, so that no answer is given to a question nobody saw (opus-C, gpt6-C): Enter answers Remove or Reject only once a frame has drawn the question, and what a refused write, a resize or a screen the band is blind on leaves unsure is unsaid again (Approve's rule, below), so a burst of Enters in one read never answers it; an Enter within 500 ms of the last -- whichever surface took it while the menu was up, and a swallowed one too, since timing cannot tell a slow key repeat from a second tap (`MCP_ENTER_HELD`) -- answers no question, and the first swallow says `That Enter came too soon after the last one to answer the question: wait a moment, then press Enter.`; opening the full transcript or the model's question panel over the menu unsays what it drew; and while the full transcript (Ctrl-O) covers the menu, the menu takes no key. A choice runs the verb warnings-only (`.warnings_only`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:545-560`): the row's status follows, and only a warning reaches the transcript. Remove removes the name and the file its question named, both kept as it asked (`Job::Remove`, `command::remove_chosen`; no words parsed again, so a server named `--help` is removed, not answered with the overview), where fx runs `remove NAME`, which refuses a name both files hold after the question said which file; a read that finds the name in the other file before the answer cancels the question; Reject is the trust choice `reject` sends. **Gated, not offered**: fx's `Log in` and `Log out` choices sign in to a remote server, which is not built (below), so neither is a choice, and a server that needs a login says `Interactive MCP authentication is unavailable here.` on its screen where fx says `Its tools show here once you log in.`; its status is MCP-V's `needs login`. c1db9191's tabs, catalogs (Tools, Resources, Prompts), info screen, `v`, `r`, `p`, `z`, `a`, `s` and `d`/`x` keys went with its menu. `p5-mcp-menu-new` (40x120 and 40x60) and `p5-mcp-empty` against fx v0.0.16 (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/mcp-menu/`). **The verbs are fx v0.0.16's** (`src/core/mcp_host/verbs.zig@481104fd`, `src/mcp/command.rs`), in its words with `xfx` for `fx` and `~/.xfx/mcp.json` for `~/.fx/mcp.json`: the line split as fx's lexer splits it (`command_lex.tokenize_argv`, `src/core/shell_command/command_lex.zig:140-226@481104fd` -- quotes, backslashes, an unquoted operator a word of its own), and one it refuses -- a quote never closed, a backslash at the end -- `/mcp: that command line has an unmatched quote or a character xfx doesn't take here.` (`app_mcp_runtime.zig:525-531`); `help`, `-h` and `--help` the overview, `VERB -h` the verb's usage, a mistake its sentence and the usage, an unknown word `'X' isn't a /mcp command.` and the overview; `auth`, `trust`, `path`, `reload`, `resource`, `prompt` and `list --connect` refused with where each went (`verbs.zig:64-73`, `:105`); `list` the `NAME SOURCE TRANSPORT STATUS` table -- its SOURCE column one cell wider than fx's, `~/.xfx/mcp.json` being a byte longer than `~/.fx/mcp.json` (`p5-mcp-verbs` `list`, `list-after`) -- then each config note as `note: …`; `show NAME` its `status`, `version`, `transport`, `url` or `command` (fx's quoted command line, `commandLine`, `src/core/mcp_host/config.zig:67-83@481104fd`), `source`, `error`, `next` and `tools N` rows, after waiting while a try is under way -- for at most the server's start timeout and 60 s, since one worker answers every `/mcp`; `add NAME URL` and `add NAME -- CMD [ARGS...]`, with `--env K=V`, `--header K=V` and `--project`, the name checked before anything else, written through `config::update` (MCP profile row) and said as `Added X to FILE.`, with `X is also in FILE; the one in ~/.xfx/mcp.json is used.` when the other file has it too; `remove NAME [--project\|--profile]`, `Removed X from FILE.`; `approve NAME \| --all` and `reject NAME`, on the settings keys the menu's choices keep them under, the entry the runtime last read approved (`Approved X, which runs: CMD` or `connects to: URL`, `Rejected X. xfx won't start it in this project.`, `No project server named X in .mcp.json.`, `No project servers are waiting for approval.`). Every answer is under `mcp`, usage included: a verb's output one neutral notice and its errors one warning (`VerbJob.run`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:585-602`), and a change reloads in place and says nothing (`MCP client runtime` row). `p5-mcp-empty` and `p5-mcp-verbs` against fx v0.0.16 (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/mcp-verbs/`). **In typed order, where fx's is the order of completion**: fx runs each typed `/mcp` on a detached thread and reports it when it ends (`startVerbs`, `VerbJob`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:551-604`), so in `p5-mcp-verbs` its `show remote`, waiting on a connect, was printed after the `show proj` and `login fixture` typed after it; that is a race no receipt can pin, and xfx answers on the MCP runtime's one worker thread in the order typed -- so a `show` that waits holds the lines typed after it, and the menu's reads, for that wait. A word, a flag or a URL typed is said back spelled by `terminal_safe`, where fx says it as typed, and only as far as its first `=` (`/mcp add has no --env= flag.`, where fx says `--env=TOKEN=…` whole) -- a server's name too, which a hand-written profile can give one; a `--env` or `--header` value is never said back. **Refused before the write, where fx writes it**: `add --project --header Authorization=…` is `Couldn't add X to .mcp.json: xfx won't send an Authorization header from .mcp.json; add the server without --project to keep it in ~/.xfx/mcp.json.` -- xfx's reader skips such an entry (MCP project servers row), so the shared file would hold a token xfx never sends -- matched ignoring case, as the reader matches it. **And nothing the reader would skip**: the entry `add` would write is put to the reader's own rules first (`config::unreadable`, `parse_entry` over the writer's JSON), so a header name with a blank in it (` Authorization=…` included), a reserved one, a control in a value, or a URL the reader won't connect to (plain `http` off loopback, credentials, a fragment) is `Couldn't add X to FILE: its "headers" isn't valid.` / `its "url" isn't valid.` with nothing written, where fx writes it and says `Added`; a `${…}` in a project's URL is left for the expansion an approval brings, after what no expansion changes is checked (its syntax, the scheme, and no literal `@` or `#` anywhere after `://`, since where the authority ends is the expansion's to say -- **residual**: an `@` in a templated path is refused too, where fx writes it), and a profile's URL, which nothing expands, is checked as written. A choice `approve` or `reject` can't save says xfx's `Project MCP choices were not applied: …`, where fx says `Couldn't save that choice: …`. **`/mcp ` completes its argument** as fx's does (`verb_completions`, `buildCompletion`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:459-515`; `src/tui/mcp_args.rs`): the eight verbs with what each does, then `show` and `remove NAME` for each server whose name a command can carry, `login` and `logout` for a remote one, `approve` and `reject` for a project one, its status beside it, read twice a second; under the argument, no header, six rows, the hint row left to the status line (`p5-mcp-verbs` `complete-args`, `complete-show`). Up, Down, `C-j` and `C-k` move the mark, Tab writes the marked line, Escape closes it for the line, and **Enter submits the marked line in place of what was typed** (`resolveSlashSubmission`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:1819-1824@481104fd`), so `/mcp remove web` with only `web2` configured removes `web2`, as fx does in `p5-mcp-verbs`. A read that drops a line above the mark keeps the mark on its line, not its index, and one that drops the marked line closes the list until the text changes, so Enter submits what was typed; and a `/mcp` line the history walk hands back opens none until it is edited, so Up goes on walking (`history_slash_recall_until_edit`, `picker_state.zig:451-466`, as a recalled slash word is). A workspace's `.mcp.json` servers wait for the person, unasked: fx v0.0.16 has no launch prompt (`ba0ea4a9`; c1db9191's raw two-row prompt, `main.zig:1818-1832@c1db9191`, its `1`/`2`/`3` and its Escape dismissal are gone), and the status line says `X is waiting for approval · /mcp` once the servers settle (MCP client runtime row). Nothing starts one before it is approved -- not the launch, a reload, or opening the menu, whose tool list for a held server is fx's reason for holding it (`heldReason`, `src/core/mcp_host/runtime.zig:2038-2053@481104fd`), never a start. **The approval's screen** is fx's (`proj  waiting for approval`, `Approve X for this project? It runs:` or `It connects to:`, then what it runs or reaches wrapped at spaces and never cut, `enter confirm     esc cancel`; presentation `:437-499`, `:507-515`), spelled before it is wrapped, so a control spelled out wraps as the cells it takes. **Where xfx's differs -- deliberately, because upstream's is unsafe to copy**: (1) it shows what the child is given, from the entry the runtime read (`ShownEntry`: its rows and the digest an approval binds, from one read) -- the command line, each word as a POSIX shell would read it back -- a word xfx expands (`${NAME}`, `${NAME:-default}`) in double quotes, every other `$`, `"`, `\` and `` ` `` escaped, so the reader expands what xfx will, and a control character beside an expansion an ANSI-C piece between quoted ones (`"a"$'\u001B'"${X}"`; one inside a default, which has no exact spelling there, stays in the quotes as the row spells it, `\x1b`) -- or the address; then one row `env: NAME=value` for each variable a stdio entry sets, or `header: Name: value` for each header a remote entry sends (a `header_env` header as `X-Team: ${TEAM}`, `bearer_token_env` as `Authorization: Bearer ${TOKEN}`, as they are sent) -- every value as written, since a pending entry is never expanded: a literal, a default with its text (`${NOPE:-./y}`), a `${VAR}` as the name it is, and never a value from xfx's environment. fx's shows the command line or the address alone (`confirmLines`, presentation `:442-481`). xfx's rule since 2026-11-04 (`c4d252f5`): a variable changes what runs as much as the command does -- `"PATH": "./x:${PATH}"` makes `node` run `./x/node`, since the command is resolved with the child's PATH, and `NODE_OPTIONS`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_*` or `PYTHONPATH` change it as much -- so a key alone hides what is approved. The entry is the repository's own file, so its text shows nothing the file does not; a credential written into it literally (by a person, or by `/mcp add --project --env`) is shown as the file shows it, and one in xfx's environment never is. Values a person typed are still never said back by the verbs (`typed`), and a profile's are never shown by `list` or `show`: neither is an approval. A server's command line and address -- in `show`, on the menu's screen, and in `approve`'s sentence -- are its file's, `${VAR}` and all, a profile server's and an approved project server's alike: what an expansion puts in is for the server (the profile and project rows; `ServerConfig::shown_command`, `shown_url`), and reaches a screen only if the server prints it to its stderr (the client runtime row). Every row is spelled as the approval view spells a command it asks about (`approval_view::command_text`: every control `\xNN`, a line break included, and each bidirectional, zero-width or invisible code point the table names `\u{NNNN}`, so a `U+202E` cannot draw the command backwards); a row longer than 1024 bytes, which would fill the screen, is its first 512 and last 256 bytes with its length and where it is whole between them (`[N bytes in all; .mcp.json has every byte]`), never cut without saying so -- and such an entry is not shown whole, so Enter approves nothing (`A row of X's entry is too long to show here whole, so nothing was approved: .mcp.json has every byte, and /mcp approve X approves the entry as xfx read it.`). A row the width wraps goes on in rows led by `↳ `, two columns in, where each row of the entry's own is four in -- every wrapped piece, of a command line or address alone too, so none passes for a row the entry has (`v`, a run of spaces, `env: PATH=/usr/bin`; an address `https://a.example/mcp?q=`, spaces, `header: X-Team: blue`, opus-B). The cost: fx's goes on four columns in, so a wrapped command line or address here is led by `↳ ` where fx's is not. (2) It takes the rows it needs past fx's thirteen, up to the space the screen gives a panel, and Enter approves only what a frame drew whole -- every row in the panel's rows and none cut with `…`, as the row measures it, a cluster at a time: one that could not, or that something wrote over since (a refused write, a resize, a screen the band is blind on), approves nothing and says `xfx couldn't show all of what X runs on this screen, so nothing was approved.`. (3) Enter approves the entry that frame drew, by its digest (`TrustAction::ApproveShown`), so what is saved is the entry xfx read and showed, and a reload admits it only while the file still holds it -- a server a reload keeps keeps its screen's entry only while that entry's digest is the fresh read's, so an entry respelled (`"type": "stdio"` added) is shown, and approved, as it now is: a rewrite read before Enter approves nothing (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx showed it; nothing was approved.`, and the screen shows the new one next), and one on disk not yet read is saved as the entry shown, which the next read finds changed and leaves waiting. fx's Approve runs `approve NAME`, which approves what its host holds when the verb runs (`verbs.zig:631-646`). An approval is for that entry, not for its name. Upstream keys it by name alone (`resolveAdmission`, `src/core/mcp/project_config.zig:825-832@c1db9191`, unchanged at `:938-945@481104fd`) while its auto mode writes `.mcp.json` without review like any other workspace file (`src/core/tooling/tool_admission.zig:663-693@c1db9191`, `:662-692@481104fd`): a model's file write could swap an approved server's command or address and have it run on the next reload, unasked. In xfx a changed entry is pending again (MCP project servers and trust, below) and waits for the menu or `approve`. Nothing reloads the MCP configuration without the person -- launch, and an `add`, `remove`, `approve` or `reject`, typed or chosen in the menu, are the only triggers; there is no file watch and no model tool that reloads. The typed `approve --all` keeps fx's behaviour: every entry the last read showed as a pending server, not ones added later (`CHANGELOG.md:165@481104fd`), and not one that parses only once expanded (`"type": "${T:-stdio}"` is shown as a configuration error, not as a server); `approve NAME`, typed, is the one way to approve such an entry: it has no server to find by name, so `approve` approves the entry the runtime last read, bound to that read's digest as the parsed path is, and only while `.mcp.json` still holds it (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx read it; nothing was approved.` otherwise, and fx's `No project server named …` for an entry the runtime never read), saying what it runs or reaches but never its `env` or `headers` -- fx's `approve` finds only servers that parse (`verbs.zig:631-646@481104fd`), so this is xfx's, kept from c1db9191's `trust approve <name>`; `reject NAME` reaches such an entry the same way. An approval is for the entry, not for the workspace code it loads: `node server.js` is approved as that command line, and an edit to `server.js` asks nothing (a later unit looks at that). What a server or a repository supplied -- the menu's names, addresses, command lines, errors, stderr tails, tool names and descriptions -- is spelled as fx's footer spells it (`encodeTerminalSafeInline(.., 4096)`, `row_text.zig:127-134@481104fd`, through `terminal_safe`: controls, C1, zero-width, bidirectional and byte-order-mark code points, and the other default-ignorable ones the approval view names (SEC-A4), spelled out rather than dropped) and cut to its row, where fx writes such a byte as `?` or spells it; an approval's rows are spelled as the approval view spells a command (above), and cut only as above. A server's name is said only as far as its first `=`, as the verbs say it (`typed`): a value after one may be a secret. **Gated, not built** (signing in opens a browser and stores a token, which needs its own decision -- zbrain open-loop #88 covers Codex and Grok only): `login NAME [--no-browser]` makes fx's checks first -- the server is there (`No MCP server named X. /mcp list shows them.`), it is remote (`X runs a command, so it has no login.`), nothing holds it back (`X is waiting for approval: /mcp approve X.`, `X is disabled.`; `login`, `verbs.zig:765-806@481104fd`) -- and then says `Interactive MCP authentication is unavailable here.` (`mcp.zig:154-160@c1db9191`) where fx looks for how to sign in: `Couldn't sign in to remote: fx couldn't find how to sign in to it.` in `p5-mcp-verbs` comes from a discovery xfx never starts. `logout` of a remote server is fx's `X wasn't signed in.` (`verbs.zig:689-716`), true of every server since xfx keeps no login, and `remove` never says fx's `, and deleted its login`. A bare `add slack` (fx's Slack server, to sign in to) says `Interactive MCP authentication is unavailable here.` and writes nothing; `add slack URL` is an ordinary add, as fx's is. **Narrower than upstream's**: a settings write keeps no `backups/` copy, as no xfx settings write does. **The line shell** says the TUI is where MCP runs, and starts no server. |
| `/skills` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/skills [list|add|install|show|create|remove|path] [name|url|path]`, "browse and manage skills", Extensions, with an argument (`commands.zig:483`; grammar `src/builtins/skills.zig:93-104`, `parseCommand`). **Where skills come from** is upstream's discovery (`skill_runtime.zig:336-1046`) over **xfx's own roots only**: `.xfx/skills` then `skills/` in the workspace and each ancestor short of the home directory, then the managed root `~/.xfx/skills`; candidates in byte order, a directory holding a regular `SKILL.md` whose frontmatter is upstream's (`name`, optional `description` inline or as a `>`, `>-` or `|` block, 64 KiB header cap -- `skill_contract.zig`), a link followed only inside its workspace ancestor or a directory the profile's `skill_symlink_authorities` or `XFX_SKILL_SYMLINK_AUTHORITIES` names, and never in the managed root (below), the same directory counted once, and every candidate skipped said as `! skills: skill discovery warning: …` before the answer. **The decided default:** upstream also reads other agents' roots (`.opencode/`, `.codex/`, `.claude/`, `.agents/`, `.claw/skills` in the workspace and at home, `src/builtins/skills.zig:16-38`); xfx does not, because on a machine with another agent installed it would put that agent's instructions into xfx's prompt. **The list the TUI reads** is discovered on a worker thread -- first at launch, so the first frame does not wait for it, then again on request -- and held between refreshes (`beginStartupSkillsLoad`, `app_bootstrap_runtime.zig:278-289`; `CatalogRefreshTask`, `requestRefresh`, `pollRefresh`, `skill_runtime.zig:2181-2310`); the `$` menu, the slash popup, the ghost after the caret and `/skills` all read it. One action waits for a refresh and a newer one takes its place (`queueRefreshAction`, `:2353-2383`): the launch's own, which says what discovery skipped once the first list lands; `list` and `show`, which open the menu when the refresh they asked for lands; and the sentence of a `create`, `remove` or install, said once the list holding the change lands -- `Installing from …` and every refusal at once (`queueSkillsNoticeAfterRefresh`, `app_commands.zig:1903-1951`). A refresh that fails says `* skills: Skills could not be refreshed. The previous catalog was not shown as current.` (at launch: `Skills could not be loaded. Run /skills to try again.`). There is no loading row: upstream's menu has none, only `No skills available.` and `No skills found.` (`composeEmptyRow`, `skills_menu_presentation.zig:411-431`). **The TUI**, bare or `list`: upstream's menu in the band under the composer, which stays and is its search -- `Skills N` and the source tabs `All`, `xfx` and `Workspace` (upstream's tabs for the families xfx reads; its `Claude`, `Codex`, `Agents`, `OpenCode` and `Claw` tabs are absent, there being no such skills to list), a blank row, one row per skill with its name and, from the widest name plus four, its scope (`xfx · Workspace`, `xfx · Global`), the marked row bold and the rest dim (`skills_menu_presentation.zig`); managed installs first, then workspace skills, a search ranking name prefix, then name, then description, source and path (`SkillMenuView`). Up/Down and `C-k`/`C-j` move and stop at either end, PgUp/PgDn page, Tab and Shift-Tab change the source, Enter puts the marked skill's **token** in the composer in place of the search, and Escape closes it and empties the search; the hint row says `↑↓ navigate     tab source     enter use     esc close`. `show <name>` opens it on the skill's own tab with it marked, or with the name as the search when two skills share it; `* skills: Skill 'nope' not found.` otherwise. **A `$`** typed outside an `@` path opens the same menu on the word after it, shown while some skill matches that word (`app_input_runtime.zig:1629-1641`, `menuVisible`); a space ends it, and Enter makes the word a token. A `$word` that arrives in a paste -- text, or beside a pasted image -- and names a skill opens it too (`maybeOpenPastedSkillMenu`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:612-663`). **The slash popup** lists the skills its query finds after its commands, in the menu's `All` order, each by its name with no slash, its description and its short source (`workspace .xfx`, `workspace skills/`, `global .xfx`), under `Results N` once a skill is among the rows (`prepareSlashMenu`, `slashMenuRowContent`, `composeSlashMenuHeaderRow`, `picker_presentation.zig:1100-1266`); Tab or Enter on a skill row makes the slash word its token, and Enter runs a command instead when the query is one's exact name (`bindSelectedSlashSkill`, `submitSlashPickerSelection`, `app_input_runtime.zig:2810-2919`). **The ghost after the caret** (`visibleInlineCompletion`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:598-640`): a `$word` ending the draft with no menu showing -- after the space that ends the `$` menu is taken back, say, or on a recalled line -- has the rest of the first skill name it begins (discovery order, ASCII case aside, `firstSkillNameCompletion`) painted after it in the dim grey and closed by a reset (`\e[38;5;245m…\e[0m`, `appendInlineCompletionSuffix`, `input_presentation.zig:955-973`), cut to the cells the row has and not shown when its first character does not fit; a slash word ending a draft that has other words before it has the rest of the first command it begins, in registry order, a name before its aliases (`firstSlashCompletion`, `command_specs.zig:490-506`). A draft that is one slash word is the popup's and has none. Right with nothing selected and Tab take it -- a skill's as its token in place of the `$word`, a command's as the letters with no space -- and Enter sends the draft as typed. Escape dismisses it until the draft stops being that kind of word, as Escape on the `$` menu does its word's; with a turn running, Escape arms the interrupt over a skill's instead. None shows under a question or while `/help`, `/settings`, `/model`'s list, the session picker or a skills menu owns the band, and a recalled slash word shows none until it is edited (`inlineCompletionSurfaceAvailable`, `:703-727`; `picker_state.zig:142-167`, `:451-467`). A token is the skill's **name**, painted bold in the composer and in the card's accent on the card, a space after it when the next character would join a word; it is one unit (a delete over it takes it whole), an edit that takes one or puts one in is an undo boundary as an image badge's is, a kill and a yank carry it, and a recalled line brings it back. The prompt carries it as `$name` (`"text":"$demo run it"`), and the turn **binds** the exact directory the menu chose. **What the model is told**, on every request of every front end (TUI, line shell, `ask`) from the skills discovered as the turn starts: upstream's `<available_skills>` system block before the project instructions, byte for byte -- two sentences, one `Root N: <dir>` per directory, one `- <name>: <description> (location: skill:<namespace>:<root>/<leaf>)` per skill, the namespace upstream's SHA-256 of the advertised names and paths, values written as upstream's model scalars, descriptions cut at 1024 characters, the block kept to upstream's budget -- two percent of the model's context window at four bytes a token (a 200k window is 16000 bytes), from the catalog the TUI reads at launch or, in the line shell and `ask`, from the window the Gateway is known to give the model; 8000 characters when no window is known (`SkillPromptBudget.resolve`, `skill_runtime.zig:3061-3083`) -- by shortening descriptions and then omitting entries from the end with `Omitted skills: N.` (`renderSkillPrompt`, `:3115-3322`) -- and, when the prompt names skills (a bound token, a `$name` outside quotes, code and `@` paths, a leading `/name`, or "use the X skill", not after a negation -- `collectExplicitSkillSelections`, `:2696-2797`), upstream's `Explicitly invoked skill content for this query:` block after the project instructions with each skill's `SKILL.md` whole in `<skill_content name=… location=… resource="SKILL.md" complete="true">` (`buildExplicitPromptSection`, `skill_invocation.zig:205-289`), on that turn's requests only. The TUI says `* 1 requested skill loaded` / `└ Loaded skill demo` under the card, or `! Requested skills · 0 loaded · 1 failed (ctrl+o for details)` with each failure on the full transcript; the line shell says the same rows as `[shell]` lines. A prompt with a token is not steered into a running turn, as upstream's is not (`sameTurnSteeringEligible`). **`path`**: `* skills: xfx workspace roots are auto-discovered from .xfx/skills and skills/.` and `xfx managed install root: <home>/.xfx/skills` -- upstream's third line, about compatibility roots, is not said, xfx reading none. **`create <name>`** writes upstream's template `SKILL.md` into the managed root (`* skills: Created <path>`), a name with `/` or `\` refused in upstream's words. **`remove <name>`** deletes only a managed skill (`* skills: Removed skill 'made'.`); any other is refused naming where it is (`Skill 'demo' comes from workspace .xfx/skills, not the xfx managed install root. Remove it from <path>.`). **`add`/`install <source> [--skill <name>]`** read upstream's pasted forms (`npx skills add …`, `skills.sh/<owner>/<repo>/<skill>`, `<owner>/<repo>@<skill>`) and install **from a local directory**: `* skills: Installing from <source>...`, then `* skills: Installed: <name>` per skill -- a top-level `SKILL.md` under the directory's name, each other one under its own directory's, copied without `.git*` or links into the managed root through a staged directory and two renames under `~/.xfx/.skill-install-locks/<name>` (`copySkillDirWithOptions`). **The second decided default:** a source that is not an existing directory is answered as upstream answers a clone that failed -- `* skills: Failed to install. Check the source path or URL and try again.` -- and nothing reaches the network; upstream would run `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/<source>.git` (`installFromGitHub`, `skills.zig:265-280`). Anything else is `* usage: /skills [list|add|install|show|create|remove|path] [name|url|path]`. **The line-oriented shell** answers the same forms as `[shell] skills: …` lines, the usage line on stderr; bare, `list` and `show` print upstream's listing (`Visible skills (N):`, grouped, `listSkillsSummary`, `skill_runtime.zig:2964-3004`). **Where a link may go** (`canonicalPathHasReadAuthority`, `skill_runtime.zig:586-681`): a link in a workspace root -- the root itself, a candidate, its `SKILL.md` -- is followed only when it resolves inside the workspace ancestor the root hangs off, or inside a directory the user named: the profile's `skill_symlink_authorities` (an array of at most 32 absolute paths with no `..`, profile-only -- a `.xfx.json` that carries it is reported and ignored -- the workspace's own entry replacing the global list, each canonical where it resolves and as written where it does not yet; `config_runtime.zig:1784-1877`) or `XFX_SKILL_SYMLINK_AUTHORITIES` (colon-separated absolute paths, as written, a relative or `..` entry skipped). By default none is named, so a link out of the workspace is refused with fx's diagnostic, and a link in the managed root is never followed. **Fail closed:** an unreadable list in any profile layer names no authority at all (`invalid_value key=skill_symlink_authorities skill_symlink_authorities must be an array of at most 32 absolute directory paths without .. components`), where upstream drops only the layer it could not parse. The authorities are read once, when the configuration is loaded at launch. **Narrower than upstream's**: a candidate's root is found missing through linked directories, where upstream's walk stops at a link. Receipt: `.prd/tui-phase4/receipts/s1/`. |
| `/copy` | slash | implemented | Puts the conversation's last reply on the system clipboard: the newest assistant step with any text, exactly as the model wrote it -- markdown kept, nothing rendered, no line break added -- so a turn that ended on a tool call still copies the text before it. The tool is `pbcopy` on macOS and `xclip -selection clipboard` on Linux, found on `PATH` and judged by its exit status; there is none elsewhere. It says `No assistant reply to copy.`, `Copied to clipboard.` or `Failed to copy to clipboard.` (topic `clipboard`). Takes no argument in the TUI. The reply is the session log's, not the screen's, so a tab the screen showed as a space is copied as a tab; the TUI copies on a thread of its own and says how it went when the tool exits; a copy still running when the session ends is given a second, then its tool is killed and its thread joined before the signal handlers go back. `commands.zig:484`, `app_commands.zig:1974-2001`, `native.zig:15-52`. |
| `/fast` | slash | implemented | **The TUI**: upstream's toggle (`toggleFastForModel`, `session_commands.zig:733-751`). On, it turns Fast off -- `* fast: off`; off, it turns it on -- and Ultra off -- for a model whose catalog row offers Fast, `* fast: on`, and says `* fast: This model does not come with a fast mode.` for one that does not. Takes no argument: `/fast on` is `✗ command: Unknown command. Try /help.`. Decided on the band against the row, and said at once; before any catalog is read it is held, the catalog asked for, and answered when it arrives -- in order behind any other held. Saved as upstream's `persistPreferenceTargets` saves it (`session_commands.zig:781-791`): the model in force under `models.<provider>` -- the one a process override (`XFX_MODEL`) pinned, too -- `provider`, `fast_mode`/`ultrafast_mode` and `fast_mode_model_bound`; a layer above the profile that will still decide `model`, `fast_mode` or `ultrafast_mode` for a fresh session is named (`saved to user settings (scope=user); fresh sessions here use higher-precedence model=process_override`, `:170-240`). The save is written before the change is said, as upstream persists before it announces (`:781-797`): on a writer thread of the band's, with `* fast: on` -- and whatever was typed or streamed after it -- held until the write answers, so the save's line is the one above it. Recorded in the session, shown as `⚡︎` on the hint row, and sent as described on the `/model` row (llmux offers none). A turn with Fast on that goes without it because the catalog that would offer it cannot be read says `Fast mode is unavailable for this model right now; continuing at standard speed.` in its answer, as upstream's operational text (`orchestrator.zig:7253-7262`). A resumed session keeps Fast's binding only when it goes on with the provider, the model and the Fast setting the configuration bound (`restoredFastModeModelBound`, `app_session_runtime.zig:6318-6328`). The line-oriented shell sends no speed and says so. `commands.zig:492`. |
| `/ultrafast` | slash | implemented | **The TUI**: upstream's `/ultrafast [on|off|status]` (`handleUltrafast`, `session_commands.zig:704-859`). Bare or `status`, `* ultrafast: requested: on|off`; `on`, for a model whose row offers Ultra, turns Fast off and says `* ultrafast: requested on`, and for one that does not warns `! ultrafast: Ultra mode is unavailable for this model and may increase cost.` and changes nothing; `off` says `* ultrafast: requested off`; anything else is `✗ usage: /ultrafast [on|off|status]`. Held for the catalog like `/fast`. Saved, recorded and shown (`⚡︎` in Ultra's yellow) as described on the `/model` row; a turn in Ultra that cannot be sent in it -- not the Gateway, not `openai/…`, a row without it -- fails rather than going at another speed. A turn in Ultra a response of which the Gateway's finish does not confirm as served at `ultrafast` (`providerMetadata.gateway.serviceTier`, `client.zig:3451-3463`) says, once per turn and in its answer as upstream's operational text, `Ultrafast was requested, but Gateway did not confirm it was served; this response may have used a standard or lower tier.` (`orchestrator.zig:8015-8022`). The line-oriented shell sends no speed and says so. `commands.zig:493`. |
| `/image` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row, with `/img` as its alias: "attach an image by path", Media (`commands.zig:476`). **The TUI** reads the file the path names -- relative to the workspace, `~` and `~/…` under the home directory, absolute as written; quotes and backslash escapes taken off -- **once**, whole, and puts a badge `[Image N]` at the caret; the prompt says `[Image #N]` in its place and the image goes after its text. Its type comes from its first bytes (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP), never from its name, and a file over 20 MiB is refused by its size before it is read. A path spelled inside the workspace may not leave it through a symlink. **Unlike upstream, the configured read table is asked first** -- at the file the path resolves to (every link followed, so a link is judged by the file it reaches, never by its own name), under `read` and `read_file`, as `read_file` reading that file is judged, in every permission mode, and asked again, before a byte is read, of the file the open reached, by its descriptor's path (`fcntl(F_GETPATH)` on macOS, the descriptor's link under procfs on Linux; elsewhere a table with rules refuses), so a directory on the way swapped for a link after the first answer is judged where it leads: a deny refuses `/image` with `` ✗ images: failed to attach image: a configured permission rule denies reading `<path>` ``, and the file is never opened; an `ask` asks nobody, the attach being the person's own act. Upstream's attach consults no rule on any of its routes (`load_inline_image_attachment`, `image_attachments.zig:1880-1885`, into `loadUserImageAttachment` and `readImageHeaderBytes`, `:194-238`, `:1825-1834`, from `handlePastedBytes`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:459`, and `extractInlineImageAttachments`, `image_attachments.zig:1729`; the same at `481104fd`), which is unsafe to copy: a path named in pasted text would send the provider a file the table keeps from the model. And anything but a regular file is refused without waiting on it: the path is `stat`ed before it is opened, then opened non-blocking and judged by its own descriptor, so a FIFO named `x.png` is `NotRegularFile` where upstream's attach opens before it stats (`readImageHeaderBytes`, `:1825-1831`; `481104fd` `:2032-2038`) and would wait for a writer; xfx takes the open of upstream's own `openVisionRegularFile` (`:408-469`): `lstat` first, then `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK|O_NOCTTY`, the descriptor's `fstat`, and a link, a FIFO, a socket or a non-directory on the way are all `NotRegularFile` (`:414-418`, `:443-455`). A rule's refusal is named `PermissionRuleDenied`, not the system's `AccessDenied`. Upstream's words: `✗ usage: /image <path>` (no topic), `✗ images: image file not found`, `unsupported image type`, `image exceeds the 20 MiB limit`, `failed to attach image: <Error>`, and `* images: attached image: <name>`. Deleting the badge drops the image; a yank of the killed badge and a recalled line bring the image back under a fresh number each time, as upstream's kill ring and recall do (`kill_ring.zig:120-190`; `tui-composer-edit-contracts.test.ts:763-797`, `:852-878`), and an undo never brings a badge back: an edit that takes a badge or puts one in -- its paste, a deletion, a kill, a yank -- is a boundary of the edit history, as upstream's is for every structured entity (a pasted-text block too), so an undo after it finds nothing to take and the composer stays as it is, with nothing said (`composer_replacement.zig:223-232`, `composer_kill_ring.zig:44-118`, `composer_undo.zig:252-268`, `app_input_runtime.zig:397-402`). A badge whose number the prompt also says in words -- a typed `[Image #1]` beside badge #1 -- is sent under the session's next free number, the words left as typed (`mapSubmittedImages`, `input_submit_runtime.zig:1615-1692`). A command typed after the badges runs with them kept. Numbers are never reused, and a resumed session's new images number past its own. The Gateway gets upstream's v4 `file` part (`mediaType`, base64 `data`) after the text, and every later request re-sends the history's images; llmux gets Anthropic's base64 `image` block on the user message. On the Gateway a turn carrying an image is sent only to a model the catalog tags `vision` and `file-input`; a model the catalog does not list, or an unread catalog, is refused before any request with upstream's `Unable to verify image support for this model, …`, and a listed model without those tags with `request failed: SubscriptionNativeImageUnavailable` -- upstream's forced `vision` tool route is not ported (`vision` below). llmux decides for itself and its refusal is reported as it sends it. Each image is saved beside the session log, owner-only, as `images/image-<id>-<digest>.bin`, recorded by an `images_attached` event, and read back -- checked against its digest -- when the session is resumed; one that cannot be is left out with a notice. The badge is an OSC 8 hyperlink to the file the image was read from, as upstream's is (`writeImageBadge`, `image_attachments.zig:1336-1340`), in the composer, on the card and on a replayed card. A clipboard image is copied the moment it is pasted and its badge links that copy, as upstream's does (`insertImageAtCursor`, `image_commands.zig:138-147`): upstream's session exists from launch and its copy goes into it, xfx's opens with the first prompt, so its copy goes where upstream puts one with no session -- a private temporary directory, removed when the process ends (`session_store.zig:102-118`) -- and the session keeps its own once the prompt is recorded, which a resumed card links. A card's badges are the prompt's tokens of the images the turn sent (`expandPlaceholdersWithLinkedBadges`, `image_attachments.zig:1560-1620`): a typed `[Image #1]` that names no image of the turn, or one renumbered away from, is words and not a link. An image a request cannot carry -- over 5 MiB once encoded, over 8000 pixels a side (2000 once the request holds more than 20 images), or of a size its header does not say -- is left out of that request on either wire, and upstream's note goes in front of the text: `[Image #N not sent: <type> is <W>x<H> pixels. This request permits at most <limit> per side and 5 MiB encoded per image. The original is saved at <copy>. …]`, or `No local image file is available; ask the user for a smaller copy.` when the session keeps no copy. `<copy>` is only ever a copy xfx keeps -- the session's, a paste's own, or in an unsaved session an attached file's temporary one -- and never the file an image was attached from, which can change after it was attached, as upstream names only its own (`savedImagePath`, `noteAttachmentLocations`, `src/core/images/image_attachments.zig:1050-1062@481104fd`); the same image is named the same on every turn. The history keeps the image and every request decides again; the transcript shows no note. The line-oriented shell says where the command works. A path to an image pasted into the composer is its badge at once, with no notice -- a `/image ` the paste begins with, or an `/image` alone in the composer, is the command it was for and goes; a path over 20 MiB says so and stays as words, and so does one the read table denies (above), any other that does not load stays in silence, and a paste that names an image is never collapsed. A path typed into a prompt is attached as the prompt is sent, `/image`'s path rules and its words: one that is missing, not an image, not a file, or means nothing is left as words, one the read table denies is said and left as words -- the prompt still goes, since no edit to the draft would make the rule allow it -- and any other failure is said under `images` and the prompt waits with its draft. A path is a word that ends `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif` or `.webp`, `@`-prefixed or quoted or escaped, its sentence punctuation left after the badge; a quoted `@"…"` as the file picker writes it is the file's exact name. A path pasted into `/model`'s search is the words it is. `image_commands.zig:22-57`, `image_attachments.zig:14-18`, `:194-238`, `:1336-1386`, `:1685-1705`, `:1825-1866`, `image_attachments.zig:841-920`, `:1708-1760`, `:1872-1998`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:27-95`, `:355-377`, `:445-622`, `input_submit_runtime.zig:756-776`, `image_data.zig:157-197`, `:272-368`, `vercel_protocol.zig:778-817`, `orchestrator.zig:5971-5986`, `:7215-7227`. |
| `/images` | slash | implemented | Upstream's `/images [clear]`, "manage pending image attachments", Media (`commands.zig:477`): `* images: N pending` with one ` - <name> (<type>)` row per badge in the draft, `no pending images`, or with `clear`, `cleared pending images` and the draft emptied. Any other word lists. Neutral throughout. `image_commands.zig:58-84`. |
| `/paste` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row, "attach an image from the clipboard when supported", Media (`commands.zig:491`), and Ctrl-V the same. On macOS `osascript` found on `PATH` writes the clipboard as PNG into a private temporary directory, removed after it is read (`image_attachments.zig:1779-1823`); on Linux `xclip -selection clipboard -t image/png -o` is read, where upstream has nothing. Read on a thread of its own; the badge lands at the caret with no notice. `* images: no image found on clipboard` when there is none, `✗ images: failed to paste clipboard image: <Error>` otherwise. Takes no argument in the TUI. `image_commands.zig:86-104`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1561-1567`. |
| `/trace` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: "copy a private diagnostic trace", Product, no argument (`commands.zig:486`). Writes a private Markdown report about this session to `$TMPDIR` (or /tmp) as `xfx-trace-YYYY-MM-DD-HHMMSS-<12 hex>.md`, owner-only (`0600`) and synced -- written to a hidden stage (`.<name>.partial`) and renamed onto its name without replacing anything there, so a process killed at any moment leaves the whole report or none, where upstream writes under the name itself and a kill mid-write leaves a truncated report -- and puts the **file** on the clipboard: on macOS `osascript -l JavaScript` found on `PATH` runs upstream's pasteboard script with the path as its last argument and a five-second deadline; elsewhere nothing is run and the report is only saved. One notice with no topic, in upstream's words: `Trace copied to clipboard. Review and redact it before sharing.`, `Clipboard copy failed. Trace saved at <path>. …` (error), `Trace saved at <path>. …`, or `Could not create trace.` (error). The report holds xfx's own facts in upstream's section names where xfx has the fact: Summary (time, version and revision, platform, build channel, provider, model, effort, permission mode, workspace), Current State (session id and directory, turns, token totals, the credential **by its variable only**, pid; in the TUI its terminal size, `/stats` counters and work in hand), Configuration (the settings files, whether each was read, the layer each setting came from -- never a value), Problems, Network Calls (TUI, from the full transcript's records and the session-title requests, in the order they were made, as upstream's one ring holds every model call -- `writeNetworkCallsSummary`, `app_commands.zig:3005-3030`; a title request is in no transcript, as upstream's reports no event), Tool Calls, Permissions, Session Log (the log, turn by turn) and Transcript Timeline (TUI, what the screen was given, colour stripped). Text from a person, a model, a tool or a settings file is masked by upstream's rules plus `scripts/check-no-secrets.sh`'s credential shapes; `KEY=${VAR}` is left as written, as upstream leaves it; a daemon URL loses its credentials. Never uploaded. Takes no argument in the TUI. A session that ends with a report in flight joins it: the report is written whole, and its file copy is given a second and then stopped. **Differences:** no `Preparing trace...` notice replaced in place -- the document is append-only, so the outcome is the one notice -- and no `Failed to build trace.`, which building a string cannot reach; upstream's `ps` snapshot, renderer events and debug-log tail are absent because xfx has none of those facts. `## Context Compaction` is upstream's: the process's newest 64 compaction decisions and failures (`trace.zig`), the newest 24 one line each under `last=N failed=M`, in upstream's event names and details where xfx takes the same step, the provider's refusal masked, and the failed ones under `## Problems` (`writeCompactionSummary`, `app_commands.zig:2724-2775`; e2e `tui-compaction-activity.test.ts:506-549`) -- its line says `(always recorded)` where upstream's names its `FX_TRACE` switch, which xfx does not have. The TUI's report has upstream's `## Session Title` section (`app_commands.zig:2779-2816`) after it, as upstream's does; the line shell's, which asks no model for a title, says the session's title as a `session_title:` line of `## Current State` instead. The line-oriented shell writes the same report without the screen's sections, says `[shell] …` on standard output and `xfx: …` on the diagnostic stream for the two failures. `app_commands.zig:624-660`, `:2183-2290`, `native.zig:172-225`. |
| `/compact` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/compact`, "summarize context into a fresh window", Session, no arguments (`commands.zig:487`). It replaces the conversation's older turns, in what every later request carries, with upstream's compacted form: one user message, `<compacted_conversation>`, holding each compacted turn's user message and final reply **word for word**, what the assistant did in between, a line per tool call (`T1 read_file notes.txt (2 lines): <why>`) and the session's rules, facts, decisions, status and open entries (`checkpoint.zig:275-302`). The newest turns stay as they are -- at most four, within 40% of the room the request aims for after compaction -- and the oldest is never kept once a newer one is (`window.zig:20-26`, `:169-318`). When a turn did work besides its message and reply, or a message may set a rule (`ledger.zig:203-247`), the conversation's model is asked for notes with upstream's request word for word (`summarize.zig:159-162`, `ledger.zig:68-106`), no tools, at its lowest listed effort, once more on another model family through the Gateway when it fails or says nothing, and once more for the turns a reply skipped (`model.zig:103-131`, `summarize.zig:470-494`); otherwise nothing is sent. Inside a turn the request goes right after the conversation the turn was about to send -- its instructions, tools, tool choice and options but Ultra, every message, then upstream's after-the-conversation request listing every turn by how it begins and each tool call by a line -- when the room the conversation leaves fits it and the turns fit one request, the conversation's own model alone, and a follow-up the same way; one that fails, or does not fit, or follows a refused request writes the turns out instead (`askFirstNotes`, `summarize.zig:571-595`; `complete`, `text_completion.zig:68-80`; `CompactorCaller.send`, `:225-247`; `roomAfterConversation`, `window.zig:79-89`). A notes request is recorded nowhere of its own: the local usage ledger that kept its cost is retired (US-U5, as upstream's in `a41fc2d4`), and one a turn ran makes that turn's `usage_recorded` frame due even if the turn then fails. The checkpoint is a session event, `context_checkpoint {covers_through_seq, compacted}` (`session_event.zig:169-182`), so a resume continues from the compacted form while its screen still shows every turn. **Nothing is written into the transcript**: the activity row says `• Preparing compaction` then `• Compacting (Ns)` while it runs, nothing on success, and otherwise upstream's sentence -- `No context to compact.` (one turn, or none older than the kept ones) and the busy warning go after 1.5 s, a failure or `Compaction cancelled. Try /compact again when ready.` stays until Escape or the next line (`activity_status.zig:16-67`, `compaction_activity.zig:118-124`). While a turn runs or a prompt waits it is refused with the warning; a line typed during one waits and runs after it; the Escape pair or Ctrl-C stops it. Every request a turn is about to send -- its first, and each after a tool step -- is measured, and one that reaches 80% of the model's usable input has the turns before the one in progress compacted first, on the turn's clock, the turn going on with the compacted form (`settings.zig:8`, `orchestrator.zig:7286-7322`). The window is the catalog's, read in the background at launch as upstream reads it (`app_entry_runtime.zig:335`, `startWarmup`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:321-349`) -- a turn waits for that read while it is on its way and nothing else gives the window (`requiresResolvedRequestCapabilities`, `orchestrator.zig:5383-5395`; `resolveForRequest`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:614-669`) -- or, on the Gateway, the one upstream knows the id by (`contextWindowSize`, `vercel_model_policy.zig:13-42`); with neither the model never compacts by itself, as upstream's does not. The estimate is scaled by the provider's count of the request before it -- by its bytes, or with the same images by the text added -- and the compactor's room by the same ratio, until a compaction replaces the history that was counted (`calibrateProviderRequest`, `prompt_context.zig:153-171`; `compactionSize`, `orchestrator.zig:6187-6200`; `:7458`). A request the provider refuses as too large for the window -- a `413`, or a `400` in one of upstream's eight phrasings (`isContextOverflowFailure`, `orchestrator.zig:4428-4445`) -- is compacted for and sent again, once per turn, window or not, when nothing of the answer was shown (`provider_overflow`, `:8099-8124`). A request with nothing left to compact for it -- refused as too large, or still past the window once compacted -- is not sent, and the turn ends with upstream's line, `✗ system: request failed: ContextCapacityExceeded` (`orchestrator.zig:7343-7350`, `:7487-7490`; `retention_exhausted`, `window.zig:297-318`; `formatErrorBody`, `app_process_runtime.zig:113-126`). A compaction the turn's first request needs runs before the turn opens in the log, so one that fails leaves no turn behind and a resume shows nothing of it, as upstream keeps a failed turn only once it has execution (`finishExecutionOnlyFailureIfNeeded`, `finalization.zig:213-246`); one between a turn's steps that fails ends the turn with its steps kept and no error text, live or on a resume, as upstream's (concluded `final`, finish reason `failed`, `TurnPresentationOutcome.failed`, `types.zig:198-203`). A log write whose landing is not known is upstream's `SessionPersistenceUncertain` (`session_log.zig:561-574`), and its row says `Compaction could not confirm saved context. Reopen the session before retrying.` (`activity_status.zig:44-45`). The window is cut at an exchange, as upstream's is (`selectRecentContext`, `window.zig:169-251`; `split`, `:271-295`) -- a steer, and the answer it cut short, go with the tool step after them and those after the last step with the last (`after_tool_step_count`, `:219-224`): the newest exchanges stay within the kept room, and a turn cut inside of -- the one in progress, whose earlier reads a long turn can then compact and go on past, or an older one -- keeps its first message, images included, after the checkpoint, its earlier part carried as `Turn in progress, whose first user message follows this:` until a later compaction takes it to its end (`checkpoint.zig:290-295`; `contextHistoryRange`, `history_range.zig:36`); a compacted turn's images go with it, as upstream's text-only turns do (`compactor.zig:266`). The checkpoint frame gains `open` -- omitted when there is no part in progress, so a whole-turn checkpoint is written as before -- and may cover through an exchange inside a turn; an xfx before this refuses such a log as `session ... cannot be trusted and was not read: a checkpoint covers part of a turn`, and reads every other log as before; the xfx that first wrote such frames, which kept a steer with the step before it, refuses in the same words one whose cut falls before a steer, while this one reads the cuts that one wrote. The context meter keeps the last number a turn's provider said until the next turn's request, the compacted one, is measured: upstream's moves only on a turn's usage, and a notes request is never reported to it (`app_callbacks.zig:1447-1451`, `text_completion.zig:225-284`). **Not ported**: upstream saves each compacted turn and tool call as a record `read_tool_result` can open and folds earlier compactions into them (`records.zig`, `summarize.zig:173-185`) -- xfx has no such tool, so its request says the calls "will not be available later" and every compaction keeps the turns of the ones before it; the notes are not linted (`lint.zig`); a conversation larger than one notes request is clipped rather than sent in parts. The line-oriented shell has no activity row and says so, pointing at the full-screen shell. |
| `/settings` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: "browse and update settings", Appearance (`commands.zig:488`). **The TUI** opens upstream's settings menu in the band under the composer, which stays and is its search: `Settings N` and `All`, `Interface`, `Agent`, `Notifications`, `Advanced`, the active tab bracketed, a blank row, then one row per setting -- the label (the marked one bold, the rest dim) and, at two past the widest label in the filtered list plus four, its choices with the one in force bold, or its value alone in bold -- and `↑↓ navigate     tab category     ←→ change     esc close` on the hint row (`settings_catalog.zig`, `settings_menu_presentation.zig:73-324`, `input_presentation.zig:729-765`). Up and Down wrap; Tab and Shift-Tab change the tab and put the mark on top; typing filters by every word against the label, the description, the value and upstream's setting name (`No settings found.` when nothing is left); Escape closes it and empties the search. **Left and Right change the marked setting at once, through the setting's own command, and say so in its words** (`applySettingsCatalogChange`, `app_commands.zig:4110-4210`): `Status line context`, `session` and `workspace` are `/statusline <item>` with both of its notices; `Reasoning effort` is the session's effort (D2b's), its choices `default` and the levels the catalog lists for the model in force -- the value alone for a model with none while the default is in force, `default` alone when a level was carried over -- each change made the session's at once (a turn running keeps its own), written as `effort` and announced `* effort: saved to user settings (scope=user)` and nothing else; `Permission mode` (`ask  auto  full access`) is `/permissions <mode>`; `Sound level` (`off  on`) is `/sound <level>`; `Slash menu categories` writes `slash_menu_categories` and takes the category column off the slash popup, `Collapse tool calls` writes `collapse_tool_calls` and draws a tool group as its header alone (the running and cancelled rows go too; the header still counts every call), `Session titles` (`off  on`, Agent) writes `session_titles` and decides whether a fresh session's first prompt asks a model for the session's title (see the generated-session-titles row), and `Prompt history` writes `prompt_history.enabled` and, off, keeps a submitted line neither in the recall nor in the file -- each announced `* <slash menu categories\|collapse tool calls\|session titles\|history>: saved to user settings (scope=user)`, or in error with `active for this process but` when the write failed and the change stands. `Startup scrollback` writes `startup_scrollback` and is `/settings startup-scrollback <value>`: `* settings: startup_scrollback: off (applies on next launch)` and nothing else; the next launch with it off starts on the cursor's row under the shell's output, pushing only as much into scrollback as leaves eleven document rows (`prepareStartupViewport`, `app_lifecycle.zig:944-988`), and on -- the default -- pushes everything above the cursor as before. Switching `Collapse tool calls` on collapses the groups **still on the screen** at once -- each one's call rows and cancelled lines taken out where they stand, the rows under them moved up, the band floating up with them, and the group a turn is still writing rewritten as its header with the rows it lost given back (upstream re-projects the visible transcript, `app_commands.zig:4120-4133`); what is already in scrollback stays as it was. **Enter does nothing** except on `Model`, whose row is the model id in force: Enter or Right there opens the catalog `/model` lists **under the row**, inside the menu (`settings_menu_presentation.zig:15-19`, `:277-310`): up to six ids at the value column, the marked one bold, or `Loading models…`, `No models found` or `Models unavailable` dim four columns in; the arrows move its mark, Left or Enter uses the marked model as a picker does (`* Switched to …`) and closes the list, Tab closes it with the tab change, and Escape closes the settings menu and leaves the list up as `/model`'s menu, as upstream's does (`fx-settings-model-40x120-esc`). Each new key is profile-only, read at launch with upstream's defaults (categories on, collapse off, titles on, startup scrollback on, history on). `/settings startup-scrollback [on\|off]`, either word in any case, saves that value or, alone, the other one; any other argument is `✗ usage: /settings [startup-scrollback [on\|off]]`. `Fast mode` and `Ultra mode` are `/fast` and `/ultrafast on\|off` (D2c) when the row differs from the session -- the same `* fast: on` or `* ultrafast: requested on`, the same save, Ultra on turning Fast off -- with `off  on` only for a model whose catalog row offers the speed or while it is on, and the value alone otherwise (`optionCount`, `settings_catalog.zig:314-315`). **Not ported**, each with nothing behind it in xfx (`.prd/tui-phase4/ssot.md` §Tensions): `Sound level`'s `max`. Not matched: switching collapse **off** expands only the open group -- a group written collapsed never had its calls on the screen; settings backups and shadowing suffixes. **The line-oriented shell** has no menu and says where it is. |
| `/alias` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/alias [name] [command]`, "show alias availability", Extensions, between `/settings` and `/credits` (`commands.zig:489`). It takes the rest of the line, and every form -- bare, a name, a name and a command -- is upstream's one neutral notice, `* aliases: Aliases are not yet configurable.` (`commandHandleAlias`, `app_commands.zig:2043-2050`): nothing is kept, and nothing reaches the model. The line-oriented shell says the same sentence, `[shell] aliases: Aliases are not yet configurable.`, on standard output. **Difference:** upstream's help entry is also a search key of its `/help` catalog (`helpCatalogSpecMatches`, `command_specs.zig:457-473`), so a catalog search for `name` or `command` finds it there; xfx's registry has no help entry, so its catalog finds it by its name, its summary and its category. Receipt `.prd/tui-phase4/receipts/c7`. |
| `/statusline` | slash | implemented | Toggles the footer's three optional segments, each kept in the profile as `statusLine.{context,session,workspace}` and **off until the profile turns it on**, as upstream's are (`settings_catalog.zig:58-60`) -- so the context meter, which xfx used to draw whenever it had measured one, is now shown only after `/statusline context`. Bare, the TUI opens upstream's compact picker in place of the composer row: a bold `Status line` title, a blank row, `Context`, `Session` and `Workspace` each with `off  on` and the value in force bold, and `↑↓ navigate     ←→ change     esc close` on the hint row; the arrows and Ctrl-J/Ctrl-K move the mark (wrapping), Enter, Left and Right flip the marked item **silently** (nothing is written to the document unless the profile write failed), Escape closes it, and every other key is swallowed while it is up. `/statusline context|session|workspace` -- an exact, case-sensitive word -- flips that item and says `* statusline: saved to user settings (scope=user)` then `* statusline: <item>: on|off`; anything else is `✗ usage: /statusline [context|session|workspace]`. A write that fails says `✗ statusline: active for this process but not saved to user settings (<reason>)` and the change stays in force for the session. The segments follow the model in upstream's order: the session title (xfx's own -- the first line of the session's first prompt, cut to 32 cells -- where upstream shows a generated title and falls back to that same prompt when it has none), the context meter (`12k/200k 6%`, or `12k` when the provider publishes no context window, as on the Gateway; nothing before a turn has measured one), and the workspace's full path with ` (<branch>)` from the nearest `.git/HEAD` -- a linked worktree's `gitdir:` file followed, a detached HEAD shown as `detached:<12 hex>` -- placed last and cut from the left to the room the other segments leave. The branch is read at launch, when the segment is turned on, at the end of every turn and on a resize, not on every frame as upstream does. Profile-only: a project file's `statusLine` is ignored with a diagnostic. The line-oriented shell has no footer and no picker: there, an item named is flipped and kept in the same words, and a bare `/statusline` prints the three values and the usage. Not matched: upstream's shadowed-setting warnings and settings backups, and its rule that a workspace entry may not set `statusLine.workspace`. `commands.zig:494`, `app_commands.zig:2094-2107`, `:3863-3941`, `render.zig:312-470`, `statusline_identity.zig`. |
| `/stats` | slash | implemented | The terminal writer's counters, as upstream's `/stats` prints them: one neutral notice, `* stats: ansi_bytes=N, redraws=N, debounced_resizes=N, footer_updates=0, stream_chunks=0` (`app_commands.zig:1009-1022`). `ansi_bytes` is what the terminal took from the session loop's writer -- frames, appends, clears and queries alike -- by the kernel's own count, so a refused frame adds nothing and a torn one adds the prefix that reached the terminal; the launch's probe and mode set and the exit's restore are outside it. `redraws` is whole repaints that landed because something else wrote on the screen: a resume, a `/clear`, a Ctrl-L. `debounced_resizes` is bursts of resize signals answered once, at a size that really changed. The last two keys are literal noughts because upstream increments neither. Upstream's own `redraws` has no increment outside its tests at `c1db919`, so its line reads `redraws=0` where xfx's can read more. Takes nothing: with anything after the name the TUI answers it as an unknown command. The line-oriented shell draws no frames, so there it says where the counters are rather than printing zeros about frames that never existed. `commands.zig:473`. |
