Upstream's row: `show your provider's usage and spend`, Account, no arguments, help entry `/usage` (`src/builtins/commands.zig:454`). /cost, its alias until 0.0.13, went with v0.0.16: typed, it is `✗ command: Unknown command. Try /help.`, and the slash popup offers nothing for it. **The TUI** opens it in the band in place of the composer and writes nothing to the document (`src/ui/footer/usage_presentation.zig:19-878,1558-1625`): a heading, `usage:` bold and the sign-in's label -- `API key (AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY)`, the variable named, never its value -- with the status in the right corner (`updated 2m ago`, `nothing fetched today`, `updating…`, dim; a failure in normal weight, `rate limited, try again shortly, last updated 2m ago`), the name cut in the middle first and the status dropped only when a 12-column name would not fit beside it; the period table (`period  spent  requests  tokens`: `today`, `7d`, `month`, UTC days); the budget table when the cache holds one (`budget  size  used  avail  use%  resets`, no `resets` below 55 columns, no `size` below 47); and the model table for the period Tab picks (`model (month)  spent  share  requests  tokens`, share and then tokens dropped before a name is cut), at most 10 rows and a dim `N more` row summing what is below the window, which follows the selection; the selected row bold behind `❯`; table names bold, column labels dim, no colour in any theme. Enter opens the selected model's detail screen: `usage: <model>, model N of M`, its period, tokens (`input  output  reasoning  cache read  cache write`, narrowed below 62 columns) and perf (`cache hit  p90 ttft  p90 tok/s  errors`, narrowed below 48) tables, dashes for a period it had no usage in. Numbers as fx writes them: `$0.4335` below a dollar, `$1,234.56`, `1,234`, `104K`, `5.8M`, `81.0%`, `600ms`, `1.3s`, a tie rounded as Zig rounds it. With nothing to tabulate, one dim line: `fetching usage from AI Gateway`, `(press r to retry)`, `no usage yet`, or why nothing can be asked -- `not signed in to AI Gateway` / `(set AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY)`; `this provider's usage isn't shown yet` / `(use /provider and pick AI Gateway to see AI Gateway usage)` for `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` and llmux (the user's decision, 2026-11-06); `the provider settings can't be used` / `(send a message to see why, then reopen /usage)` for an unreadable provider setting or a refused `XFX_GATEWAY_URL`. An empty model table: `no usage today (UTC)`, `no usage in the last 7 days`, `no usage this month`. The hint row (`input_presentation.zig:794-813`): `↑↓ model     enter detail     tab period     r refresh     esc close`, `↑↓ next model     esc back     r refresh` on a detail screen, `esc close` with nothing to tabulate, each in fx's three widths. Keys (`app_input_runtime.zig:1196-1211,2127-2129,2177,2228-2244,2825-2832`): ↑↓ and Ctrl-K/Ctrl-J move the selection, on a detail screen too (the next model's details); Tab and → step the period forward, Shift-Tab and ← back, wrapping, never fetching, and doing nothing on a detail screen; `r` fetches unless a fetch is in flight; Escape goes back from a detail screen, then closes; the period is kept across opens; everything else is swallowed. **What is asked, and when:** nothing at launch; opening draws the usage cache at once and fetches past the 60 s floor; while it is up, `r` and the 15-minute timer; closed, nothing -- the event loop ticks the runtime only while the screen is up, and every way the screen goes closes the runtime with it; quitting, and a sign-in switch that lands while it is closed, cancel the fetch in flight. Every "now" is the usage clock (`crate::usage::clock`). **Where xfx differs:** a model name is spelled through `mcp::terminal_safe` (`\x1b`, `\u{202e}`) where fx shows `?` (`safeText`, `:367-385`), so a name with a hidden character is not drawn as another name; the no-sign-in line names no /login, which xfx has not (the sign-in waits on the user, #88); and the unreadable-provider state is xfx's own. Not here: the Codex and Grok views and their two hint lists, the fx login (member) scope and its budget fetch, deferred with their sign-ins (U9). Takes nothing: with anything after the name the TUI answers it as an unknown command. **The line-oriented shell** has no footer to show it in and says where the dashboard is. |
| `/credits` | slash | implemented | Upstream's `* credits:` notice, answered to `/balance` as well -- upstream's own alias, metadata on this row. On the Gateway, one authenticated `GET {origin}/coding-agent/v1/credits` to the origin a turn is sent to, with the turn's bearer and no redirect followed (`gateway.zig:725-822`); the answer's string fields `balance`, `used` and `plan`, one `key=value` per row in that order (`* credits: balance=42.17` with `used=…` and `plan=…` under it), `no data returned by gateway` when it has none of them, and an error for anything else in upstream's words -- `HTTP 404: not found`, `API access denied · HTTP 401 · …` for a Gateway error object, `invalid JSON response from gateway` (`gateway_error_format.zig:64-145`, `output_contracts.zig:1513-1541`). With no credential it asks nobody and says how to set one. With a provider selection xfx could not read it asks nobody and says the refusal a turn says (`provider=rejected: …`, quoting the key and the value): the Gateway it stands on is the compiled default, not a choice to send the key to. **On llmux** it asks nobody and says upstream's `Credits are unavailable for the selected provider.` (`gateway_provider.zig:89-101`): the daemon has no balance to report -- its reset-credits route is one account's rate-limit reset entitlements and its keys-usage route is per-key usage at llmux's own estimated prices (`llmux/src/proxy/server.rs:1583-1613`). **The TUI asks at once**, as upstream does (`app_commands.zig:2052-2082`): not on the work queue -- so a turn in flight does not hold it, a full queue does not refuse it, and a Ctrl-C meant for the turn does not drop it -- but on a short-lived thread of its own, started with every signal the session owns blocked so it is never waited for on the way out, with what to ask (the endpoint and key of the provider in force) as the runtime last published it. Where upstream blocks its UI thread on the request, the notice here arrives when the answer does. Takes nothing. **The line-oriented shell** prints upstream's `credits` command lines, `[credits] key=value`, or `[credits] error: …` on stderr. `commands.zig:490`. |
| `/status` | slash | implemented | Opens upstream's read-only panel in the band in place of the composer, and writes nothing to the document (`commandShowStatus`, `app_commands.zig:522-536@481104fd`): `Status`, a blank row, then one labelled row per fact, its value wrapped under one column -- the panel's layout, keys and scroll are its occupant's (`full-screen TUI`). The rows are upstream's, in its order (`StatusSnapshot.panelRows`, `output_contracts.zig:731-783`): **Session** first, `<id> · not saved yet` or `<id> · saved`, saved meaning `xfx --resume <id>` opens it now, and `none · this shell can't save sessions` when the store refused this shell's session; Model; **Provider** and **Endpoint** only off the Gateway -- the llmux daemon and its url, or `rejected` for a provider selection xfx could not read; Auth (the credential source's name -- an environment variable or `llmux-keyless-loopback`, never a value), then Auth help when one is owed; Permissions (`ask`, `auto`, `full access`); Workspace; Turns; Session grants; Step limit (`unlimited` for 0); Ultrafast (`requested` / `not requested`); Build (`<channel> · <rev>`). Every label and value is made terminal-safe. **The session's id is allocated at launch, in memory** (decided 2026-11-04 for P-status, reversible): upstream opens its session at launch, launching xfx and opening `/status` write nothing, so the id the first session will take is shown from the start and its first write is still the first prompt's -- or a `/rename` typed before one, which opens the session to hold its title; a reset shows the next session's id, a refused session's id is never asked for again, and a failure the store never saw (a workspace that cannot be scoped) keeps the id `/status` names. **The facts are the live session's**: the snapshot the runtime last published -- its configuration, the model the selector holds, the conversation's turns and grants -- with this shell's mode, reset and `/ultrafast` request in force now; read without asking the runtime anything, so it opens at once mid-turn. While it is up it is rebuilt on every tick its turns, saved state or grants move, keeping its scroll (`collectInfoPanelFacts`, `:541-552`): a session saved as its first turn runs, a turn that ends. The runtime publishes the open as it lands and the counts when a turn ends, so an "always" answered mid-turn reaches Session grants at the turn's end, where upstream's reads its live engine the moment the approval closes. With no credential on the Gateway it reads the profile settings once, at open, as upstream does (`session_commands.zig:283-296`), and a file that does not read is `Settings  couldn't be read: <error>` in place of Auth -- upstream's `InvalidSettingsFormat` for one that is not a JSON object (a directory in its place too, which upstream calls `StreamTooLong`) and `StreamTooLong` for one past 64 KiB, and xfx's `unreadable_settings` for one that could not be read, whose operating-system error upstream names and xfx's reader does not keep; a rebuild reads no file. **Where xfx differs** (approved divergences, `scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml` `status-panel-*`): a **Sandbox** row after Permissions (`none`, UPSTREAM.md deviation 2) -- the confinement in force is a safety fact upstream's panel has no row for; owner the user, until the next pin's review. No **Updates** row: xfx has no updater (deviation 3), until the sign-in and updater epic. No **Connected** row: upstream's lists the providers signed in, which xfx has none of; xfx's `provider_rejected` is a different fact -- a selection it could not read -- and it is said by Provider (`rejected`) and Auth help, which quotes the value as written only when it has a provider name's shape (a lowercase word of at most 16 characters, `[a-z0-9._-]` with a letter first) and gives any other value by its length only; `status`, `doctor` and a refused turn share the sentence. A value in that shape is shown as written whatever it is, so a short lowercase secret would be. Step limit says xfx's compiled 25 (deviation 1), Auth help names xfx's two variables where upstream's points at its sign-in, and the id is xfx's 30-character one, which wraps at 50 columns where upstream's 12 do not. Takes nothing: `/status now` is an unknown command. In the line-oriented shell it prints the `status` command's lines about that shell's own model and conversation. `commands.zig:455`. |
| `/sound` | slash | implemented | Upstream's switch for its notification sounds, which on xfx is the terminal bell. `/sound` toggles off and on (a toggle reads `turn_end` alone and never lands anywhere else), `/sound on` and `/sound off` set; each answers `* sound: <level>`. Any other argument -- a second word, `ON`, `max` -- is `✗ usage: /sound [on|off]` and moves nothing. Both switches move together and are saved as `notifications.turn_end` and `notifications.attention_required` in `~/.xfx/settings.json`, each through the profile's shared preference writer (`provider::profile::write_preference`: read, set the one key, staged `0600` file and rename, every other key kept). **The TUI rings `BEL`** -- one byte, its own vector after the tick's frame, accepted by the output check only as a declared bell intent and counted in `/stats`' `ansi_bytes` -- when a turn that answered or failed ends, once its answer has been presented rather than when the provider finished; and at once when a permission question or a batch of the model's questions opens, on either plane. A turn the user interrupted, and `/model`, `/setup` and a catalog load, ring nothing. Each switch gates its own event and is in force from the keystroke. The save runs on a writer thread of its own, so the UI thread never waits on the disk; the level is said once the thread has answered, after `✗ sound: active for this process but not saved to user settings (<reason>)` when the save failed, as upstream saves, reports and then says the level (`app_commands.zig:4014-4027`) -- everything typed after it waits behind it, and a `/clear` after it waits for the write and hands the line off with the old screen. Where a session starts: `XFX_SOUND` (`0`, `false` or `off` is off; any other word that is not blank is on), else the profile's `notifications` (each switch on its own, a workspace entry over the global file; profile-only, so a project `.xfx.json` cannot set it), else upstream's own split -- on for macOS, off elsewhere. **Not ported**: `max`, whose UI cues (input cleared, slash menu opened) are chimes; the AAC chimes and `/usr/bin/afplay` themselves, so where upstream on macOS plays a chime at a turn's end, xfx rings the bell (upstream rings it there only when the chime cannot be played, and always for a question); and upstream's other `on` cues -- startup, cancel, model switch -- which are chimes on macOS and bells elsewhere, and ring nothing here. **The line-oriented shell** sets and saves the same switch and says that the full-screen shell rings the bell and it rings none: its question is the stdin prompter `xfx ask` shares. `commands.zig:495`, `app_commands.zig:3959-4029`, `app_lifecycle.zig:812-817`, `:1714-1725`, `sound.zig:9-11`, `:152-215`, `hooks/notifications.zig:156-240`, `runtime.zig:10152-10163`. |
| `/workspace` | slash | implemented | Upstream's `/workspace [list|add PATH|remove PATH|clear]`, "manage additional workspace directories", Workspace (`commands.zig:496`, `app_commands.zig:198-328`, `:2119-2152`). **Bare**, the TUI looks at the saved directories again (only while nothing runs, as upstream's refresh) and opens upstream's compact picker in place of the composer: a bold `Workspace` title, a blank, `Primary` and the workspace's path, `Additional directories` and `n / 16`, a blank, then `❯ Add directory…  Grant access to another directory`, one row per saved directory with `Active · Saved` or `Unavailable · Saved`, and `Clear saved directories  Remove every saved additional directory` when there is one, the marked row bright and the rest dim (`compact_command_menu_presentation.zig:1009-1238`); `↑↓ navigate     enter use     esc close` on the hint row. Up/Down and Ctrl-K/Ctrl-J move the mark, wrapping; **Enter writes the marked choice's command into the composer and runs nothing** -- `/workspace add `, `/workspace remove <path>`, `/workspace clear` -- so a remove or a clear is always a line the user submits (`prepareWorkspaceMenuCommand`, `app_input_runtime.zig:2580-2609`); Escape closes it; every other key is swallowed. **`list`** says the snapshot, one neutral notice: `* workspace: primary=<path>`, `saved_suppressed=false limit=16`, `additional directories: (none)` or one ` - <path> saved=true command_line=false available=<b> active=<b>` row per directory (`renderInteractiveBody`, `output_contracts.zig:285-322`). **`add PATH`**, **`remove PATH`** and **`clear`** answer with the same snapshot and a change row, `add ../extra saved_changed=<b> runtime_changed=<b> launch_flag_can_restore=false` -- the path as typed, the list as resolved. A path is anchored at the workspace when relative, folded by its letters, then resolved by the filesystem, links and all, and the directory reached is what is saved; `~` is a name, not the home directory. Refused in upstream's words, `✗ workspace: Workspace update rejected: directory does not exist` (also `path is not a directory`, `the primary workspace cannot be added or removed`, `directory is not configured as an additional workspace`, `path is invalid`) and `✗ workspace: Workspace settings were not changed: additional directory limit reached` at the seventeenth. A change is the runtime's to make, between turns: while a turn runs or a prompt waits it is refused at once with `* workspace: Workspace changes are unavailable until the active and queued work finishes.`, and otherwise it is saved, read back and in force from the next tool call -- the next turn's scope, the next `/new`'s conversation, and the `@` index, which lists an added directory's files by their absolute paths after the workspace's (`file_index.zig:1032-1051`). Anything else is `✗ usage: /workspace [add PATH|remove PATH|clear]` -- upstream's usage, which leaves out `list` although it takes it. Home and `/` are admitted as upstream admits them; what any added root grants is the `ask --add-dir` row's. The line shell has no picker: bare is `list`, and the rows follow `[shell] workspace:`. The statusline's workspace segment stays the workspace alone, as upstream's (`buildStatuslineItems`, `app_render_runtime.zig:875-899`). Not matched: the three "durability is uncertain" warnings (xfx's write is a staged rename under the lock, and a read-back that fails is the `could not be reloaded` refusal instead), launch-only rows and `launch_flag_can_restore=true` (no launch flag). Receipt: `.prd/tui-phase4/receipts/w5/`. |
| `/version` | slash | implemented | In the TUI, upstream's `* version: <semver>` notice: the bare package version, no `v` and no build (`app_commands.zig:2153-2160`), whose channel and revision are `/status`'s `build_channel` and `build_revision` rows. Takes nothing there: with anything after the name it is an unknown command. The line-oriented shell prints the version, build channel and build revision on one line. `commands.zig:498`. |
| `/quit` | slash | implemented | Leaves with status 0. In the TUI, once the terminal is given back, a session that had a conversation is named on standard output in the dim grey -- `Continue session with: xfx --resume <id>`, on one row when it fits and with the command on the next row otherwise -- as upstream's `formatResumeHandoff` does (`render.zig:795-810`); Ctrl-D and the second Ctrl-C name it too, as upstream's do. A session nobody asked anything in is named nowhere and was never written: xfx creates the session at the first prompt. A session opened from the store -- by `/resume`, by `/fork`, by `--resume` -- is named whether or not it holds a turn, as fx v0.0.16 names a session whose log is on disk (D90, `tests/tla/session_manager/system-design.md:1536-1560@481104fd`): a fork made before the first prompt is (`fx-p5-effort-fork-fork-edge-40x120-quit`). With an argument (`/quit now`) it is an unknown command, as upstream's is. Answers to `/exit` as well -- upstream's own alias for the same command -- which is metadata on this row rather than a canonical name of its own: `/help` prints one line per command and says the alias on this one, `SLASH_COMMANDS` does not grow for it, and both front ends accept both spellings because both parse `SLASH_REGISTRY`. `commands.zig:457`. |
| other upstream slash commands (`/continue`, `/login`, `/logout`, `/background`, `/models`) | slash | deferred | Absent from `/help` and from the parser: an unrecognized slash line is one deterministic refusal that names the command and points at `/help`. `/mcp` was on this list and is a canonical command now, with its own row. `/exit` used to be on this list; it is answered now, as an alias on the `/quit` row above rather than as a command of its own, which is why the count of canonical names did not move for it. `/setup` was on it too and became a canonical command, which is why that count went from six to seven; `/reset` was on it as well and is a canonical command now too; `/provider` was on it until it became `/setup`'s canonical name, with `/setup` as its alias, which moved the count by nothing; `/resume` and `/rename` were on it too and are canonical commands now, and so are `/undo`, `/usage`, `/allowlist`, `/image` and `/images`. `commands.zig:414-457`. |

## Tools

Upstream's registry is the `all` list of `src/builtins/tools.zig`: 17 entries at c1db9191 (`:843-861`) and 16 at v0.0.16 (`:721-738@481104fd`), the difference `mcp_features`. (This line cited 26 entries at `:1351-1378` before; neither pin's file has its registry there.)
xfx advertises the 12 tools below, in that order, and nothing else:
7 read-only (`list_files`, `glob_files`, `grep_files`, `read_file`, `capability_search`, `skill`, `mcp_select_tool`),
3 mutating (`write_file`, `edit_file`, `create_folder`),
1 command (`terminal`), and
1 interaction (`ask_user_question`).
The two MCP tools are advertised only where xfx runs an MCP client -- the
TUI -- and after them, a configured MCP server's own tools once a turn has
selected them or called one by name (their row below). A server's tool is not read-only: it does
whatever its server does, so every call of one crosses the permission decision
too, and a stdio MCP server is a program xfx starts.

That split is the safety boundary, not a taxonomy. A read-only call is admitted
in every permission mode without asking anyone, because it changes nothing. Each
of the other four crosses a permission decision that mints a one-use authority
for one exact target and revalidates it immediately before spending it: `ask`
stops on every one of them, and `auto` admits only bounded reversible writes and
a reporting-only command grammar. **xfx can change files in your workspace and
start processes on your machine**, under no OS sandbox; those four rows are
where that begins.

The registry is a compile-time constant. `scripts/check-no-stubs.sh` reconciles
it against the `tool` rows below, and `tests/parity.rs` reconciles the counts
and the three groups named in this paragraph against the registry's real
`PermissionKind`s -- so the prose cannot drift from the code any more quietly
than a row can.

| Surface | Kind | Status | Notes and upstream evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `list_files` | tool | implemented | One directory level, sorted, ignored names omitted, capped at 100 entries with an explicit `... and more entries` line. A path that does not resolve is the preflight's `Path not found: <path>`, as the read tools say it; its other failures keep xfx's sentences, as `vercel-labs/fx@c1db9191` has no `list_files` to take them from. `tools.zig:509-532`, `list_files.zig:80-115`. |
| `glob_files` | tool | implemented | `pattern` plus optional `path` and `mode=matches\|count`. Sorted before it is capped at 100; skips ignored and gitignored directories; does not follow symlinks. A `path` that is a file is matched by its name, one match or none, as upstream's is (`glob_files.zig:189-198`); a root the system will not open is upstream's access-denied envelope, and any other failure to open it `Unable to resolve glob search root: <path> (<error>)` (`:104-109`), with upstream's error names and no reason on the row. `tools.zig:534-562`, `glob_files.zig:88-245`. |
| `grep_files` | tool | implemented | Literal substring search with `path`, `include`, `case_insensitive`, `mode=matches\|files_with_matches\|count`, `head_limit`, `offset`, and `context_lines` (bounded at 5). Regular expressions are not supported, matching upstream. A `path` that is a file searches that file, `include` judged by its name; one that is neither a file nor a directory is `Not a regular file or directory: <path>`; a root the system will not stat or a file root it will not open or read is upstream's access-denied envelope, or `Unable to stat grep search root: …` / `Unable to scan grep file root: … (<error>)` (`grep_files.zig:243-302`), with upstream's error names and no reason on the row. `tools.zig:564-597`, `grep_files.zig:161-530`. |
| `read_file` | tool | implemented | Line-numbered UTF-8 output with `start_line`/`line_count`, 400-line default, 2000-byte line clip, 256 KiB output cap, and an explicit sentinel stating how many of the file's lines were shown. Binary files are named, not dumped, and a target that is not a regular file (a directory, a FIFO, a socket, a device) is refused before a read of it could block. Its failures are upstream's: a path that does not resolve is the preflight's sentence with the argument as written (`Path not found: <path>`, `Path is outside the workspace: <path>`, `Cannot resolve path "<path>": …`, `tool_admission.zig:2465-2495`), as it is for `list_files`, `glob_files` and `grep_files`; its decoder's and validation's are `read_file.zig:44-101`'s; and a read that resolved and then failed is upstream's `tool_execution_failed` JSON envelope -- `read_file requires a regular file`, `read_file failed`, or the access-denied one, with the target and the error (`read_file.zig:184-216`, `tool_result_errors.zig:323-409`) -- sent to the model, shown whole in Ctrl-O, and with no reason on the row (`Failed src`), as upstream's in-call failures have none (`tool_presentation.zig:1253-1301`). **An image file** -- PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP by its bytes, not its name -- is attached to the result for the model to see, the text saying `image attached (<type>, <n> bytes)`, when the request could carry it: read whole, its size in its header, at most 8000 pixels a side and 5 MiB once encoded; otherwise `image not attached: <type> (<n> bytes); <why>. Use an available image tool to save a smaller copy to a new file, then read_file the copy. …`. The Gateway gets the image in a user message after the results, `Attached image(s) from the tool result.` and a `file` part, the result itself a `content` output; llmux gets an `image` block inside the `tool_result`. The image is saved beside the session log (`tool_images_attached`) and sent again with every later request and after a resume; on the Gateway a model the catalog does not tag for images is sent the result without it and fx's sentence why. `tools.zig:599-627`, `read_file.zig:119-372`, `:228-278`, `vercel_protocol.zig:679-739`, `execution_memory.zig:672-766`, `gateway_step.zig:256-283`. |
| `write_file` | tool | implemented | Creates a file, or replaces one that has been read in full and has not changed since. Same-directory staging, identity plus SHA-256 revalidation, atomic rename, preserved permission bits, parent `fsync`. An existing target is measured by `stat` before it is loaded and refused above the complete-read ceiling, so an enormous preimage is declined rather than allocated. The approval it asks carries the **pair**: the preimage it read and the text that would replace it, each escaped and bounded to 64 KiB at the permission boundary, so under `XFX_TUI=1` a whole-file replacement is reviewed on the screen the `full-screen TUI` row describes rather than as a digest and a 160-byte preview. A path with nothing at it shows `(nothing)` as its before; an existing target that is not UTF-8 is named as bytes rather than rendered lossily. `tools.zig:629-651`, `write_file.zig:1-237`, `file_mutation_contract.zig:566-617`. A made change tells the model upstream's `wrote <path> (<n> bytes)`, the file's size after it (`file_mutation_execution.zig:159-187`), which a resumed session's Ctrl-O lists above the diff. |
| `edit_file` | tool | implemented | Replaces exactly one occurrence of `old_string`. Zero or several occurrences are refused rather than guessed; an edit that changes nothing reports `No changes to <path>`. Same read proof, preimage bound, and replacement path as `write_file`. `tools.zig:653-680`, `edit_file.zig:1-275`. A made change tells the model upstream's `edited <path> (<n> bytes)`, the file's size after it (`file_mutation_execution.zig:159-187`), which a resumed session's Ctrl-O lists above the diff. **A file that is not UTF-8 is not edited**: fx v0.0.16 edits one and shows its stray bytes as `\\xNN` in the diff and in the saved edit (9db199e4, `dupeValidUtf8`, `text_utils.zig:444-460@481104fd`, `execution_memory.zig:1405-1406@481104fd`); xfx refuses it (`edit_file cannot edit <path>: it is not UTF-8 text`, `src/tools/mutate.rs:437-442`) -- and before that its read, `binary or non-utf8 file omitted (<n> bytes)` (`src/tools/read.rs:1162`), is no read in full, so the read proof refuses it first (`p5-transcript-diffbad`). No diff of one is drawn; a `write_file` over one reviews its before as `<n bytes of non-UTF-8 data>` (`non_text_summary`). |
| `create_folder` | tool | implemented | Creates a directory and any missing parents. An existing directory is reported as already present rather than treated as an error. `tools.zig:707-729`, `create_folder.zig:1-352`. |
| `terminal` | tool | implemented | `exec` action only. A recognized read-only command runs as an exact argv with no shell; anything else needs an approval and then runs through the platform shell with the exact command, cwd, and environment that were fingerprinted. Operands must be relative, free of `..`, and must resolve inside an authorized root. Commands that compile or run project code are **not** on the automatic route. Bounded output, wall-clock timeout, SIGINT cancellation, process-group kill, and exit/signal reported as facts. A command its turn stopped is sent `SIGTERM` first and killed once a 700 ms grace is spent, as upstream's cooperative stop (`command_runner.zig:85`, `:2473-2493`); the model is told `aborted by user`, and the TUI's full transcript shows what the command wrote and how it ended (`signal 15`). One past its deadline is killed at once, as upstream's timeout is a force stop (`command_runner.zig:3395-3400`), and the full transcript shows what it wrote and `timed out`; the model is told xfx's own sentence, where upstream's `shell` answers its JSON status record (`"state":"stopped"…"error":"TimeoutExpired"`) -- that record, the `action: run` / `yield_time_ms` / `timeout_ms` rows upstream's Ctrl-O lists under a command, and the per-call `timeout_ms` itself are its `shell` tool's schema, a tool-set difference rather than a rendering one: xfx's `terminal` takes `action: exec`, `command` and `cwd` and has one fixed ceiling. The command starts as a shell would start it, with no signal blocked and `SIGPIPE` at its default, whichever xfx thread runs it -- as does every child process xfx starts (`xfx::process`) -- so a `timeout`, a test runner's signals and job control work inside it. Captured stdout and stderr are escaped for `&`, `<`, and `>` before they are placed inside xfx's own `<stdout>`/`<stderr>` frame, so a program cannot close the quotation and counterfeit an `<exit_code>` or a project-instructions tag. An admitted `git` runs with `-c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null -c log.showSignature=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null --attr-source=<the empty tree>`, the `diff`, `log`, and `show` subcommands with `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`, and `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` in its environment. What that switches off, measured on git 2.54: the monitor, external diff and textconv, every attribute source but one (so no `filter`, `diff` or `merge` driver is selected), `gpg.program`, hook files (`git diff` and `git describe --dirty` write the index even with optional locks off, and `post-index-change` runs on that write), and `status`'s index write. What no option switches off makes the command ask instead: a hook defined in configuration (`hook.<name>.command`, git-hook(1)), which runs on that same index write -- auto runs no git whose configuration defines one, or whose configuration cannot be read -- and `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes`, which no option skips, is read before auto runs git -- for every git directory from the working directory up, a linked worktree's common directory and every submodule's -- and one that names a `filter=`, `diff=` or `merge=` (a macro's definition included), or cannot be read, makes the command ask. `--attr-source` and `core.attributesFile` reach a submodule's child git too (pinned: a submodule's own `.gitattributes` selecting a filter its own `config` defines runs on unhardened `git status` in the superproject and not on auto's). Not covered, and user-authored: an embedded `sub/.git` directory or an external git directory a `gitdir:` file names outside `.git/modules` is not discovered for its `info/attributes` (auto can neither write into a git directory nor complete one). `configured_hooks` reads the configuration of the repository the working directory finds, with what it includes, not each submodule's; a submodule child under `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` ran neither its hook file nor its `hook.<name>.command` on `status`, `diff` or `describe --dirty` (measured, git 2.54). Every command's environment sets `GIT_TRACE2`, `GIT_TRACE2_PERF` and `GIT_TRACE2_EVENT` to `0`: `trace2.*` targets come from the global configuration only and `-c` does not reach them. Git older than 2.40 refuses `--attr-source` and runs nothing, so there every git command the grammar recognizes fails, in every mode -- deliberately, failing closed rather than running without the option; a command the grammar does not recognize still runs unhardened through the reviewed shell after an approval; upstream runs its git without these (xfx is stricter: unsafe to copy). Durable actions are a separate deferred row. `tools.zig:85-95`, `terminal.zig:180-232`, `command_effect.zig:249-355`, `local_executor.zig:52-73`. |
| `skill` | tool | implemented | Upstream's tool, its description and closed schema word for word: `location` (required) and `resource` (`tools.zig:159-160`, `:542-572`). A `location` is the `skill:<namespace>:<root>/<leaf>` handle the turn's `<available_skills>` block gave out, resolved against that block's roots and refused as `skill failed: StaleSkillLocation` when the namespace is another catalog's or `InvalidSkillLocation` when the leaf is empty, `.`, `..` or has a `/`, `\\` or NUL in it (`Locations.resolve`, `skill_contract.zig:85-108`); any other location is the path it spells, looked up in the turn's catalog and, failing that, among the skills discovered afresh (`skill.zig` `prepareInput`). The skill is opened again and checked to still be the skill advertised -- a regular `SKILL.md` whose name has not changed -- through a handle on its directory opened without following a link, which the load reads through from then on, and the `resource` (empty or absent: `SKILL.md`) is read whole, every component opened from that handle without following a link, absolute paths and `.`/`..` refused as `InvalidSkillResourcePath`, at most 1 MiB of UTF-8, and only while its size and modification time are the same after the read as before it (`readSkillResourceFile`, `skill_invocation.zig:1371-1409`). A resource other than `SKILL.md` is given only while the held `SKILL.md`, read again from its start, still has the size, modification time and name it was checked with (`revalidatePrimaryIdentity`, `:1316-1329`), at every `offset` of the named form too; otherwise the answer is `skill failed: SkillResourceChanged`. A file replaced by a link to another is refused unread, and one replaced by another file is the one checked or nothing. Upstream compares no inode at load, and neither does xfx: a `SKILL.md` renamed over the held one after the check is not seen, by either, the handle still being the directory checked; the answer is `<skill_content name=… location=… resource=… complete="true">` and the text, or upstream's sentence for why not (`Skill "x" was not found at advertised location "…". …`). A discovery that skipped candidates puts `<skill_discovery_warning details="context_notice" />` in front. **Read-only, and what that means here**: it reads only under a discovered skill directory -- xfx's own roots, which include the managed root in the home directory and the workspace's ancestors, outside the workspace's scope -- changes nothing, and is admitted in every mode; a configured rule over the `skill` category decides it, judged on the skill directory's name. Its row is `Loading skill <name>` / `Loaded skill <name>`, or `Reading skill resource <resource>` for another file (`skill.zig` `presentation`). **The legacy named form**, which the advertised schema does not name, is decoded as upstream decodes it (`decode`, `skill.zig:28-84`): `name`, with or without a `location`, and an `offset` that only it takes, each refused in upstream's sentence when it is the wrong type (`skill field "offset" must be a non-negative integer`, `skill offset requires the legacy named resource form`, `skill requires an advertised location`). A name with no location is looked up among the skills discovered afresh, a name with one must be the name of the skill there (`prepareIdentity`), and the resource comes back a chunk at a time: up to 20 KiB from `offset`, cut back to a line break, as `<skill_content name=… resource=… offset="N" next_offset="M">` and the text, with `<context_limit name="skill_chunk_bytes" action="truncated" … next_offset="M" …/>` before the close while more is left (`loadByIdentityWithOptions` in chunk mode, `skill_invocation.zig:920-998`); an offset inside a character or past the end is refused. Its row is `Reading skill resource SKILL.md` once it pages past the start. |
| `ask_user_question` | tool | implemented | Advertised under its own `PermissionKind::Interaction`, which mints no authority. **The TUI session asks in the band**: `tui/worker.rs:452` builds one `TuiQuestioner` per session over the control channel the approval prompter already reads, and `Runtime::ready` hands it to every conversation it opens (`tui/worker.rs:647`), so a question the model asks there reaches a panel and its answer -- or the cancellation sentinel -- becomes the tool result. The other front ends build their `ToolContext` with no requester and answer with the fixed unavailable sentinel: the line shell (`interactive.rs`) and a non-interactive run (`xfx ask`, `app.rs`). **Observed end to end on a real pseudoterminal, against a release binary**, by `scripts/smoke-tui.sh`'s `23-question-panel` and `23b-question-cancelled`: one question of a two-question batch on the screen at a time, the first answered by ordinal and the second through the freeform slot, and the answer document read off the **next request's** tool result -- correlated by call id on a `tool` message, since the tool's name is advertised in every request -- as `[{"question":"Which depth?","answer":"Thorough"},{"question":"Anything else?","answer":"다시 묻지 마 — später"}]`, in question order and unclipped. Escape declines the batch, the model is told the byte-exact `(user cancelled the question)`, and the turn carries on; Ctrl-C stops the turn instead -- proved against a completion that asks **and then writes a file**, where the write runs when the question is answered and never happens when it is interrupted. Five **local adaptations** of the pinned upstream contract (`ask_user_question.zig`, `text_utils.zig`, `question_answer.zig`, `question_prompt.zig`) rather than verified differences, because on (4) and (5) only xfx's half has been read: (1) `permission_request_id` is not advertised; the approval-screen route is a separate P3-APPROVAL row. (2) The line-oriented shell returns the availability sentinel, because fx's interactive shell is xfx's *TUI*, not xfx's line shell. (3) Question, label, description and answer text are terminal-safe encoded and then size-bounded with a refusal rather than truncated, using the `max_output_bytes` reason when the encoded document itself would not fit the caller's limit. (4) Unicode in the synthetic `Other` freeform slot is implemented and observed: the shell decoder delivers `Input::Text(char)`, one insertion per (possibly multi-byte) character, and `23-question-panel` types `다시 묻지 마 — später` into the slot on a real terminal, checks the caret's column against `unicode-width`'s measurement of it, and finds that string whole in the answer document on the wire. Upstream's prompt inserts single bytes (`question_prompt.zig:322`) and its paste or IME route into that prompt is untraced here, so what is unverified is the **comparison**, not xfx's behaviour. (5) An ordinal for a choice the window is not showing is refused: xfx windows the options on a short screen and declines a number the user cannot see (`src/tui/question.rs`'s `an_ordinal_for_a_choice_the_window_is_not_showing_is_refused`), where upstream's `selectOrdinal` bounds only against the option count (`question_prompt.zig:389`) because its prompt has no window. `tools.zig:1374`. |
| tool permission modes | tool group | implemented | `ask` requires a real TTY approval, discloses a bounded excerpt of the change, states what "always" would grant, and denies when there is no approval channel; `auto` admits bounded workspace writes and a reporting-only command grammar that cannot compile or run project code; `yolo` skips policy and prints a warning to stderr. Above all three, and before any of them: `write_file`, `edit_file`, and `create_folder` refuse a target whose path passes through `.git` or `.xfx`, because those directories configure what xfx and git are then allowed to execute. The names are compared without case, since macOS's default volume is case-insensitive and `.GIT` reaches the same directory there. The set is explicit rather than every dot directory, so `.github` stays writable. Decisions mint one-use authorities that are spent before they are revalidated. Under `XFX_TUI=1` the `ask` approval is the inline band panel rather than a line read from the terminal, with the same disclosure and the same grant scoping; every other rule on this row is unchanged. **The line read from the terminal quotes nothing raw**: its summary and its "always" sentence carry a command, a search's pattern, a tool's label and paths the repository named, and both are spelled as the TUI approval spells a command -- an `ESC` is `\x1b`, a carriage return `\x0d`, a C1 control or a bidirectional, zero-width or invisible code point its `\u{…}` -- with the line break spelled `\x0a` too, since the next lines are the choices, and a backslash spelled `\\`, so a typed `\x0a` does not read as a break. The one field not spelled twice is a write's or an edit's quoted change, which arrives in the permission boundary's bounded excerpt (already `\\` and `\u{001B}`). The question opens with `SGR 0` and a scroll-region reset inside a cursor save and restore, so a conceal, a colour or a scroll region any other writer left is not in force where it is drawn; other terminal modes (the alternate screen, character sets) are not reset. Upstream's line-mode question (`fx ask --prompt-permissions`) writes its label as it is (`cli_ask.zig:4610-4612`) and the label is terminal-safe only for a shell command (`tool_presentation.zig:353-367`); any other tool's is its name and its raw argument (`:717`, `tool_dispatch.zig:597-613`), so a search pattern reaches the terminal as the model wrote it -- the same at v0.0.16 (`cli_ask.zig:3997-3999`, `tool_presentation.zig:756`). Unsafe to copy. **What xfx itself writes before the question cannot leave state for it either**: on `xfx ask` with a terminal stdout and in the line shell the model's answer arrives with every control blanked to a space except the line break, the carriage return and the tab -- the TUI channel's rule (`tui/bridge.rs`'s `inert`) with the tab kept -- and to a pipe or a file byte for byte; a turn's failure message, which can quote a tool name or call id the model sent, has every control blanked on any stream; and `ask --json` on a terminal writes `DEL`, the C1 controls, the line and paragraph separators and every default-ignorable code point (the bidirectional controls among them) as `\uXXXX` -- the same JSON to any parser -- where serde_json would write them as raw UTF-8, while a pipe gets serde_json's bytes. Upstream's `fx ask` makes the same terminal-or-not split for text (`selectOutputMode`, `cli_ask.zig:1381-1386`): raw bytes off a terminal (`:3556`), and on one the answer goes through its TUI presenter (`:3507-3518`, `ui/ask_presentation.zig:117-121`) rather than being written as the model's bytes; it picks its JSON mode before it asks whether stdout is a terminal (`cli_ask.zig:1382`). Reading asks nothing in any mode unless a configured rule asks about the read (the `/allowlist` row). `permission_gate.zig:72-121`, `command_admission.zig:18-149`. |
| automatic command grammar | tool group | partial | Deliberately narrower than upstream's auto classifier. Reporting commands only: no `&&` chaining, no package-manager build/test families, and no Cargo subcommand outside the alias-proof built-ins (`--version`, `-V`, `--list`, `metadata --no-deps`) -- an `[alias]` in a `.cargo/config.toml` that automatic mode may itself have written can redirect any externally implemented subcommand, so `cargo fmt` and `cargo clippy` are refused regardless of their own behaviour. Existing path operands are canonicalized and must stay inside an authorized root. Widening any of it is a review decision. `command_effect.zig:16-104`. |
| durable terminal sessions (`start`, `read`, `write`, `wait`, `monitor`, `resize`, `signal`, `close`) | tool group | deferred | Post-v0.1. A session id is a reference the model holds across turns and outlives the authority that created it. No such action name appears in the advertised schema. `terminal.zig:186-232`. |
| OS command sandbox | tool group | deferred | Upstream confines commands with a platform backend. xfx does not, and reports `sandbox=none` in `status`. `auto` bounds what xfx agrees to start, not what a started process may do. `sandbox.zig`. |
| file management (`delete_file`, `rename_file`, `copy_file`, `file_info`, `open_file`) | tool group | deferred | Post-v0.1. `tools.zig:1358-1364`. |
| `memory` | tool group | deferred | Post-v0.1. `tools.zig:1362`. |
| `semantic_search` | tool group | deferred | Needs an embedding index. Post-v0.1. `tools.zig:1363`. |
| web (`web_fetch`, `web_search`) | tool group | deferred | Post-v0.1; network egress from the agent is out of scope for v0.1. `tools.zig:1365-1366`. |

A decided default: not advertised, because its useful half is a network fetch of somebody else's instructions that a model would start on its own; `/skills add` installs from a local directory instead. |

| `capability_search` | tool | implemented | A deny on it refuses the search and no longer keeps a server's tools out of reach: a call by name is judged by the tool's own rules (the server tools row). Upstream's spec (`tools.zig:510-540`): `query` 1..4096 bytes and an optional exact `server`, upstream's decoder and its refusals (`capability_search.zig:33-84`). Searches the ready servers' tools with upstream's retrieval -- BM25 over the server, the tool's names, title and tags weighted three to one over its description, schema and the server's instructions, a clear match required, an exact name first, five per page (`capability_retrieval.zig:152-394`) -- and answers upstream's combined JSON (`{"skills":[],"mcp_tools":[{name,server,description,purpose,usage}],"counts":…,"total_matches":…}`, `state:"no_match"`, `mcp_state:"server_not_found"\|"server_failed"`). Every match is **selected**: its schema is advertised from the next model step on, within upstream's 64 KiB budget (`tool_search.zig:242-270`). A tool a configured rule denies outright is never found. **v0.0.16** (`47154a8f`): an open search -- no `server` -- that matches nothing ends with `"mcp_unavailable":[…]`, after `"state":"no_match"`, one sentence per server it could not look in, in the configuration's order and encoded as the rest of the answer is (`withUnavailableLocked`, `src/core/mcp_host/model.zig:282-294@481104fd`; `renderCombined`, `src/tools/capabilities/capability_search.zig:301-304`): `MCP server 'X' needs a login. Ask the user to run /mcp login X, then try again.` (`needsLoginText`, `model.zig:880-884`), or `MCP server 'X' is unavailable: <why>` (`it did not start` without one, `:708-719`); a search that found something, or one naming a server, adds nothing. `<why>` is the runtime's own failure text, so it reads as xfx's status words do (`p5-mcp-unavailable`: fx `its process exited with code 3; its last stderr lines: &quot;boom-stderr&quot;`, whose words belong to the runtime row, MCP-S). fx also names a server held back for a missing or unusable variable (`needs VAR set before fx starts.`); xfx's runtime has no such state, such a server fails, and its sentence is its failure's. Reading a server's list for a search is what makes its tool count known to the servers block (the server tools row). Its description drops upstream's two clauses about skills, because this build has no skill and no `skill` tool to promise (`CONTRIBUTING.md` rule 1). The cursor in a long result is an opaque string of xfx's own hash. |
| `mcp_select_tool` | tool | implemented | Upstream's spec and words (`tools.zig:635-662`, `tool_mcp_dispatch.zig:17-131`): selects one tool by its exact `mcp_<server>_<tool>` name, `Selected dynamic MCP tool \`…\`. Its executable schema will be available on the next model step; …`, or `Dynamic MCP tool not found or not allowed: …`. Its schema is closed (`additionalProperties: false`), as every xfx schema is, where upstream's leaves it open. Still advertised at v0.0.16 (`src/builtins/tools.zig:721-738@481104fd`), though a call by name no longer needs it (the server tools row). |
| `mcp_features` | tool | deferred | **Removed upstream at v0.0.16, and not advertised.** c1db9191 advertised it (`tools.zig:664-700`: resources, templates, prompts and completion on an exact server, under an `untrusted_external` envelope); v0.0.16's registry has no such spec (`src/builtins/tools.zig:721-738@481104fd`), `/mcp resource` and `/mcp prompt` answer "fx doesn't support MCP resources and prompts yet" (`src/core/mcp_host/verbs.zig:69-70`), and `p5-mcp-turn`'s request bodies name it nowhere. xfx follows: the model reaches no resource or prompt. |
| MCP server tools (`mcp_<server>_<tool>`) | tool group | implemented | A ready server's tools, named `mcp_<server>_<tool>` with upstream's sanitizing, 64-byte cut and `_2` suffixes, a name never reused for another tool in a session (`tool_names.zig:37-127`), advertised **only after a turn selects them**, after the registry, in the order selected, with the server's own input schema verbatim (`cap/fx-mcp-wire-40x120-bodies/003-completion.json`). **v0.0.16** (`0fb5cb8e`): a live tool the model calls by name with no search or selection first is selected then -- advertised from the next step on -- and its call goes on to be judged and run as any other, where c1db9191 failed it with `Dynamic MCP tool not selected for this model step` (`advertiseUnselectedMcpCalls`, `src/core/agent/runtime/orchestrator.zig:2497-2538@481104fd`; `p5-mcp-direct`, `p5-mcp-known`). A name resolves only as `mcp_select_tool` would select it -- on a ready server, not refused outright by the table in force, its schema within budget (`resolveUnselectedMcpTool`, `src/core/tooling/tool_runtime.zig:1039-1065`); one that does not is a tool xfx does not have, and the turn ends as it does for any such name. **Selecting it admits nothing**: every rule below decides the call exactly as it decides a selected one (`an_unselected_call_meets_every_admission_rule_a_selected_one_does`, every mode, rule kind and answer, each case against its expected outcome). So what keeps a server's tools from the model is a rule about the tools -- a deny on the alias, a glob over it (`mcp_*` included) or `*` -- and **no longer a deny on `capability_search` or `mcp_select_tool`**, which refuses those two and leaves a call by name to the named tool's own rules (an ask rule still asks, `auto` still holds it); fx is the same, its lookup handed the table and the tool's name only (`tool_runtime.zig:1042-1053@481104fd`). The servers block names a server's `tools="N"` only once the model's catalog has read its list -- a search, a selection or a call reached it -- never at a session's first request, and from the next turn on, since the block is the turn's; a reload that keeps the server keeps it, one that starts it anew does not (`modelSnapshot`, `src/core/mcp_host/model.zig:472-500@481104fd`; `Catalog.reload`, `:101-125`). fx reads every server whose name the called one could start with until one holds it (`ensure`, `:513-533`), and before it asks the table, so a refused name counts too (`toolSchema`, `:296-318`); xfx has every ready server's list already, so it marks the holder alone, before the table is asked. A call is judged first, as upstream judges a dynamic tool -- under its own name, with that name its one target (`tool_admission.zig:161-165`, `:2520-2525`): a configured rule (`"mcp_github_*": "allow"`), an "always" answered this session, or a saved-session rule decides it; `ask` puts it to the person; `full-access` runs it; **`auto` holds it**, because upstream sends such a call to a safety reviewer xfx does not have and an unavailable reviewer holds the action (`:1116-1157`). Then `tools/call` with upstream's leading progress token, under the server's `operation_timeout_ms`, the turn's stop cancelling it on the wire; upstream's `{"server","tool","result"}` envelope, its `result` as v0.0.16 reads one -- the server's `content`, its `structuredContent` when it sent one, and `"isError"`, `false` when the server left it out, and nothing else of the server's (`classifyCall`, `src/mcp/protocol/tools.zig:255-273@481104fd`, rewritten by `render`, `src/core/mcp_host/model.zig:835-850`), where c1db9191 passed the server's result as it came; a JSON-RPC error answering the call as `MCP protocol error <code>: <message>`, (`write_protocol_diagnostic`, `src/core/mcp/tool_result.zig:199-214@481104fd`; `p5-mcp-rpcerror`) and its `data` never told: that writer would append `; data=<data>`, but v0.0.16's runtime keeps only a failed call's code and message (`CallResult.failure`, `src/core/mcp_host/runtime.zig:117`, filled at `:2371` with `.{ .code = f.code, .message = try alloc.dupe(u8, f.message) }`) and `render` rebuilds the error from those two (`src/core/mcp_host/model.zig:841`, before the only `extract`, `:845`), so `parseProtocolError` has no `data` to fill `data_json` with (`src/core/mcp/features/tools.zig:308-311`) -- `p5-mcp-errdata` pins it with compact data, spaced and reordered data, and data holding a NUL, the same sentence on fx and xfx for each; the message the server's text as written unless it holds a NUL (`binary or non-utf8 tool output omitted (N bytes)`, `sanitizeModelText`, `text_utils.zig:535-538`) or is over 64 KiB (`MCP protocol failure: InvalidEnvelope`, `parseProtocolError`, `features/tools.zig:293-313`), and the whole held to fx's 64 KiB result cap with its marker, `\n... [tool result truncated for <tool>: original N bytes; cap is 65536 bytes]\n` (`prepareModelOutput`, `src/core/tooling/tool_result_limits.zig:7`, `:30-69`, `:126-143`), where xfx used to write its own `{"server","tool","error"}` envelope (an error object with no integer code or no string message is read here as code 0 or an empty message, where fx's wire refuses the frame, `failureOf`, `src/mcp/protocol/wire.zig:337-348`); `isError` as a failure, a `content` that is not an array or an `isError` that is not a boolean as no result at all, `MCP server 'X' ended the call: the server's answer was malformed. The tool may have run.` (`copyCall`, `src/core/mcp_host/runtime.zig:2363-2378`; `ended`, `model.zig:855-878`), and a stopped stdio server started again once within its `restart_limit` or upstream's `server_restart_failed`. The row reads `Running MCP <tool>`, then `Ran MCP <tool>`, all in the row's grey; failed, refused or stopped, the tool alone (`Cancelled <tool>`, `Failed <tool>: <reason>`; `dynamicMcpActionLabel`, `app_agent_runtime.zig:1376-1381@481104fd`), a failure's reason spelled as a server's text is (`encodeTerminalSafeInline`: controls and invisible or bidirectional code points written out). That reason is the tool's result, which differs from fx's while xfx tells the model its own envelope (`src/tools/mcp.rs:601-607`; ledger cause `mcp-call-error-text`). The group's header counts it as an `MCP call` (`2 MCP calls`), after `command` in a tie, and no longer as a command (fx v0.0.16 `tool_group_projection.zig:143-156`, `:542`, `:749`; captures `p5-mcp-turn`, `p5-mcp-live`). While it runs the activity row says `Running`, as fx v0.0.16's does: its `calling` and `N MCP calls made` (`activity_status.zig:102`, `:244`) never reach the screen (`render_input.zig:692-694`, `app_worker_runtime.zig:856`). |
