## Providers

| Surface | Kind | Status | Notes and upstream evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Vercel AI Gateway` | provider | implemented | Streaming completions over rustls. Request is `prompt`/`tools`/`toolChoice`; the response is a bounded SSE decode that requires a canonical `finish`. An HTTP endpoint override is accepted only for loopback. The completion and session-title client follows no redirect: a `3xx` is a status error the turn reports (`HTTP 307`) and a title question answers as `provider_error`, as upstream, whose completion request is `.redirect_behavior = .unhandled` (`client.zig:1561@c1db9191`, `:1576@481104fd`) and whose non-`200` branch returns the status (`:1717-1750@c1db9191`). **Divergence, kept on purpose:** a proxy from the environment (`HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`) is honoured over https, where it sees only a TLS tunnel and a remote endpoint may be reachable no other way; upstream's clients never load one -- `initDefaultProxies` is called nowhere under `src/` at either rev, so the Gateway client's `std.http` proxy fields stay null (481104fd's MCP pool only reads them, `src/mcp/io/http.zig:529@481104fd`). Over cleartext -- a loopback override -- no proxy is used, by the turn, the title, the catalog, `/credits` or the bill lookup, because one would read the bearer as sent. `src/builtins/gateway.zig:41`, `:759-765`, `src/core/gateway/gateway_json.zig:333-363`, `src/gateway/client.zig:2718-3272`. |
| `llmux` | provider | implemented | Streaming completions from a local llmux daemon over the Anthropic Messages wire. Request is `model`/`max_tokens`/`stream`/`system`/`messages`/`tools`/`tool_choice`, with consecutive same-role messages merged and `tool_result` blocks leading the message that carries them. There is no `thinking` field: pinning it to `disabled` is refused by the daemon for the default model (`fable` answers HTTP 400, measured 2026-08-22), so xfx gets adaptive thinking and **preserves** the blocks it receives rather than suppressing them. The response is a bounded SSE decode -- bounded per frame, per completion, and by tracked-block count -- that requires a `message_delta` stop reason, refuses an unknown one, and routes every delta by block index so a text delta naming a thinking or unopened block cannot reach the answer. Every content block is also **preserved verbatim in arrival order** (`thinking` with its signature, `redacted_thinking` with its payload, `tool_use` with its parsed input, and any unknown type exactly as it arrived), because Anthropic verifies the signature when a tool continuation or a resumed conversation replays the assistant's prior reasoning -- a rebuilt-from-text assistant turn is a 400 at the next step. Reasoning is never rendered: it is preserved for the wire and no renderer reads it. **Keyless**: a loopback request carries no `authorization` and no `x-api-key`, because that is what the daemon accepts as the tenant `local`, and xfx never reads or forwards an llmux key. That is enforced rather than assumed -- the endpoint must pass the loopback-service policy (loopback host, explicit port, no path, either scheme), and the client refuses `HTTP_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY`, so neither a remote URL nor a corporate proxy can carry a keyless prompt off the machine. Selected by the profile-only `backend` and `llmux_url` settings, which `xfx setup llmux` writes. An `error` frame inside an HTTP 200 fails the attempt where it arrives, and is replayable when its `error.type` is transient (`overloaded_error`, `rate_limit_error`, `api_error`) -- so the same upstream condition is worth the same number of attempts whether it arrives in-band here or as a 429 on the Gateway. |
| prompt caching and provider options | provider | deferred | Upstream sends `providerOptions`, `reasoning`, and Anthropic cache breakpoints. `src/core/gateway/gateway_json.zig:330-378`. |
| generation usage and billing | provider | partial | Each Gateway answer's bill is read from its stream: `response-metadata.timestamp` (UTC, strict), and on the finish `usage.inputTokens.{total,cacheRead,cacheWrite}`, `usage.outputTokens.{total,reasoning}` and `providerMetadata.gateway.{routing.canonicalSlug,cost,generationId}`, every piece upstream required before it counted one (`client.zig:3313-3395`, `:3428-3456`, `:3647-3670@c1db9191`); on llmux the daemon's two totals and cache counts. **Nothing adds a bill up since US-U5**, which retired the local usage ledger as fx v0.0.16 retired its own ("the session and profile usage ledgers, the usage sidecar file, Gateway generation lookups, and provider billing outcomes are removed", `a41fc2d4`): no frame records a request's cost, model or generation (the [session event log](#configuration-and-persistence) row), no `$0.00 llmux/<model>` accounting is kept for a llmux answer (the user's decision, 2026-11-06), and `/usage` reads the provider's reports ([AI Gateway usage reports](#providers)). **No bill is ever asked for**: a stream that names its generation and no cost -- upstream's `.deferred` (`gatewayUsageOutcome`, `gateway.zig:650-686@c1db9191`) -- is a reply with no bill, and xfx sends no `GET /v1/generation` at all (the route, its client and the TUI's reconciler are gone; `tests/ledger_compat.rs` holds the binary to having no such route, and a TUI resume of a session with a pending request, a turn whose bill was deferred and `/usage` to asking none). What is still read of a bill is its cache counts, which a compaction's notes request traces (`compaction model call … cache_read_tokens= cache_write_tokens=`), where upstream reads them off its usage (`text_completion.zig:271-273@481104fd`). **Not ported**: the cache and reasoning counts on each reply's own usage and `ask --json`'s `cache_read_tokens`/`cache_write_tokens` (`client.zig:3510-3519`, `cli_ask.zig:3855-3863@481104fd`), the later token-totals unit (U7). `src/gateway/client.zig:2496-2560`. |
| AI Gateway usage reports | provider | partial | What `/usage` reads since US-U4 (the screen is the `/usage` row). One authenticated `GET {origin}/v1/report?start_date=D&end_date=D&group_by=model&api_key_id=self` per range of UTC days (`reportPath`, `src/usage/report.zig:196-210@481104fd`), its `results[]` summed per model and sorted by cost, at most 256 models of 1 to 256 bytes (`parseModelRows`, `:222-280`), and fx's status mapping: 401/403 refused, 402/404 not available, 429 rate limited, 5xx server, anything else -- a redirect included -- a bad response (`statusFailure`, `src/usage/usage.zig:889-896`). The key goes only to the origin a turn is sent to, under the bearer rule: no redirect followed and no proxy over cleartext; the answer is capped at 1 MiB and the request at 15 s, connect to last byte (`usage_response_max_bytes`, `usage_request_timeout_ms`, `src/gateway/client.zig:268-269,299-308,418,430-431`). Only `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` is asked (fx's `api_key` scope); `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` and llmux are *unsupported provider*, as fx maps every source it has no reader for (`src/core/app/usage_host.zig:72-80,128`) -- the user chose fx's answer for both on 2026-11-06. An unreadable `provider` or `backend` setting is refused before anything is sent, as a turn refuses it (xfx's own state: it leaves the provider at the Gateway default, which must not be what sends the key). The fx login (member) scope, the budget, Codex and Grok are deferred with their sign-ins. **Where xfx differs, on purpose:** a report whose model name holds the key is refused (fx would show it, and cache it); a declared length over the cap is refused before the body is read (the same `network` failure fx reaches after reading 1 MiB); and a failure's diagnostic is a status code or a fixed phrase, never a byte of the answer; and a cancelled fetch stops before its next range and drops the request in flight. **Where serde_json cannot copy Zig's `std.json`:** a duplicate key anywhere refuses the report, as fx's default `duplicate_field_behavior = .@"error"` does, and an integer past `i64` is not a number, as fx's `number_string`; but an integer literal past `u64` is read as the float of that size (fx: refused in a field it reads), and a literal past `f64` or nesting deeper than 128 refuses the whole body (fx: only a field it reads). |
| AI Gateway usage dashboard runtime | provider | partial | What `/usage` runs on since US-U4 (the screen is the `/usage` row): the TUI opens it with the sign-in the runtime last published, ticks it only while the screen is up, closes it with the screen, tells it a sign-in switch and quits it when the session ends; its clock is `crate::usage::clock::now_ms`, the one the capture harness pins in a debug build. fx v0.0.16's `Dashboard` (`src/usage/usage.zig:229-616@481104fd`) and fetch policy (`src/usage/policy.zig`): opening shows the usage cache at once and never waits on the network; a fetch runs on open past a 60 s floor, on `r` whenever none is in flight (the floor never holds `r`), and every 15 minutes while open through the same floor (`policy.zig:8-53`); one fetch reads today, and the finished days of the 7d window and the month once per UTC day (`plan`, `:55-89`); a worker thread stores the answer through the cache, an answer the cache would refuse is the `cache` failure (`usage.zig:705-708`) and an unwritable cache shows the answer from memory (`unsaved`, `:713-723`); every snapshot re-reads the file for what other processes stored (`reloadEntryLocked`, `:484-505`); the screen redraws when the redraw counter moves or the minute turns (`app_commands.zig:849-860`); a clock corrected backward never holds a fetch (`policy.zig:30-36`). `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` and llmux open as *unsupported provider* and an unreadable provider setting as *provider rejected*, from the report client's question. Closing leaves the fetch in flight running, as fx's `close` does (`usage.zig:300-304`): it finishes and stores its answer under its own sign-in, so reopening within the floor shows fresh numbers from the cache, and nothing new starts while closed. **Where xfx differs, on purpose (requests that carry the key):** a sign-in switch (a different key, or the same key on another Gateway origin), an unavailable sign-in and quitting cancel the fetch in flight, so no further request goes out with the old key and a switch fetches the new sign-in at once instead of owing it a fetch (`fetch_owed`, `:236-238`); letting go of the fetch and the worker's store race for one compare-and-swap: a worker that loses drops its answer, and one that won just before -- its answer already in -- stores it under its own identity, the old key's (fx stores it too, `:596-601`), which a dashboard switched to another key or to no sign-in never shows (the same key on another origin shares the identity, so it can show that answer from the cache); the cache entry and the floor are the key's, as fx's identity has no origin (`identityOf`, `cache.zig:21-35`), so the same key on another origin shows the numbers cached from the first and waits out the same floor; each fetch carries the epoch it started in and closing moves it: a fetch that ends after a close stamps its key's floor with the time it failed (not the time it was taken in), clears an older failure when it kept its answer, stored or held in memory because the cache cannot be written (fx's `finishJob` clears it on any `.stored`, which its `unsaved` also is, `:587-595,713-723`), and shows its numbers and its own failure only through the cache; a store reads the time under the cache's lock, so a later today another process stored while it waited is never taken for one stamped ahead of the clock (fx reads it before the store, `:697-705`); a sign-in switch that lands while `/usage` is closed -- a provider switch, a resume onto another provider -- cancels the old key's fetch in flight as well (`Dashboard::sign_in_changed`; fx sees a switch only at `open`, `:596-601`); the 60 s floor is kept per key across switches and unavailable sign-ins (fx forgets it on a new sign-in, `:465-470`, so switching away and back fetches again at once); a fetch cancelled by a switch or quit holds the floor, as fx's does (`.cancelled => {}`, `:612`); a worker thread that cannot start is a `network` failure that holds the floor and gives the timer a start (fx records nothing and retries on every pass, `:570-574`); and quitting waits at most 1 s for a cancelled worker, then lets it go (fx joins it). The Codex and Grok plans, the member scope and its budget fetch are deferred with their sign-ins. |
| transport-owned retry and team routing | provider | deferred | xfx's turn owns attempts and sends no team header. `src/gateway/client.zig:1459-1494`, `:1810-1825`. A run of 5xx answers therefore ends xfx's turn after its third attempt (`gateway::DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`), about a second in, where fx v0.0.16 keeps retrying: the terminal program status reads `error` from then on where fx's still reads `working` (`p5-lifecycle-osc-retry`; the `terminal program status (OSC 7501)` row). Owner unit: **RETRY-BUDGET** (queued). |
| `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` credential | provider | implemented | Resolved when nonblank; highest precedence. Reported by source name only. |
| `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` credential | provider | implemented | Resolved when nonblank; second precedence. Reported by source name only. |
| `llmux` keyless loopback credential | provider | implemented | Resolved from **configuration alone**: an `llmux_url` that is present and passed the loopback-service policy. Not "the daemon answered" -- resolving a credential does no I/O, or `status` and `doctor` stop being safe to run. Reported as `llmux-keyless-loopback`, never as a secret, and `auth_refreshable` is false. Provider-scoped: it is not a Gateway credential and a Gateway key is not a substitute for it, so a provider with no credential is a refusal rather than a fallback to another provider's. |
| `fx login` credential | provider | deferred | OAuth credential store. `src/core/auth/auth_runtime.zig:685-700`. |
| stored API key credential | provider | deferred | Keychain and profile-stored keys. `src/core/shared/types.zig:90-96`. |
| Codex / ChatGPT subscription | provider | deferred | Second provider family. `src/core/shared/types.zig:90-96`. |

## Configuration and persistence

| Surface | Kind | Status | Notes and upstream evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| project settings `.xfx.json` | persistence | implemented | Upstream `.fx.json`. Profile-only keys are ignored with a diagnostic. `config_runtime.zig:341-379`, `:548-576`. |
| profile settings `~/.xfx/settings.json` | persistence | implemented | Upstream `~/.fx/settings.json`. `config_runtime.zig:381-403`. |
| profile home `~/.xfx` | persistence | implemented | Every writer that can be the first to need it -- the session store, the settings writers (`xfx setup`, `/settings`, `/allowlist`, `/workspace`, the TUI's error report), the MCP profile (`/mcp add`, `/mcp remove`, and their `--project` form, whose edit of the workspace's `.mcp.json` holds the profile's `mcp.lock` as upstream's does, `profileDir`, `src/core/mcp_host/config_file.zig:147-155@481104fd`), the prompt history, `/skills create` and `/skills add` -- makes it the one way (`crate::profile_home`), and the usage cache (`~/.xfx/usage-cache`, a library with no caller yet) makes it the stronger way, on descriptors (`mkdirat` `0700`, opened `O_NOFOLLOW | O_DIRECTORY`, `fchmod`ed only when it made it, an existing one that is a link or not private refused, a missing home refused), saying its refusals in its own words: a missing `~/.xfx`, and each missing directory below it the writer needs (`skills/`, `skills/<name>/`, `.skill-install-locks/`, an install's transaction and every directory it copies), is made `0700` from its creation, one level at a time, whatever the umask. A home directory that is not there is refused (`the home directory <path> is not there, and xfx does not make one`), as upstream refuses it (`Store.initFromHome`, `src/core/config/settings_store.zig:343-347@481104fd`). **A `HOME` that is unset, empty or relative is no home at all**, fx v0.0.16's rule (`homeDir`, `src/core/shared/io.zig:475-483@481104fd`, 21a55a45; xfx's one reader, `crate::config::home_dir`, which every profile path, `~` in the file index and the usage cache come from): only a path that starts at `/` is a home, `..` components and all, so `relative/home`, `./home`, `~` and `../home` are unset, and no profile path is ever built relative to the folder xfx was started in. Before, xfx dropped only an empty one, and a relative `HOME` naming a folder under the workspace put the profile in the project: the session store's `sessions/<id>/events.jsonl` and `session.json`, and `history.jsonl` with its lock (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/relative-home/`). With no home, nothing is made: `xfx ask` refuses to record (`--no-save` still asks), `xfx setup` refuses to save, `sessions` and `session` say `HOME is not set`, `status` reports no session and `doctor` warns that it cannot read the session store. Children still inherit `HOME` as it is (the terminal tool's environment, an MCP server's six variables), as upstream's do. **With no home the TUI runs the session unsaved, as fx does** (its `v2_store` stays null, `initializePersistence`, `src/core/app/app_session_runtime.zig:1438-1449@481104fd`): the launch says `! history: durable prompt history unavailable` after its configuration notice (`app_bootstrap_runtime.zig:667-673`, `prompt_history_runtime.zig:49@481104fd`), every turn runs and is recorded in memory only (`SessionStore::unsaved`: no directory, no log, no lock, no manifest, no image copies, no rules file, its paths under `/dev/null` so a write that reached one would fail), its images held in memory and sent again on every turn as fx's turn keeps them (D44, `src/core/session/session_store.zig:43-50@481104fd`); an attached file -- by `/image`, a pasted or dropped path, or a path typed into the prompt -- gets a private temporary copy when it is attached, as fx captures every attachment into its process temporary folder (`captureImageAttachment`, `app_session_runtime.zig:1331-1344@481104fd`; on its paste route `input_paste_runtime.zig:490-493`, on its typed-path route `input_submit_runtime.zig:1218-1223`), and the note for an image a request leaves out names that copy on every turn, a pasted image its paste copy. The copies are written only under the trusted temporary root, fx's real path of `/tmp` (`createTempSnapshotDir`, `image_attachments.zig:246-273`) and never `$TMPDIR` as given, so a relative or in-project `TMPDIR` cannot put one in the working tree: a fresh `0700` directory of a random name, each copy `0600`, a name already there unlinked and made again rather than written through (a symbolic or hard link planted at it is not followed), a write that fails removed at once. A copy that cannot be written refuses the attachment, as fx's capture refuses it (`captureImageAttachmentForAdmission`, `image_attachments.zig:328-347`): `✗ images: failed to attach image: <name>`, no badge from `/image`, a pasted path left as words, a typed path stopping the prompt with the draft kept. Removing them is best effort: on a normal exit or a panic's unwind the directory goes with the shell; a `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` or `SIGHUP` that ends the process re-raises the signal without destructors, and the copies stay where they are, owner-only in their owner-only directory, as paste copies always have, and what needs a saved session says so in fx's words: `/status` `Session  none · this shell can't save sessions` (`src/core/session/session_commands.zig:404-408`), `/resume` `✗ session: unable to list saved sessions: SessionStoreUnavailable` over a picker that reads `Unable to load sessions.` (`writeSessionPickerError`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2436-2447`), `/fork` `! session: /fork needs a saved session` before it asks whether a turn runs (`openForkMenu`, `:2072-2076`), `/rename <title>` `✗ session: no active session to rename` with the window's title kept (`renameActiveSession`, `:3141-3146`; `app_commands.zig:3031`), `/permissions remember` and `revoke` `saved-session permission rules require an active saved session` (`app_commands.zig:610-617`), and `/quit` names no `--resume` line; `/new` starts another unsaved session and the history line is not said again. An approval's "always" lasts the process. The line shell still refuses to open, `` xfx cannot record a conversation because no home directory is set; run `xfx ask --no-save <prompt>` to ask without recording `` (`crate::interactive::NO_STORE`): it is xfx's, with no counterpart in fx. An existing `~/.xfx` that is a link, not a directory, another account's, or open to group or other is **refused by every one of them and never `chmod`ed**, in the session store's words (`` <path> cannot hold session state: it is mode 755; session state must not be reachable by group or other (`chmod 700 <path>` makes it private) ``; `/skills` says it after `Failed to create skill '<name>':` or `Failed to install:`; `/mcp`'s verbs after `Couldn't add <name> to <file>:` or `Couldn't remove <name> from <file>:`, where upstream says `something went wrong` (`describe`, `src/core/mcp_host/verbs.zig:313-326@481104fd`); the error names upstream gives the two cases, `DurablePathUnsafe` for a link or a file and `PrivateStatePermissionsUnsupported` for another account's or an open one, are what the profile's whole-file writer the verbs replaced answers, test-only now), and nothing is written in it or where a link points. Below it, the two kinds of directory are told apart as upstream tells them apart: **private state** -- `.skill-install-locks/`, and the store's `sessions/` and `sessions.staged/` -- is held to the same rule, a link there refused (upstream opens it with `follow_symlinks = false`, `openOrCreateVerifiedPrivateChild`, `src/core/shared/io.zig:712-742@481104fd`; `acquireSkillInstallLock`, `src/builtins/skills.zig:684-702@481104fd`); **user content** -- the managed root `skills/` and a skill's `skills/<name>/` -- is used as it is when it is there, and followed when it is a link to a directory, so a skills directory linked into dotfiles keeps working (`createSkillTemplate` → `makeDirRecursive`, `src/builtins/skills.zig:247-252@481104fd`, `src/core/shared/io.zig:1077-1093@481104fd`). Outside the usage cache the checks are on paths, with a trusted `$HOME` and stable ancestors; an opener that pins the directory it checked (the cache's `open_dir_in`), and access-control lists (which the mode bits do not show on macOS), are not handled. **Divergence, for the session store's reason:** upstream `chmod`s an existing `~/.fx` it can open to `0700` and goes on (`openOrCreateVerifiedPrivateChild`; `Store.initFromHome`, `settings_store.zig:370-381@481104fd`), and makes `~/.fx/skills`, a skill's directory and an install's transaction with the default directory mode (`makeDirRecursive`; `createInstallTransaction`, `src/builtins/skills.zig:669@481104fd`; `ensureDir`, `:786-798`) -- so its `/skills create` on a machine with no `~/.fx` leaves one the umask's mode until a settings write repairs it. xfx keeps its home private from the start and stops at one that is not: a `~/.xfx` that has become a link or another account's redirects or exposes everything in it, and silently changing a directory xfx may not own would be a worse answer than stopping. Workspace `.xfx/` directories are not the profile's: nothing here makes them, and the write tools refuse a path through one. Nor is a workspace's `.mcp.json`, which `--project` edits: it is the project's shared file, replaced where it is and keeping its mode, a link or a file with another hard link refused, as upstream writes it (`write`, `src/core/mcp_host/config_file.zig:157-171@481104fd`); only its lock is the profile's. |
| exact-workspace settings entry | persistence | implemented | `workspaces["<root>"]`; exact match only. `config_runtime.zig:405-443`. |
| environment overrides | persistence | implemented | `XFX_MODEL`, `XFX_PERMISSION_MODE`, `XFX_MAX_AGENT_STEPS`. Blank values are ignored. `config_runtime.zig:445-453`. `HOME` is read only when it is an absolute path; an empty or relative one counts as unset, and children still inherit it unchanged (the `profile home` row). `XFX_TUI=1` opts a bare invocation into the partial TUI (see the `full-screen TUI` row); any other value, and an unset one, keep the line-oriented shell. `XFX_THEME` fixes the TUI band's palette to exactly `light` or `dark`, case-insensitively, and suppresses the start-up background query; any other value is ignored and the terminal is asked instead (see the `colored and hyperlinked TTY output` row). `XFX_SOUND` decides where `/sound` starts -- `0`, `false` or `off` silences the session, which is what a harness sets, and any other word that is not blank starts it on -- and `/sound` still moves and saves it (see the `/sound` row). `XFX_SKILL_SYMLINK_AUTHORITIES` is upstream's `FX_SKILL_SYMLINK_AUTHORITIES`: colon-separated absolute directories a link in a workspace skill root may resolve into, beside the profile-only `skill_symlink_authorities` (see the `/skills` row). |
| provider selection `provider` + `models` + `backend` + `llmux_url` | persistence | implemented | Profile-only, like `model` and `permission_mode`: a shared repository must not be able to choose which endpoint receives a prompt, so a `.xfx.json` that sets any of these is dropped with the ignored-key diagnostic. `provider` and `models` are read as of this release and are **profile-only** like the rest, so a cloned repository still cannot choose the endpoint. `provider` names a provider tag directly; `models` maps a provider tag to that provider's model, and `models[<active provider>]` outranks the flat `model` **inside one layer** while the layer order still decides between layers. When `provider` is absent it is derived from `backend`; when both are present, layer order decides first — the newer layer's key wins regardless of which key it is — and `provider` wins over `backend` only on equal sources. Any two surviving readable values that differ are reported as a `config` check named `conflicting_provider_selection` with both values and the winner explained, because a machine whose profile says two different things about where prompts go is exactly the machine an operator should be told about. An unreadable value of **whichever key is newest** is kept and refuses every turn, quoting the key and the value; no default is substituted -- nor beside the turn: while it stands, the catalog read at launch and by `/model`, the first prompt's session title, a resumed session's pending bill and `/credits` send the Gateway key nowhere. Reading never rewrites: `status` and `doctor` leave the file byte-identical, and the new keys appear only when something that already writes the profile writes them -- `xfx setup <provider>` -- through the same staged `0600` file and rename that preserves every unrelated key, and the writer keeps `backend` and `model` in sync with them for as long as the selected provider has a value an older binary can reach; when it does not, the legacy keys are left at their previous values rather than given invented ones. `backend` is read case-insensitively and trimmed. `llmux_url` is held to the **loopback-service** policy, which is *not* the `XFX_GATEWAY_URL` rule: a loopback **address literal** (`127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` -- the name `localhost` is refused, because it resolves wherever the resolver says) with an explicit port and no path, under either scheme, and never userinfo. Neither llmux client honours a proxy or follows a redirect, because the policy can only see the URL xfx names and both are ways a reply could send the request elsewhere. A remote host is refused even over https, because the request travels with nothing to authenticate it and TLS does not make it local -- that is what keeps `auth=llmux-keyless-loopback` true by construction. A refused value is a diagnostic rather than a string anything downstream may trust. **Neither key falls back, and neither inherits.** A later layer whose value is refused clears the accumulated one rather than deferring to an earlier layer, so the operator's most recent word is never silently overridden by an older one. An unreadable `backend` is kept as the rejected value and refuses every turn, quoting it, because guessing would send the prompt and the Gateway credential to an endpoint nobody chose; `backend: llmux` with no usable url refuses and names `xfx setup llmux`. Neither is fatal to `status` or `doctor`, which still describe a machine whose settings are broken, and `doctor` reports both as a `provider` Fail. No new environment variables. The runtime axis and every rendered field are called `provider`; `backend` is the settings key that selects it. |
| config diagnostics | persistence | implemented | Non-fatal; surfaced as `doctor` `config` checks and said at every launch as upstream says them: the TUI writes `* config: <n> configuration issue(s) (ctrl+o to view)` in the document and the diagnostics, one `<layer>: <cause>; key=<key>` row each, on the full transcript only (`writeCollapsedStartupNotice`, `app_bootstrap_runtime.zig:267-272`, `:666-687`); `xfx ask` writes `xfx ask: config <layer>: <cause>; key=<key>` per diagnostic on stderr after the yolo warning (`cli_ask.zig:1716-1727`), and the line shell `xfx: config …`. xfx adds its note after the key (for a bad action, which word it could not read), each line one terminal-safe line of at most 256 bytes. `config_runtime.zig:578-593`. |
| `ask --no-save` | persistence | implemented | Load-bearing: the default records the turn, and this flag opens no store at all, so nothing is created under `~/.xfx` -- not a session directory, not a manifest. It conflicts with the resume flags, because continuing a conversation while refusing to record it would fork its history in silence. |
| session event log | persistence | implemented | `~/.xfx/sessions/<id>/events.jsonl`. An `assistant_message` records the provider's own replayable state under **two disjoint fields**: `raw_content` is Anthropic Messages blocks and is only ever written by that wire, and `responses_state` is OpenAI Responses items for the wires that produce them; a turn writes at most one. `wire` names the wire **and its authority** that produced them, and is written only when there is state to replay, so a record with nothing to carry is byte-identical to one an older binary wrote. Replay is keyed by authority, not by shape: a session recorded under one wire and resumed under another **drops** the state -- with a one-line notice on stderr naming both wires -- and replays text and tool calls as normal. The stored items are not deleted, so a later resume back onto the original authority replays them. A record naming a wire this binary does not know drops for the same reason rather than being guessed at. Both fields are additive and absent on older records; neither needs a schema version bump. A `tool_result` likewise keeps, beside what the model was told, what the person watching was shown: `ending` (unchanged, denied, exited with a code, signalled, timed out, cancelled -- an unknown kind reads as `other`), `lines` (added and removed) and `change` (the reviewed diff window), each omitted when there is nothing to say, so a resume replays the row and the full transcript as they were; none is read back for the model. One typed JSON frame per line carrying schema version, log generation, contiguous sequence, unique event id, and timestamp. Append + `fsync`, then an atomically replaced manifest publishes the exact byte boundary and its SHA-256. Readers stop at the boundary, so an unpublished, malformed, or truncated crash tail is invisible; a writable open truncates it. A sequence gap, a repeated event id, a digest mismatch, a boundary past EOF, or an unsafe session id all fail closed. Exactly one writer per session: an exclusive advisory `flock` is held for the handle's life and a second writer is refused, and every append re-checks the log's length so a change from outside xfx is refused rather than written over. Directories are `0700`, owned by the current user, with nothing granted to group or other, and are re-checked on every use -- by every read command as well as every write, and both directories rather than only `sessions` -- rather than only on creation; files are `0600` (Unix). xfx's own Gateway credential is never persisted: no variant of the event union carries it, and the credential source is reported by name only. **Model-read content is persisted**: a `tool_result` event stores a file's contents or a command's output verbatim, so a secret the model was asked to read is on disk as owner-only plaintext until the session is deleted, and `--no-save` is the only way to record nothing. A `tool_feedback` event records what the user said when they answered one call's approval, correlated by `call_id` and deliberately not a `user_message`, which would open a turn mid-batch and orphan the results still to come. It is written under **schema version 1**, which is not bumped: the envelope check is an equality, so a bump would make every existing log unreadable to solve a problem in the other direction. The consequence is stated rather than papered over -- a new binary reads every old log, but an **older** binary reading a log that contains a `tool_feedback` frame refuses that frame as an unknown variant, so preview and stable mix forward but not backward across a session whose approvals were amended. `session_started` and `preferences_changed` carry an optional `provider` (the provider's label), as upstream's preferences do (`session_codec.zig:17-23`, `session_event.zig:734`, `:1979-1995`): written when a session is opened and when a resume goes on under a provider other than the one the log last named, absent from logs written before it existed -- which replay as before -- and ignored by an older binary, an additive field inside variants it knows; a resume keeps a Fast binding only when the provider, the model and Fast are the ones the session last ran under (`restoredFastModeModelBound`, `app_session_runtime.zig:6318-6328`). `permission_grants_cleared` -- written by `/permissions reset`, withdrawing every `permission_grant_recorded` before it -- is added the same way, under version 1, with the same consequence for a session that was reset. A `usage_recorded` frame keeps the answering step's two totals, which the context meter and a replayed summary read, and `turn_output_tokens`, what the turn's summary row said; a turn that failed after a request completed, or after one was sent and lost, writes one too, ahead of its conclusion, so its row replays as it was shown. **The local usage ledger is retired** (US-U5, as fx v0.0.16 retired its own, `a41fc2d4`): the frame no longer carries `requests` (each request's time, duration, model, tokens, `cost`, `generation_id` and the `origin` a pending bill was owed by), `unaccounted` or `lines_added`/`lines_removed`, and the runtime no longer writes its frames about a later bill, `settled` and `rejected`; a compaction between turns writes no frame. **A log written with them still loads, resumes and replays**: those fields are read past -- the variant ignores a field it does not know, the envelope alone denies them -- so an old frame replays as its totals and an old `settled`/`rejected` frame as nothing, as fx reads its old `usage` fields past (`session_event.zig:1808`, `session_codec.zig:1464-1469@481104fd`); a fork, which writes each frame again, writes them without. `tests/ledger_compat.rs` holds a session the build before (`120f7b20`) wrote in its TUI, with every one of those fields, to resuming, replaying (five prompts and answers sent back in order, totals 15600/920) and appending frames with none of them; an older binary reads what this one writes, a known variant with fewer fields. A `turn_concluded` frame for a turn stopped while its automatic compaction ran carries upstream's `cancellation_origin: "compaction"` beside its reason, written only then (`ConversationInterruption`, `session_event.zig:100-160`), and a resume replays that turn as its card alone, from the field rather than the sentence (`app_session_runtime.zig:4796-4800`); a frame without it is the turn's own stop, as every log before it, and a value other than `turn` or `compaction` is refused as upstream's reader refuses it. Every interrupted turn also records `terminal_reason: "cancelled"|"failed"` -- upstream's `InterruptedTerminalReason`, its event's `reason` (`session_event.zig:101`) -- and a resume picks the turn's last line by it (`app_session_runtime.zig:4796-4803`); a frame without it is read by its sentence, as before. A `tool_result` carries `created_at_ms`, when it was recorded, as upstream's persisted result does (`execution_memory.zig:52`), and a resumed call's full-transcript entry is stamped with it (`transcript/runtime.zig:5826`); one without it carries its turn's stamp. `session_log.zig:2185-2195`, `session_replay.zig:158-207`, `session_event.zig:169-202`. |
| `/usage` tracking start `~/.xfx/usage.json` | persistence | implemented | **Retired with the local usage ledger** (US-U5): it was `{"tracking_started_at_ms":N}`, written once by the first turn that recorded a request, which said whether one of the ledger's windows reached back past it -- upstream's `coverage` record (`~/.fx/usage.jsonl`, `usage_report.zig:500-540@c1db9191`). xfx neither writes nor reads it any more, and **an existing one is left in place, byte for byte**, as fx v0.0.16 leaves its old `usage.jsonl` and `usage-v2.json` "in place and ignored" (`a41fc2d4`): the binary does not hold the name (`tests/ledger_compat.rs`), a turn makes none (`tests/sessions.rs`), and one an older build wrote is unchanged after a turn. Removed, not repurposed: `/usage`'s own cache is `~/.xfx/usage-cache/` ([AI Gateway usage cache](#configuration-and-persistence)). |
| AI Gateway usage cache `~/.xfx/usage-cache/` | persistence | partial | What `/usage` draws first since US-U4, under the profile's home. fx v0.0.16's `~/.fx/usage-cache/<identity>.json` (`src/usage/cache.zig@481104fd`), beside `<identity>.lock` and, between write and rename, `<identity>.tmp`, in fx's schema 2: `schema_version`, `identity`, `today {day, at_ms, models}`, `finished {day, at_ms, days_7, month}`, `budget {at_ms, value}`, and `codex` and `grok` written `null` (`Wire`, `:172-195`). The identity is fx's SHA-256 over `fx-usage-cache-v1`, the sign-in kind, the subject (for an API key, the key) and the team, in hex (`identityOf`, `:21-35`), so the key never reaches the file and a fixture seeded for fx seeds xfx -- which assumes a high-entropy key: the digest is unsalted and its prefix public, so a short key could be guessed offline from a file name. Reads take no lock; a store takes the exclusive lock (advisory: it orders xfx processes, and the directory and its writers are trusted -- another process of this user could still swap the stage before its rename), reads, merges -- each part the newer, a part stamped in the future yields, finished days by day (`merge`, `:88-113`), the caller's own copy counted too -- and writes a `0600` stage it renames into place (`store`, `:261-297`); what the reader would refuse is never written. A file over 4 MiB, from another schema or identity, or with one row, name or number fx refuses (256 rows, 1-256-byte names, finite non-negative numbers, `:197-236`) reads as absent, never partly. An unset, empty or relative `HOME` is no cache (`usage.zig:524-529`, 69a01a40); a missing `~/.xfx` is created `0700` (`:537-547`). **Divergences, kept on purpose:** a report whose model name spells the key -- any 12-byte run of it as is, in hex of either case, or in base64, standard or URL-safe, at each of the three alignments, where a shifted run keeps only its whole groups so the shortest base64 run is 9 bytes; not percent-escapes, base32 or a reversal (`report::KeySpellings`) -- is refused before it can be cached, where fx refuses nothing and would store it. It guards against an accidental echo, not a hostile Gateway: the configured Gateway is trusted with the key (the bearer rule), and the session log keeps its reply text anyway (`SessionEvent::AssistantMessage`). fx reads the file and writes the stage through a link (`readFileAlloc`, `:247`, `writeFile`, `:293`: Zig's `OpenFileOptions` and `CreateFileOptions` have no `follow_symlinks`, 0.16.0 `lib/std/Io/Dir.zig:498-629`; fx pins 0.17.0); xfx opens `~/.xfx`, `usage-cache` and every file in it without following a link and refuses -- leaves in place, writes nothing -- a link, a hard-linked file, a directory or a FIFO where one belongs; `~/.xfx` and `usage-cache` must also be owned by this user with nothing granted to group or other, the session store's rule (`verify_store_dir`), or the cache is refused on read and on write, not repaired; what xfx creates it sets to `0700`/`0600` on the descriptor whatever the umask (fx's are the umask's). A `codex` or `grok` part that is not `null` refuses the file: xfx has neither sign-in. An entry whose rows each pass but whose period sum overflows to infinity is refused too, where fx would add it up; no real report comes near. So is a day outside `0..=i64::MAX/86400000` or a stamp before 1970, and a row list is refused at its 257th row while it is read, not after every row was built. Creating the lock retries `ENOENT`: two processes creating the same name at once get it on macOS (measured 2026-11-06), where fx's store fails and shows the fetch from memory. A store waits at most 5 s for another holder of the lock -- the lock wait, not a bound on the whole store -- where fx's lock waits without a bound. |
| session manifest and index | persistence | implemented | `session.json` is a rebuildable projection: it summarizes the log and names the boundary it was computed from, and any disagreement between the two refuses the session rather than preferring either. The manifest is staged under a process-unique name and cleaned up by RAII, so xfx never unlinks a stage another writer owns. The listing is a projection over manifests, deterministic (newest first, ties by id) and bounded: the whole directory is sorted before the scan cap drops anything, the newest candidates survive it, and `truncated` says so, so `last` can never mean an arbitrary old session. `session_projection.zig:15-43`, `:248-280`. |
| session resume | persistence | implemented | `ask --resume <last\|ID>` and `--resume-id <ID>`, and a bare `xfx --resume <last\|ID>`, which opens the TUI on that session -- refused before the terminal is taken when it names nothing or another xfx holds it, announced as upstream's `* session resumed: <title>` with the saved turns replayed under it as they were shown live -- with no welcome line and no reset of what the shell had printed, as upstream's launch does -- and continued by the next prompt with its history and grants. The TUI's `/resume` picker resumes from inside a session (its own row). `last` is scoped to the current workspace and never crosses one; an exact id may be resumed from another workspace and writes an explicit durable `workspace_rebound` event before the turn, keeping the origin root intact. History and the recorded model are restored -- whatever the profile chose, unless `XFX_MODEL` names one (`cli_ask.zig:1035-1042`, `:1810-1811`); `ask`, which goes on with the configured provider, restores it only from a session recorded on that one -- and the **permission mode is not**, because a `--yolo` turn recorded once must not become a default later; the mode and model a resumed `ask` runs under are written just before its turn. **The TUI does not restore a recorded model xfx would not have written** (`model_id_problem`), since it paints the restored model in the hint row and `/settings`: it goes on with the launch's model, and the first turn after the resume records that one -- the open itself writes nothing -- so the log is clean again once a turn runs. `xfx ask --resume` applies the same check to the model it sends, and prints its history notices, which quote the wire and the model the log gave a step, with every control character made a space. Upstream refuses the event frame carrying such a model as invalid (`parsePreferences`, `session_event.zig:1972-1976`, over `validateModel`, `session_codec.zig:67,3165` at `c1db9191`; `:68,3830` at `481104fd`, ASCII controls only); xfx keeps the conversation openable, because the field is the only one that is bad and the launch has a model of its own to put in its place. `session_store_types.zig:130-141`. |
| session fork | persistence | implemented | The store's half of upstream's `/fork` (D58; `Session.fork`, `src/core/session/session_adapter.zig:2436-2457@481104fd`; `openFork`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:1714-1868@481104fd`): `SessionStore::fork(source, before_turn, workspace)` makes a new session holding the source's turns before `before_turn` -- before turn 1, a session with no turn holding what was set before the first prompt; turn 0 refused; a kept turn that has not ended refused -- whose line 1 is its own `session_started` with `forked_from {id, seq}` (an optional field, which an older xfx reads past), then the source's events through the frame that ended the last kept turn and nothing written after it, then the source's title as it is called now, marked ` (fork)` once. The source is read without its lock and never written. `/fork` (its row) is what reaches it. Where it differs from upstream, and why: the kept events are **re-framed**, not copied byte for byte -- an xfx frame carries its log's generation and an event id, so a copied line would be refused as another log's -- at the source's sequence numbers and times, so a checkpoint's coverage and a turn's timing hold in the fork; only the images the kept frames name are **hard-linked** (upstream links every blob), each checked against its recorded digest first and copied owner-only when a link is refused or would not be a private file of this account, and a pasted image's recorded path names the fork's own copy; a missing or damaged one refuses the fork, as upstream finds no fork point past one; **a grant the source has withdrawn since S stays withdrawn**: when the copied frames recorded an "always" the source no longer holds, a `permission_grants_cleared` follows them and then the grants the source still holds, where upstream copies lines 2..S as they are -- a withdrawn "always" must stay withdrawn, not come back under a new id; **the saved-session rules are the source's remembered denies, as they stand, and no allow**: upstream's are log lines it copies as of S (`setPermissions`, `session_adapter.zig:2205-2208@481104fd`), xfx's are `permissions.json` beside the log, which has no S, so the fork's own file holds each deny with its id and generation under the source's next generation and nothing that widens it (the `/fork` row says what follows) -- read with `O_NOFOLLOW`, written in the stage owner-only through `link(2)` and read back, and a store that cannot be read, or is a link, refuses the fork; the title is cut where a character ends to fit the 240-byte limit with its mark, and every control character in it -- C0, DEL and C1 (U+0080-U+009F) -- is a space, because the reducer refuses a title past the limit or with C0 or DEL and a terminal may read C1 (upstream checks none of these); line 1 keeps the source's roots, which the copied rebinds are checked against, and the fork is rebound to the workspace it is made in as a resume rebinds, where upstream writes the caller's workspace on line 1. Built in `~/.xfx/sessions.staged/<id>` -- every folder made `0700` from its creation, then chmodded, so no umask opens it in between; a `sessions.staged` that is a link, open, or another account's is refused as an insecure parent -- and renamed into `sessions` once its manifest is published, so a listing never meets one half made; one the process died building is left there, owner-only and inert. |
| permission rules and grants | persistence | implemented | Configured rules from settings and session grants recorded when a user answers "always". A command's key is its text **and** its working directory, so an approval in the workspace does not carry into a `--add-dir` root; a mutation's key is the target's **canonical absolute path**, so a grant recorded in one workspace does not authorize the same relative name after an exact-id resume rebinds the session to another one. The approval prose still names the file the way the user does. **An "always" answer persists across processes for the same durable session id**: the approval prompt says so and names the id, and `session` lists every standing grant by exact `tool` and `target` rather than as a count. `/permissions reset` withdraws them durably: a `permission_grants_cleared` event empties the list a resume reads, and only answers given after it stand. Configured *rules* are upstream's table under `permission` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` (the profile's, or this workspace's entry, whole-table replacement), read at launch and written by `/allowlist` (its row above). **A table that does not read refuses every tool** (divergence, unsafe to copy): when the profile is there and does not read, or its `permission`, its `workspaces`, this workspace's entry or that entry's `permission` is not what it must be, the launch puts `"permission": "deny"` in force -- every tool left out of the request and every call refused, in every mode but full access, which consults no rule -- and says `permissions: every tool is refused: a permission table did not read (<reason>)` -- in full access, which consults no table, `permissions: full access consults no table, so it is not applied; every other mode refuses every tool: a permission table did not read (<reason>)` -- (TUI warning and line shell, with `; fix it, then /allowlist reads it again`; `xfx ask` on stderr, judged by the mode the run is in, `--yolo` included). A bad value of another key, or anything in the project file, does not close the table; a grant stays exact, never glob-shaped. Saved-session rules (`/permissions remember`, its row above) persist per session in `~/.xfx/sessions/<id>/permissions.json`. **An amended approval is carried, and carried as context**: when the approval channel returns a sentence beside its answer, that sentence reaches the model as a user message placed after *every* tool result of its step, and is journaled as `tool_feedback` with its `call_id`. After all of them, because the two wires serialize the same prompt differently and only one ordering is unambiguous on both: the Anthropic wire **merges** a run of same-role messages and **hoists** the `tool_result` blocks to the front of the merged one, so a sentence placed between two results of a batch would arrive above the result it was about, while the Gateway wire writes each message literally, one wire message per xfx message, in order. Placed after every result, the sentence keeps its meaning on both; the `call_id` in the journal keeps the correlation the position no longer carries. It never edits the arguments that ran and never widens, narrows, or re-keys a grant. A `fatal` result journals the sentences of the calls that completed and sends no request; a cancelled step journals none. **Interim**: no shipped surface produces one yet -- `TtyPrompter` answers `y/a/n` and reports none -- so the draft editor in the approval band is still outstanding (P3-APPROVAL Unit 3). `permissions.zig`, `session_permission_state.zig`. |

Omission markers are bounded by the same file cap and name the reason. Containment is decided on canonical paths, so a symlinked directory inside the workspace cannot pull in an external file -- neither its bytes nor its provenance; a link that resolves inside the workspace is ordinary layout and is delivered once, under its real scope. | `ask --add-dir <PATH>` | persistence | implemented | Repeatable. Authorizes one extra directory for this turn, beside the directories `/workspace` saved for the workspace (each skipped, not fatal, when it is not there); a path named here that is not a usable directory fails the turn before any request is sent. An added root is a root like the workspace: reads, and in `auto` the same bounded writes, as upstream admits a write contained in an active added root without review (`tool_admission.zig:665-679`; e2e `write_trusted_added`, `tests/e2e/file-tool-paths.test.ts:1170-1179`). What `auto` refuses there, and in the workspace, is upstream's list of files that decide what runs later -- `.zshrc`, `.bashrc`, `.bash_profile`, `.profile`, `.ssh/authorized_keys`, `.ssh/config`, `Library/LaunchAgents`, `Library/LaunchDaemons`, `.config/autostart`, `.config/fish/config.fish`, `.git/hooks`, `.git/config`, `.git/config.worktree` (`sensitiveAutoWriteTarget`, `tool_admission.zig:679-693`, the same at `481104fd` `:678-692`) -- which upstream sends to its reviewer and xfx, with none, leaves to `ask`. **xfx refuses more, on purpose** (with home as the workspace, upstream's list let an ordinary write install code that runs at the next shell, ssh login or git command): the rest of the same kind -- `.zshenv`, `.zprofile`, `.zlogin`, `.zlogout`, `.bash_login`, `.bash_logout` (upstream's own shell snapshot names the zsh ones as zsh's user files, `src/core/terminal/shell_snapshot.zig:95`), all of `.config/fish` (`conf.d` and `functions` load too), all of `.ssh` (`rc`, `environment`, `authorized_keys2`, `config` includes), git's global `.gitconfig` and `.config/git` (a `filter.<driver>.clean` there is run by auto's own `git diff`), `.config/systemd` (Linux's launch agent), `.xfx` (the profile home, when a root is at or inside it), `.mcp.json` (a project's MCP server commands), and the startup files of the programs that run their rc on the next start: `.vimrc`, `.vim`, `.config/nvim`, `.emacs`, `.emacs.d`, `.config/emacs`, `.tmux.conf`, `.config/tmux` -- it follows links to them, comparing **identities, not spellings** (`src/permission/links.rs`): when auto is about to admit a write, every protected entry below the home directory, every root, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `$ZDOTDIR` -- and, when `HOME` is relative and so no profile home, below that `HOME` taken from where xfx was started, git's global configuration there included, since a command xfx runs is still handed it as it is (`LinkBase::from_environment`; kept on purpose, of the unsafe-to-copy kind: upstream has no link check to copy and its `homeDir` hands a relative `HOME` to nobody; no capture row shows it, so it is this sentence and `tests/permissions.rs`, not a ledger entry. **Its limit is one folder**: a command run elsewhere -- in a terminal `cwd` the model names -- finds that `HOME` under its own folder, and a link planted there is not looked at: its shell reads `<folder>/<HOME>/.zshrc`, and a git it runs reads `<folder>/<HOME>/.gitconfig`, which the configuration walk does not seed (it asks git with this base's absolute `HOME` and seeds `.gitconfig` from it alone); a write is checked before any command picks its folder, and a base per root would miss their subdirectories while reading as complete, so it is not added. It needs a link already under such a folder's relative home, which only the person can plant: auto refuses a write to `.zshrc` and the rest by name) -- is opened through its links and recorded by device and inode, every protected directory is listed for the links and directories inside it (a linked directory in its turn), together with every file the user's git reads as configuration (`src/permission/gitconfig.rs`): git's own answer -- `git config --list --show-origin --includes -z`, run in each root with xfx's own `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and the profile's home, every `file:` origin (the system file, an environment-selected global, the repository's `.git/config` and what they include; it runs nothing, measured with every hook, monitor, filter, pager and helper armed) -- joined with a static walk from those files, the default global ones and every git directory's `config` and `config.worktree`, which follows every `include.path` and `includeIf.*.path` whatever its condition, byte for byte as git parses it (whitespace is git's `isspace`, paths are bytes, an include path git versions read two ways asks), keyed by file and resolution directory (two hard links in two directories are two visits), bounded per seed -- 10 includes deep, git's own limit, and 64 files wide -- and 4,096 visits in all, so a superproject's hundred submodule configurations are not one configuration's bound; git missing or failing (a `safe.directory` refusal included: a repository owned by another user makes auto ask for every write in it), an origin xfx does not know, or a configuration it cannot read or parse asks; and the write asks when its target or any directory above it is one of them -- so `~/dotfiles/zshenv` is `~/.zshenv` when `~/.zshenv` links there, `repo/rc` is `.ssh` when `~/.ssh/rc` does, and a case variant, an NFD spelling or the `/System/Volumes/Data` path of the same directory is the same directory. A protected path not there yet (`~/.config` linked to `repo/config` with no `fish/` in it, or a dangling link) is its nearest existing directory by identity plus the missing ASCII names, folded; a missing name that is not ASCII, an entry xfx cannot inspect, a loop, or more than 65,536 entries listed in one check makes auto ask rather than clear the write. Also refused past upstream's list: `.aws/config` (`credential_process`), `.kube/config` (an `exec` plugin), `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` (hooks); not `.cargo/config.toml`, which is project content like `build.rs`. Upstream matches the written path's own components only). Upstream's three `.git` runs (`.git/hooks`, `.git/config`, `.git/config.worktree`) are one `.git` component here, at any depth, file or directory -- a run must be consecutive, so they miss `.git/modules/<name>/config`, `.git/modules/<name>/hooks/*`, `.git/info/attributes` and a submodule's `.git` file -- and a write inside any git directory found by its contents (a `HEAD` file with `objects` or `commondir`: a bare repository, the target of a `gitdir:` file, a root inside `.git`) asks -- and so does the write that would complete one (a `HEAD` beside `objects` or `commondir`, or either beside a `HEAD`), since each piece alone is an ordinary write and a directory assembled from them is a repository whose `config` the user's git reads. The bar applied: a precondition auto can create is a blocker; one only the user can author (a hard-linked configuration, an include path only git reads one way, a pre-existing attribute naming the user's own driver) fails closed because failing closed is cheap, not because auto can reach it, and it compares every name as a case-insensitive volume does (`fold_case`): ASCII case and the thirteen non-ASCII characters full Unicode folding sends onto ASCII, so `.zſhrc`, `.proﬁle` and `.ßh/authorized_keys` -- each of which a case-insensitive APFS volume opens as the ASCII name, and each of which upstream's case-exact comparison (`pathContainsComponentSequence`, `:695-716`) and an ASCII-only one let by -- are refused too. The lookup is `getenv`, and macOS's answers `KEY=…` with `KEY`'s value. Where xfx's expansion still differs from upstream's: it expands every string of an approved entry before parsing it -- the URL, `header_env` and `bearer_token_env` included -- where upstream expands only command, arguments, environment and header values after parsing (`:647-674`); a variable set to the empty string takes the default, where upstream's takes it only when unset (`:613`); and there is no 1 MiB cap on what expansion produces (`:550-551`). A repository's server may not carry an `Authorization` header and is never `required`. **An entry xfx can't read is a note in fx v0.0.16's words** (`renderWorkspaceDiagnostic`, `project_config.zig:161-215@481104fd`): `.mcp.json server '<name>' was skipped: <why>.`; a name outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, which no xfx tool name can carry, is `its name has a character xfx won't print` when a terminal can't show it (fx's `skipUnprintableNames`, `src/builtins/mcp.zig:99-116@481104fd`) and `xfx can't read it` otherwise (fx skips only the first kind) -- either before the entry is read and in the workspace's place among the notes, where fx reads the entry first, so one it can't read is that entry's note instead, and gives the name note last, after the profile's expansion notes; a whole file refused is fx's own spelling, `.mcp.json server 'unknown' was skipped: invalid_json.` (a key twice included), `root_must_be_object`, `servers_must_be_object`, or `xfx can't read it` for one it can't open or over 1 MiB (`parseWorkspaceJsonInternal`, `:543-559`; `invalidResult`, `workspace_config.zig:57-62@481104fd`). **`--project` writes** (`update` with `File::Project`, from `/mcp add\|remove --project`): the workspace's `.mcp.json` edited in place as the profile is, `mcpServers` only (an `mcp` key there is left alone), keeping the file's mode -- a new one at the default mode less the umask -- staged beside it with `create_new` and renamed, so nothing partial is left; never through a symbolic link or a file with another hard link, refused before it is read, nor one with no write permission (`config_file.zig:157-171@481104fd`, `io.zig:593-615@481104fd`). **Stricter than upstream**: a repository's server never gets xfx's own credentials. In its entry `${AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY}` and `${VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN}` expand as if unset -- a default stands in, and without one the server does not start -- and a `header_env` or `bearer_token_env` that names either keeps it from starting too, before the person is asked to approve it. The menu's sentence then says the variable is withheld on purpose: `.mcp.json server '<name>' field <field> uses '<VAR>', which xfx withholds from project servers; a server in ~/.xfx/mcp.json may use it.` An `http` server runs none of the repository's code, so upstream's rule -- every variable resolves from the whole environment (`workspace_config.zig:42-51`, `server_auth.zig:78-90`; v0.0.16 the same, `workspace_config.zig:42-51`, `mcp_host/config.zig:231-239`) -- would let one approval click send the gateway key to the host the file names; unsafe to copy. A profile server, which the person wrote, may still name them. |
| MCP client runtime | persistence | implemented | Stdio servers -- the configured program with six variables of xfx's environment, `HOME`, `LOGNAME`, `PATH`, `SHELL`, `TERM` and `USER` (one whose value is an exported shell function, `() {...`, dropped), then the server's own `env` over them, and nothing else inherited -- defence in depth, not a boundary: a server runs as the person, and a process of the same user can read xfx's launch environment (`ps -E` shows it on macOS) -- in a process group of its own, stopped by that group (end of input, `SIGTERM`, `SIGKILL`) when the session ends or the server leaves the configuration; a direct `docker run` gets upstream's `--cidfile` and its container is removed with it (`docker_run.zig:94-133`) by a `docker rm -f` given that same environment, so the server's own `DOCKER_HOST` or `DOCKER_CONTEXT` reaches the same daemon (`docker_run.zig:35-44`, `:71`, `server_transport.zig:933-936`). **The environment is fx >= 0.0.15's and deliberately not the pin's**: c1db9191 hands a server fx's whole environment, its Gateway credential included (`server_transport.zig:115-124`, `:943`); v0.0.15 and v0.0.16 give these six and the config's `env` (`inherited_names`, `src/core/mcp_host/config.zig:200-225@481104fd`), and no child inheriting xfx's credential is the rule the terminal tool already keeps. A server that relied on an inherited variable -- a proxy (`HTTPS_PROXY`), `TMPDIR`, the locale (`LANG`, `LC_*`), on Linux `DISPLAY`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, `NODE_*`, a token such as `GITHUB_TOKEN` -- must now name it in its `env`; a credential named there is a grant and is passed. Every other child xfx starts (`git ls-files`, a clipboard tool, `osascript`) keeps the inherited environment without `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` and `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` -- and streamable HTTP without OAuth (JSON or event-stream answers, the server's session id sent back, no redirect followed, a 401 shown as `Needs authentication`; the server's `headers`, then each `header_env` header and an `Authorization: Bearer` from `bearer_token_env` with the values of the variables they name, read at connect -- an unset one fails it as `McpHeaderEnvironmentMissing` / `McpBearerEnvironmentMissing`, `server_auth.zig:64-110`; no proxy from the environment unless the URL is https, so over loopback http neither those values nor the request reach an `HTTP_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY` -- upstream loads no proxy at all, `initDefaultProxies` being called nowhere under `src/` at `c1db9191` or `481104fd`). Upstream's legacy `initialize` as the default (`2025-11-25` asked, the four legacy versions accepted), `notifications/initialized`, paged `tools/list`, `startup_timeout_ms` per try, every server on a thread of its own. **fx v0.0.16's tries** (`src/mcp/core/engine.zig:24-28`, `:193-216`, `src/mcp/core/request.zig:76-79@481104fd`): a server that fails to come up is `retrying` and is tried again after 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30 and 30 s, and `failed` after its eighth failure in a row -- about 91 s, which fx's `p5-mcp-failed` capture pins between 84 and 100 s (`bad failed · /mcp` on both); a sign-in it needs fails it at once. Why it is down is fx's words (`writeFailure`, `describeFailure`, `src/core/mcp_host/runtime.zig:2276-2307@481104fd`) with `xfx` for `fx`: `it refused or never answered xfx's initialize request`, and for a stdio server how its process ended and its last three stderr lines, each cut to 240 bytes, joined with ` / ` and cut from the front to 240 (`process.zig:188-190`, `:719-756`) -- `its process exited with code 3; its last stderr lines: "boom-stderr"`, byte for byte fx's `fail-server-1`; each line reaches the screen through `terminal_safe`, which spells a control byte or a bidirectional override where fx writes `?`. **The same words reach the model**, and through it the provider: `capability_search` on a failed server answers `MCP server '<name>' is unavailable: <words>` (JSON, its quotes as entities), and a call to a server that stopped and could not start again carries them too -- so a configured stdio server's stderr tail, spelled and at most 240 bytes, is now sent where before only an error name was (`McpInitFailed`). fx sends the same text (`unavailableText`, `src/core/mcp_host/model.zig:705-711@481104fd`, fed by `lastError` -> `failureText`, `runtime.zig:1281`, `:2020-2036`); a server that prints a secret to its stderr sends it to the model in both. A reload takes the change **in place** (`Runtime.reload`, `runtime.zig:631-641`; `src/mcp/engine.zig:167-186@481104fd`): a server whose configuration is the same keeps running whatever its state -- a failed one stays failed, a reload after `add`, `remove` or a trust choice is not a retry -- a changed or removed one is stopped before the reload goes on, a try still in its handshake or in the teardown of a failed one included -- a try holds the server's process from its start until it publishes or closes it, one try at a time (two calls that find a server gone spend one restart, and the second waits for the first's try only until its own turn is cancelled); a stop cancels the try's handshake -- so an HTTP request in flight, or a stdio server whose output a process outside its group holds, does not keep it -- closes what it can, and waits for the rest at most 20 s after those closes (a group's grace and a container's removal); a reload stops what it drops one after another, each marked stopped before any is closed, and the end of a session waits 20 s for a reload under way and then stops, one after another, both what is in force and what that reload had not yet stopped (its process group ended and collected; fx holds a same-name start while the dropped one is stopping and waits 3.5 s for every server at its end, `Engine.stopping`, `Engine.deinit`, `src/mcp/engine.zig:145-161`, `:194-199@481104fd`), so a replacement never runs beside it and `/quit` -- which also waits out a reload under way, and lets none start a server after it -- leaves none behind; the new set is in force at once, and the reload says nothing. Nothing reloads to start a failed server again: c1db9191's menu `r`, which did, went with that menu, and `/mcp reload` says `/mcp reload is gone: xfx reloads after add, remove, approve, and reject.` (`src/core/mcp_host/verbs.zig:70@481104fd`) and starts nothing, as fx v0.0.16 has neither; a resource or prompt read waits through `retrying` as through `connecting`; a config that cannot be read again is one warning, `The MCP config changed, but xfx couldn't load it again. Your current servers are still active.` (`app_commands.zig:340-345@481104fd`). The launch's `MCP startup: …` notice is gone with it: the status line says what needs attention (`notifications and status line` row). **Differs from fx v0.0.16**: an HTTP try against a port that refuses ends at once in xfx, so such a server is `failed` about 91 s after launch, where fx's tries there last 10-20 s each and it was `failed` between 110 and 270 s (`/mcp list` every 4 s, then at 270 s); a ready server whose connection drops is `failed` at once and started again only by a call, within `restart_limit`, where fx's goes back through `retrying` (its idle reconnect, `a97f9d9e`, not measured); a start that fails to spawn is `McpSpawnFailed`, where fx says `its command was not found` or `its command could not be run (permission denied)`; and a stdout line that is not MCP is dropped without fx's `it wrote "…" instead of MCP`. Requests a server sends are answered (`ping`) or refused (`Method not found`). **Narrower than v0.0.16's, pending a user decision**: no `server/discover` probe and no `2026-07-28` protocol. c1db9191 sent the probe only under an e2e variable; v0.0.16 sends it to every server before the legacy `initialize` (`src/mcp/core/era.zig:18-25`, `:98-107`, `:476-480@481104fd`), and with it its engine's framing -- ids from 1, a `_meta.progressToken` equal to the id on every request, `initialize` and `tools/list` too (`meta`, `era.zig:459-474`), and `"params":{}` on `notifications/initialized` (`writeInitialized`, `:493-496`) -- and it no longer accepts `2024-11-05` ("fx speaks 2025-03-26 and newer", `src/core/mcp_host/runtime.zig:2296`; `legacy`, `era.zig:24`), which xfx's stdio still does. `p5-mcp-wire-new`'s frames differ in exactly those four places (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/mcp-wire/`). **Open question for the user** (not decided by the 0.0.16 MCP-W unit): send `server/discover` with `2026-07-28` before `initialize`, with that framing, and drop `2024-11-05`? Until then xfx keeps the legacy handshake as it is. No elicitation, sampling or roots, which xfx therefore does not advertise in `initialize`; the `sse` transport is parsed and listed and not connected (`McpTransportUnsupported`); `notifications/tools/list_changed` is not followed until the next reload; HTTP OAuth is gated (`/mcp`'s row). **Stdio writes** hold no lock while they wait and are bounded: a call's own timeout covers its write, a notification or an answer to the server's own request waits at most 1 s, a cancellation 100 ms (`stdio_dispatcher.zig:18-19`); a call already cancelled or past its deadline puts nothing on the wire (fx looks at the cancellation before the first byte, `:549-551`, and races the deadline); a write stopped once under way -- part of it sent, or any of it refused by a full pipe even with nothing sent, fx's `.writing` (`:668`) -- or failed by the server, ends the connection and kills the server's group (`:585-594`, `:743-751`); a refused frame with nothing sent could keep the connection, since the stream would still be whole, and does not on purpose: a server that stops reading would then hold xfx's reader up to 1 s for each request it sends, and every call behind it to its own deadline -- an availability cost, with no safety difference either way, and every group signal goes out before the leader is collected (fx v0.0.16, `src/mcp/io/process.zig:9-11`, `:557-559`). **Differs from c1db9191** in three places: `notifications/initialized` is bounded by 1 s, not by the startup timeout (`server_transport.zig:900-906`; it is 54 bytes, sent just after the server answered `initialize`); a close during a write lets the pipe go and stops the server with end of input, `SIGTERM`, the grace and `SIGKILL`, where fx kills the child at once (`closeStdin`, `stdio_dispatcher.zig:1702-1717`); and a write refused because xfx already closed the input only reports the connection closed, while one the server fails ends it and kills the group as fx does. xfx also puts `SIGCHLD` back to its default at startup, which fx does at neither pin, so a launcher's ignore cannot have the kernel collect a leader before its group is signalled. |
| saved additional workspace roots | persistence | implemented | `workspaces["<workspace>"].additional_directories` in `~/.xfx/settings.json`: a list of at most 16 unique absolute paths (`settings_store.zig:1996-2009`; a list out of that shape is a `config` diagnostic and adds nothing), each the **real path** the directory resolved to when `/workspace add` saved it, written under `settings.lock` with every other key kept; the last one removed takes the key, then an empty workspace entry, then an empty `workspaces`, out of the file (`removeWorkspaceIfEmpty`, `settings_store.zig:1498-1505`). Profile-only: a project file's `workspaces` is ignored with a diagnostic. Every surface uses them -- the TUI, the line shell, `ask` -- and a saved directory is a root of a turn **only while it is still the directory it was**: one that is gone, or that has become a symbolic link to somewhere else, is listed `Unavailable` and is not in the scope, rather than the somewhere else being in it (`resolveObservedDirectory`, `workspace_access.zig:615-633`) -- within a session and at the next launch alike, which at launch is narrower than upstream's (`resolveSavedDirectory` follows a saved spelling again, `workspace_access.zig:534-561`), so a hand-written spelling that is not a real path (`/tmp/x` on macOS) is listed `Unavailable` here until `/workspace add` names the directory, which rewrites the entry to its real path as upstream rewrites a matched entry (`settings_store.zig:1352-1357`). Not matched: upstream's launch-only roots and `--no-additional-dirs` (its interactive command line has `--add-dir`; xfx's shells take none), so every entry is `saved=true command_line=false` and `saved_suppressed` is always false. `workspace_access.zig`, `settings_store.zig:1308-1450`. |
| prompt history | persistence | implemented | The band owns its line editor, so it owns a recall. Every non-blank line the composer submits is remembered -- a prompt once the runtime has taken it, as upstream records one only after its admission (`input_submit_runtime.zig:872-886`), so a prompt refused for a full queue stays in the composer and nowhere else; commands beside prompts, because a command is a line the user typed and running one again is the commonest reason to reach for a recall -- newest first, capped at a hundred entries, with adjacent repeats folded into one. `C-p` and `C-n` walk the list from wherever the caret is; Up and Down are the composer's own row movement first, and at the draft's first or last visual row they take the caret to the draft's start or end before they become the walk (`input_completion_runtime.zig:265-284`) -- so a multi-row draft stays navigable by arrow, and a recalled line, whose caret is at its end, takes two Ups to step past: one to its start, one to the line before it. Entering the walk **captures the half-typed line** and stepping forward past the newest entry hands it back, so a recall never costs what was being written; neither end wraps; an edit ends the walk and the edited text is what the next one comes back to, while a caret move does not, because reading a recalled prompt before stepping further back is what an arrow is for there. `composer_history.zig:445-540`. **It is kept on disk as upstream's is** (`prompt_history_store.zig`): every recorded line is appended to `~/.xfx/history.jsonl` (`0600` in a `0700` directory) as `{"schema_version":1,"timestamp_ms":…,"workspace_root":"<abs>","text":"…"}` under an exclusive `history.lock` (two seconds, then a failure), with a collapsed paste written as the text it stands for and a prompt carrying an image not written at all (`!has_images_for_submit`, `input_submit_runtime.zig:884`) -- it is still recalled in the session that sent it, its image back under a fresh number; a record past 256 KiB is not written, the workspace's latest line is not written twice, a torn last line is cut before the next append, and past 1 MiB the file is compacted to its newest 1000 records and at most 1 MiB through a staged rename. A launch recalls the newest hundred lines of its own workspace. The append runs on a writer thread of its own, in submission order, so the band never waits on the lock or the `fsync`; a save that fails is said once, `! history: failed to save input history (<reason>); submission continued`, when the thread reports it, until one works again. `/settings`' `Prompt history` off keeps a submitted line nowhere -- not in the file and not in the session's recall -- and a launch with it off recalls nothing. A record upstream would write as base64 (text that is not UTF-8) is never written by xfx and is skipped when read. One boundary, deliberate and not a qualification of the row's status: the **line-oriented shell** still has no recall of its own: it reads lines through the terminal's canonical mode, which gives backspace, word erase and line kill and nothing else, and adding one there means owning the terminal state that shell guarantees it never changes. A recalled collapsed paste comes back as the **block** it stood for rather than as its summary's words, under a paste number this session has not used -- upstream's renumbering across a recall, recorded in full on the `full-screen TUI` row and driven by scenario 21 of [`.prd/06-qa-harness.md`](../.prd/06-qa-harness.md). |
| generated session titles | persistence | partial | The TUI asks a model for a fresh session's title at the keystroke that submits its first prompt, on a thread of its own beside the turn and never in its way, as upstream's `snapshotPrompt` does for every prompt it hands its worker (`src/main.zig:1479-1481`): upstream's request -- its instructions as the system message, the prompt trimmed and cut to 2048 bytes as the user's, no tools, `toolChoice` `none`, `maxOutputTokens` 128 -- to the Gateway's fixed `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` (`builtins/gateway.zig:46`), one attempt, 15 seconds at most (`session_title_generation.zig:16-25`, `:142-176`). The answer's first line, trimmed of blanks and quotes, cut to 60 bytes and cleaned of control characters, is the title (`sanitizeGeneratedTitle`, `:67-87`); nothing usable is no title, and the one the first prompt gives the session stays.
