Up and Down are the composer's own movement first, take the caret to the draft's start or end at its first or last visual row, and become the walk only from there (`input_completion_runtime.zig:265-284`), so a multi-row draft is still navigable by arrow, and `C-n` steps forward from wherever the caret is (upstream's `history_next`, `shortcuts.zig:17`). `C-p` is not the backward walk: it is upstream's model catalog (`open_model_catalog`, `escape_parser.zig:271`), the menu `/model` opens, with the draft lent out of the composer -- through the effort column a choice in it goes on into -- and given back as it was, text, blocks and caret, when the flow ends: a model applied, Escape, a second `C-p`, `C-c` or `C-d` (`app_input_runtime.zig:2356-2404`, `:1666-1676`, `:2775-2779`); over the `/help` catalog or the `/statusline` picker it does nothing (`:2406-2418`). A recalled line that begins with `/` opens no completion menu until it is edited, so a further Up keeps walking (`picker_state.zig:451-466`). Entering the walk **captures the half-typed line**, and stepping forward past the newest entry hands it back, so a recall is not a gesture that destroys what was being written. Neither end wraps: past the oldest line is nothing, and the near end is the captured draft. An edit ends the walk and the edited text is what the next one comes back to; a caret move does not, because reading a recalled prompt before stepping further back is exactly what an arrow key is for there. Nothing is written to disk and nothing survives the process: a session recall rather than a history file, which is the boundary the `prompt history` row records. An entry carries the collapsed pastes its summaries named, so a recalled line comes back as the **blocks** rather than as words -- under fresh paste numbers, which is upstream's renumbering and is what keeps the recalled line and the draft it replaced from both answering to `#1`. The draft a walk stands aside carries its blocks the same way, so the half-typed line the walk comes back to is the whole of it. This phase adds no command, no tool and no canonical slash name -- it adds one alias, `/exit`, on the `/quit` row. **One thing is named here because it is absent from every stable release.** A stateless per-vector output check -- every vector the TUI is about to write decoded into a model of the terminal and compared, **before** `write_all` rather than after it, against an intent its emitter declares separately from the bytes: the whole model as a value (plane, cursor visibility, the shown title and the title stack, each mode as a tri-state that distinguishes *nothing has said* from a guessed default, whether scrollback was erased, the queries asked, the saved cursor), the cells against a grid built from the emitter's own inputs with colour compared through a parser of its own on each side, and a document append replayed in emission order so a row that scrolls off the top is compared **before** the scroll evicts it -- is **absent from stable releases**; preview-channel builds run it unconditionally, with no opt-in flag (`.prd/03-tui-port.md` item 21). **The transport those vectors go out on is absent from stable releases in the same way**: preview-channel builds write every byte of a session through one unbuffered counted sink -- no buffer and no flush, so there is no vector a session believes it wrote that is still held in one -- which adds up what the kernel accepted and tells a refusal and a write that took *nothing* (the screen is as it was; the vector is still owed, and the half-second budget above is what ends a run of them) apart from a write the screen took **part** of (it is not, so the session ends on it at once, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than offering the same vector again and writing that prefix twice). That is **containment**: an accepted count is a kernel receipt rather than a terminal's acknowledgement, and nothing here claims a parser recovered. Partial-write recovery covers the two repaints whose whole intended content is in hand -- the primary band's frame, and a repaint of the alternate plane a question already owns -- each with one fixed cleanup and one whole rebuild of the same repaint in the same call, never a replay of its bytes, and no approval readiness until a later write the session verifies on its own; every other `Partial` -- a plane transition, a document append or carry, a reset's scrollback handoff, the theme query, the retint -- is contained and fatal -- the session ends and leaves its give-up diagnostic -- and its bytes are never replayed (the table under this one gives each path and its reason), though a `Rejected`/`ZeroProgress` write -- an append or a refused handoff alike -- is retained and reoffered under the existing budget rather than dropped. A released binary has none of it (`.prd/03-tui-port.md` item 21; [`.prd/06-qa-harness.md`](../.prd/06-qa-harness.md) rows 3c, 3d and 3e drive the containment and both recoveries on a real pseudoterminal with the fault build). Without it -- and for any other value of it, for every subcommand, and whenever either end is not a terminal -- xfx is the line-oriented shell of the `interactive` row and changes no terminal state. |
| full transcript (`C-o`) | ui | partial | Upstream's Ctrl-O screen (`src/ui/full_transcript_screen.zig`, keys `src/core/app/input_full_transcript_runtime.zig:147-198`), on the alternate buffer through the same take-and-give-back the large-change review uses, so the document is exactly as it was when it closes. Every entry the document was given -- a prompt's card, the answer as the markdown presenter completed it (so a notice that lands while it holds a line is an entry of its own before that line, which follows whole, `store.zig:1343-1390`), a notice, a tool call's row in its group (the group's header and every call's row **word-wrapped under their tree rail, never cut**: a continuation is `│ ` under a `├` row and two blanks under the header and the last `└` row, painted in the default colour before the row's own attributes and colour are reopened from the tracked state, a wide character too wide for a one-column row a `?`; fx v0.0.16 `materializeExpandedOverride`/`wrapFullToolStatus`, `tool_group_projection.zig:1061-1100@481104fd`, `transcript_blocks.zig:904-975@481104fd`. A character's cells are the screen's own measure (`wrap::width`, which the frame clips by), not fx's `display_width`: the two disagree on some characters -- U+17D8 is one cell to fx's table and three to the screen's, a lone skin-tone modifier none and two -- and a row fx's measure fits could be cut by the frame), the turn's summary -- under a dim `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS.mmm <zone> · <Kind>` header read in the local time zone, `TZ` or the system's, at each moment's own offset so daylight saving time follows the date (fx v0.0.16's c9bc4876: `formatHeader`, `full_transcript_metadata.zig:28-38@481104fd`, and `local_time.zig:62-159@481104fd`, ported as `src/local_time.rs`): the zone's abbreviation only when it is 1 to 10 of `A-Z a-z 0-9 + -`, otherwise its offset as `+HHMM` (`isPlainZoneName`/`writeZoneName`, `:102-124`), so no `TZ` or zone file can put an escape, a C1 control or a non-ASCII byte on the screen; UTC for an offset of a day or more or one that would carry the moment out of 1970-9999 (`:70-100`); no header for a moment of zero or less or past 9999. xfx calls `tzset` once before its first `localtime_r` (POSIX asks it of `localtime` only), where upstream calls none -- no change on macOS, whose `localtime_r` reads `TZ` without it, and under each call what the inline transcript never shows: its arguments (one the call's row already shows is left out only when it is in the row's first line **and** the row fits the screen, `argumentVisibleInToolHeading`, `full_transcript_screen.zig:6594-6619@481104fd`), a command's output and non-zero status, a file's lines, and the diff of every change it made, reviewed or not (taken from the plan's preimage by the tool, handed to the screen alone and never logged). A change's lines are written **as fx v0.0.16 writes them, byte for byte** (9db199e4, `appendTerminalSafeLine`, `text_utils.zig:419-442@481104fd`, called at `diff.zig:1273@481104fd`; T-U6b): each is read back from the permission boundary's spelling (`bounded_diff_side`) and spelled again in fx's forms -- a C0 control or `DEL` as `\\x1b`, a C1 control and each mark, separator, embedding, override, isolate and byte-order mark fx names (`isNonPrintingCodepoint`, `:703-708`) as `\\u{009b}` (four lower-case hex digits at least), a line's trailing carriage return dropped, a tab kept (its cells written blank and unstyled, as the terminal leaves the cells fx's tab moves over), a backslash as itself -- so no byte of the file a terminal acts on reaches it; until T-U6 a bidirectional override, a zero-width space or a byte-order mark crossed as itself on this screen. **One addition**: the Arabic letter mark (`U+061C`), which fx writes as itself, is named `\\u{061c}` too, since it reorders the text around it as the marks fx names do (`ctrl-o-arabic-letter-mark`, `scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml`). **The review keeps xfx's own, injective spelling** (the full-screen TUI row; `review-diff-spelling`): fx's lossy form cannot be copied safely on a decision screen, where a CR-only or trailing-CR change would read identical to the person approving it. fx c1db919 wrote a change's lines raw and exited with `AnsiBandOverflow` on an `ESC[2J` (`p5-transcript-diffctl`, `-diffodd`, `-diffask`; `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/transcript-receipts/`). Those come from the session's journal as it is written, never by reading the log back. Every one of them is the model's, a file's or a command's, and none reaches the terminal as it is: an argument's and a result's text is spelled as fx v0.0.16's `TerminalSafeIndentedWriter` spells it (`full_transcript_screen.zig:5147-5194@481104fd`) -- a line break kept, a tab four spaces, every other control `\xNN` (a carriage return too), the C1 controls, zero-width and directional marks, separators and byte-order mark `\u{NNNN}` -- and a command's output is first read as fx's decoder reads it (`command_output_content.zig:52-282@481104fd`: an escape sequence dropped, a bare carriage return the line begun again, CRLF one line end, a tab kept and drawn as the spaces to the screen's eight-column stop where the wrap met it, which a word moved down keeps, as fx's wrap writes them, `assistant_wrap.zig:163-174,729-775@481104fd`, C1 `\u{00NN}`; xfx decodes characters where fx decodes bytes, so a byte that is not UTF-8 is U+FFFD, not `\xNN`, and a sequence too long to keep is kept to the last whole character before its 64th byte), then spelled the same; where fx leaves those marks as they are in a command's output, and the Arabic letter mark as it is on every row, xfx names them too (`scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml` `ctrl-o-command-marks`, unsafe to copy; `p5-transcript-safe`). A spelling is not kept whole: the result's bound or a row's end can cut `\u{202e}` into pieces, each of them printable ASCII. The call's own row above them -- the transcript's tool row and this screen's heading -- is spelled by fx's rule for that row (`src/tui/toolgroup.rs` `spelled`): a command is put on one line as fx puts it (`projectRunCommandActivitySource`, `src/core/tooling/tool_presentation.zig:256-311@481104fd`: a carriage return or a line feed one space, the spaces and tabs after it gone) and then written by `encodeTerminalSafe` (`src/core/shared/text_utils.zig:353-390@481104fd`: every control `\xNN`, a tab `\x09`, the C1 controls and the marks `\u{NNNN}`), bounded on what is written at the 14 399 bytes fx stores a command's row at (`formatRunCommandDetailBounded`, `tool_presentation.zig:432-447`) and cut back to a whole token with `...` -- and ended `...` too where fx stopped reading the command at its 14 400th byte with more left to show and says nothing of it, which is not safe to copy (ledger `call-row-source-cut`, `p5-call-row-long`); any other argument is written by `encodeTerminalSafe` as it is, at a file change's 4 096 bytes (`fileMutationDisplayPath`, `src/core/agent/runtime/tool_presentation.zig:736-753@481104fd`), and a failure's reason by `encodeTerminalSafeInline` at 256 bytes -- every reason, where fx encodes only its preflight and MCP reasons and writes a tool's own `status_detail` as it is (`failureStatusDetail`, `:1445-1493`, `:1457-1458`). The argument crosses the UI channel spelled the same way (`src/tui/bridge.rs` `spelled_shown`), live and resumed, never blanked. Where fx writes a read's, a list's or a pattern's row with the argument as it came -- the marks and the C1 controls to the terminal as themselves, the other controls dropped, the row ended at the first line break (`formatToolActionValue`, `src/core/app/app_agent_runtime.zig:1248-1310@481104fd`; `normalizeCanonicalStatus`, `src/ui/transcript/tool_group_projection.zig:317-347@481104fd`) -- and the Arabic letter mark as itself on a command's row, xfx spells them as it spells a command's (ledger `call-row-raw-argument`, unsafe to copy; `p5-call-row-safe`). An argument's own row is left out only when the heading shows its value as the model wrote it: fx looks for the raw value in the status phrase it spelled (`argumentVisibleInToolHeading`, `full_transcript_screen.zig:6594-6619@481104fd`), so a value holding anything the heading spells, a control or a mark, keeps its row (`src/tui/full_transcript.rs` `Argument::text`); and that phrase is the status entry's own, whose command is its first 120 bytes spelled (`formatRunCommandActivity`, `src/core/tooling/tool_presentation.zig:393-416@481104fd`), so a command past them keeps its row even where the heading shows it whole -- judged on the call as the model wrote it, before anything is spelled, since a bound on the spelled text could fall inside an escape fx's never splits, and with no `...` for a command read only in part, which fx's status has not either though the row has (`src/tui/toolgroup.rs` `status`, read in `full_transcript.rs` `arguments`). Whether the heading fits the screen is measured on the heading, which for a command past 120 bytes is wider than fx's status, so a short value inside a long command can keep a row fx would leave out -- the safe direction. Not ported: a command's leading `cd . &&` and its workspace root written `.` (`stripNoopCurrentDirectoryPrefix`, `workspaceRootMatchesAt`, `tool_presentation.zig:313-329@481104fd`), which the row has never had. Above every entry, under the welcome, upstream's full-detail records: `* session:` for a fresh launch (provider, model, `effort: auto`, the advertised tools, `mcp: none`; `app_bootstrap_runtime.zig:61-111`) and `* network:` for each provider request a turn settles (latency, finish reason and reported tokens, or a refusing status as a warning; `orchestrator.zig:4194-4280`) and `! recovery:` for each request the turn retries, again as the next attempt starts, and `✓ recovery: ✓ recovered · succeeded on attempt n` when one gets through (the retry row's label, then `kind:`, the cause, `attempt: n/3` -- xfx's budget, not upstream's ten -- a whole-second `retry delay:` and the diagnostic; `app_worker_runtime.zig:92-133`, `orchestrator.zig:5012-5048`); none in the document. A resumed session's turns are recorded as they replay, with their calls' arguments and results read off the log and the log's times on their headers. Footer `┃ full detail · ctrl+o close · pgup/pgdn scroll · esc close` over the hint row, no caret (`paint_plan.zig:1047`); opens at the tail and follows it; scrolled up, it keeps the entry it shows and how far into it while the page rebuilds above or below it, a width change being one more rebuild, and a position among the full-detail records keeps its row offset through a rebuild (fx v0.0.16 `pin_reading_position`/`select_visual_offset`, `transcript_presentation.zig:144-275@481104fd`, through `presentationForRebuiltPage`, `transcript/runtime.zig:11584-11590@481104fd`, 026a8f4e and b1a53b01; fx's `prepare_projection`, which would queue the last bookmark on a width change instead, is reached only from `runtime.zig`'s tests, and fx's screen keeps a reader on its `* session:` record across a width change; upstream's anchor, the entry an approval opens the screen at, has no counterpart, since this screen always opens at the tail); PgUp/PgDn page, Up/Down move three rows, Esc, Ctrl-C and Ctrl-O close, and every other key -- Home and End included -- goes to the composer behind it (`app_input_runtime.zig:973-994`); refused while a question is up, and a question that arrives closes it (a large change takes the plane over). **Narrower than upstream's**: no `system prompt:` or `skills:` line in `* session:` (xfx has no system prompt of its own, and its skill catalog is read as each turn starts rather than at launch), no diff for a change made before a resume (upstream persists one, `app_session_runtime.zig:4978`), and a result kept to its first 64 KiB on screen. A call's heading shows a command whole up to fx's own bound, as fx's wrapped heading does (`src/tui/toolgroup.rs` `COMMAND_TARGET_BYTES`; fx's test `full projection shows a terminal session launch command in full`, `full_transcript_screen.zig:997-1044@481104fd`, has no counterpart: xfx's terminal has no sessions). Every row the record was given already laid out (`Body::Block`) keeps the rows it was written as at the width of the moment, and one wider than this screen runs on to the next row, mid-word, in its colours, as fx's row walker lets a static row run on (`ProjectionRowWalker.appendWithSoftWrapPrefix`, `full_transcript_screen.zig:3968-4039@481104fd`): a question's resolution and a cancelled question's row (`src/tui/shell.rs` `Mark::Block`, `p5-transcript-question` at 60 columns), a fork's `original session xfx --resume …` line (`Shell::forked`'s `self.full.block`), a replayed turn's cancelled line (`Ending::Cancelled`), and the same rows in a `/trace` report, which lays the record out through the same function at its own width. fx's raw tool-status replay for Ctrl-O (`formatFullToolStatus`, `transcript_blocks.zig:871-902@481104fd`, 4e4b599e) is for a raw `tool_status` entry; fx's question resolution is a `question_resolution` (or default) raw entry, not one, and every xfx call is drawn through its group. |
| `@` file picker | ui | partial | Upstream's `@` picker (`src/core/workspace/file_index.zig`, `src/core/input/file_picker_path.zig`, `src/core/app/input_completion_runtime.zig:779-850`) in the band rows a menu takes, its rows starting under the `@`, the marked row on a grey bar with the matched letters bold, and the hint row left as it is (upstream's hint switch has no file-picker arm, `paint_plan.zig:946-978`). An `@` that starts a word opens it, a quoted or backslash-escaped path included. **The index** is upstream's: inside a git work tree the files `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` lists, asked of a git from a fixed list rather than `PATH`, and every directory git does not name as ignored; outside one a walk that skips the always-ignored names; at most 100,000 entries, merged in byte order, none that could not be painted as it stands. It is built on a worker thread and published in its final order a run at a time, and the first index is searched as it grows -- rows fill in while it is published, and a query nothing published yet matches says `indexing files...` (`fillProgressive`, `file_index.zig:172-230`; `input_completion_runtime.zig:838-842`); a refresh stays private until it is whole and the index before it is searched meanwhile (`searchableGeneration`, `file_index.zig:643-650`). A query is upstream's case-blind subsequence ranked on its eight facts, at most 32 rows; a path-shaped one (`@src/`, `@~/`, `@../`) lists that directory on a worker instead. Up/Down and `C-k`/`C-j` move the bar, PgUp/PgDn page it by the composer's row cap, both wrapping; Tab and Enter put the path in (`@path ` for a file, `@dir/` and the listing goes on), Enter never submitting the same keystroke; Escape closes it for that word. **Narrower than upstream's** (each recorded in `.prd/tui-phase4/ssot.md` Tensions): the prompt sent is trimmed, so a chosen file's trailing space is not in the request; no on-disk index cache; Ctrl-V attaches no image (with `/image`). Receipt: `.prd/tui-phase4/receipts/e1/`. |
| notifications and status line | ui | partial | A status row, and the terminal bell `/sound` switches (its own row); none of upstream's chimes. The TUI's band ends in a hint row carrying, in order, a missing-credential call to action, `queued N`, the permission mode -- `ask`, or `auto` and `full access` lifted out of the row's grey -- and a compact model label, with a right-aligned `esc again to clear` while that gesture is armed, or the one-time full-access warning (`/permissions` row), or -- last, in the same dim grey, and not while a question has the row -- what MCP needs from the person, fx v0.0.16's (`statusLine`, `startupSettled`, `src/core/mcp_host/menu.zig:443-559`; `hostStatusLine`, `dismissHostStatus`, `src/core/app/app_mcp_runtime.zig:136-199@481104fd`): `proj is waiting for approval · /mcp`, `bad failed · /mcp`, `2 MCP need attention · /mcp`, else `MCP config has 1 problem · /mcp list`, else nothing; silent while servers started less than 10 s ago are still connecting or retrying, rebuilt at most every 250 ms, at most 120 bytes, a name spelled through `terminal_safe`; a prompt sent leaves what needed attention then to `/mcp` and names only what needs it afterwards. A retrying server is never named. fx's sign-in news (`Signing in to X · /mcp`, `Signed in to X`) and `needs a variable` are not built: MCP sign-in is gated, and an unset `${VAR}` is among xfx's config problems (`MCP project servers and trust` row); segments are dropped from the right rather than allowed to overflow, and among the two state segments the model is the last to go. Upstream's context meter (`Context: {used}k/{total}k {pct}%`) is populated from two facts and shown only when it has **both**: the numerator is a completed turn's own `input_tokens` and the denominator is the `max_context` the provider's catalog publishes for the model in force, matched by id or by alias. Either can legitimately be absent -- a provider need not report usage, and a window is known only once the catalog that publishes it has been read, which the TUI does in the background at launch -- and a missing half removes the segment rather than defaulting it, because a row reading `0k/0k 0%` would be reporting a measurement nobody took. So a session grows a meter after its first completed turn once its catalog has been read; a provider switch keeps the turn's tokens and reads the new provider's catalog at once. Upstream's opt-in statusline items -- session title, workspace identity, git branch -- are absent because xfx has no settings surface to turn them on with (they are off by default upstream too, `settings_catalog.zig:53-55`), its reasoning-effort and fast-mode markers name capabilities xfx does not model, and its desktop/terminal notifications are absent entirely. The line-oriented shell has no status row at all. `config_runtime.zig:139-152`, `render.zig:391-460`. |
| terminal program status (OSC 7501) | ui | implemented | fx v0.0.16's Program Status Protocol reports (`src/ui/terminal/program_status.zig@481104fd`), from the TUI only: `ESC ] 7501 ; state=<s> : app=xfx ESC \` written between frames, ahead of the tick's frame as upstream's loop reports while it collects facts and commits its frame afterwards (`src/main.zig:2983`, `src/ui/event_loop.zig:80`, `:116-117`), so the launch's `idle` precedes the first frame. `working` while a turn runs, a prompt waits for one or a manual `/compact` is in hand -- upstream's worker runs a prompt or a compaction and nothing else (`WorkItem`, `worker_runtime.zig:167-170`), so a provider switch, a `/resume` listing, a fork or a `/workspace`, which xfx's runtime also takes as work, report nothing -- and not once the user has stopped it; `blocked:kind=permission` while a permission question is up, its `msg` the request's label (`<tool> <argument>`, upstream's `formatPermissionLabel` shape -- upstream's command is `shell.run <command>`, xfx's `terminal <command>`; a rule change's confirmation under upstream's label, `Remember allow for this saved session`); `blocked:kind=question` with the model's question; `done` for a turn that answered, `error` for a turn that failed (upstream's `noteTurnFinished`, and `noteFailure` on the `request failed` a failed item ends in, `app_worker_runtime.zig:1214-1216`) -- one an automatic compaction inside it failed included, as upstream finishes that turn `.failed` (`orchestrator.zig:7864-7865`, `:5615-5618`) -- and `idle` for a turn the user stopped; work that ran no turn has no result. A manual `/compact` reports its result (`done`; `error` for every failure, an unconfirmed checkpoint included; `idle` for a cancel) and nothing when nothing ran (no conversation, or a turn in the way). An unchanged report is not written again, except a new result -- a second failure in a row is reported -- and once a result has been reported, the next work settles as `idle` rather than repeating it (`4ae9eeeb`). `msg` is the text folded to one line -- each run of whitespace and controls (C0, DEL, C1) one space, invalid UTF-8 dropped -- cut to 256 bytes ending `...` on a character boundary, then standard base64 (`:164-204`): no byte of a label or a question reaches the terminal as itself, bidi marks included, and the output check refuses a report in any other shape. All four restores -- the planned exit's and the abnormal one the signal handlers and the panic hook write, each with and without tmux -- include the clear of the record (`state=clear`) right after `?2004l`, as upstream's four do (`app_lifecycle.zig:43-48`); the abnormal two are compile-time constants written in one `write(2)`, and the build refuses a restore without the clear. Delivery is the terminal's: a restore the descriptor takes only part of, or a failed handler `write(2)`, can lose it, as it can lose the rest of the restore. A stop includes it in the stop handler's restore, and the resume after that stop reports again -- `idle` when the result was already reported (`invalidate`, `main.zig:1050-1055`); a continue no handler answered (a bare `SIGCONT`, an uncatchable `SIGSTOP` and its `CONT`) cleared nothing and rewrites nothing, as upstream invalidates only around its own suspend. No query, no setting and no variable: a terminal without the protocol ignores it (`program_status.zig:1-6`). `ask`, subcommands and the line-oriented shell write no report, as upstream's come from its interactive shell alone. A report the terminal did not take is dropped, as upstream's is (`program_status.zig:124-127`, `transcript/runtime.zig:10416-10429`): the next report is the next change. **Where xfx differs:** `app=xfx` -- the user's identity decision, "a separate identity" (`.prd/01-spec.md:56`), held by `scripts/check-xfx-identity.sh:5`; the launch writes its `idle` **before** the window title, where fx writes its titles first, because xfx's title is the lead of its first frame's own vector (`Band::retitle`, `src/tui/frame.rs`) and a title written apart from the frame is the second writer that module forbids -- neither moves a cell; xfx stops only on a `SIGTSTP` (the `full-screen TUI` row, owner unit **CTRL-Z**); a question's line feed or tab reaches the reporter already spelled (the next row, owner unit **QUESTION-BREAKS**); and a run of 5xx answers reads `error` where fx's reads `working` (the `transport-owned retry` row, owner unit **RETRY-BUDGET**). |
| a question's line breaks in a program status message | ui | partial | fx v0.0.16 hands its reporter the model's question as the model wrote it, and its fold makes a line feed or a tab one space (`program_status.zig:164-204@481104fd`): `msg` reads `…? now…`. xfx's `ask_user_question` spells every control before anything shows the question (`terminal_safe`, `src/tools/question.rs:57-73`), so the reporter is handed the text `\x0a\x09`, which it keeps: `…?\x0a\x09now…`. Inert either way -- base64 of one line with no control character. The L-U1+U2 receipt's checker compares the two as one space (its `question-breaks` normalization, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/osc-program-status/osc.py`); that normalization **expires with this row**. Owner unit: **QUESTION-BREAKS** (queued). |
| colored and hyperlinked TTY output | ui | partial | Colour and OSC 8 hyperlinks inside the TUI only. Under `XFX_TUI=1` the band picks a light or dark palette at start-up -- `XFX_THEME` (exactly `light` or `dark`), else an OSC 11 background query with a 200 ms deadline, else `COLORFGBG`, else dark -- and uses 256 colours unless `COLORTERM` claims truecolor, which Apple Terminal is not believed about. Three rows carry it: the divider, the hint row, and a refusal on that row; the composer's own rows carry none, as upstream's do not. In the preview channel, a fourth does: the activity row a running turn adds, in a neutral grey of its own (`252` dark, `238` light). Published releases do not follow a theme that changes while xfx runs. A mode 2031 monitor -- paired enable/restore; a SIGCONT arms an outbound `?996n` query delivered on a checked counted paint tick, and the `997;n` reply is decoded before focus and consumed on input; the `XFX_THEME` lock still honored, band colours following it -- is in the preview channel only. Transcript re-tint is split, likewise unreleased: role-tagged plain tail/queue rows materialize using the current palette at emission, while visible document rows with no scroll are repainted candidate-only, deferred only when the plane or state is unsuitable. Materialization occurs at emission, not on notification. The pacer's pending-buffer patch is not applied by design -- pending Body/Notice rows already materialize the current palette at emission -- and native offscreen scrollback is not repainted. An answer's inline code is upstream v0.0.16's brightest neutral, `255` dark and `235` light (`theme.zig:102,145@481104fd`, `5e32bce2`; `245` and `247` before it), which the recolour carries as the body grey both ways. A code block's rows are recorded as the block's, and recoloured as upstream draws the block again in the new palette (`transcript_blocks.zig:2023-2029@481104fd`): its comment `245` to `243` and back, not to the `247` a turn's summary in the same dark grey goes to; its words and strings as the highlighter's (`p5-inline-code-retint`, `p5-inline-code-retint-light`). A table keeps the colours it was drawn in, as upstream's does: upstream draws it again from cells styled when it was added (`store.zig:3758`, `block_render.zig:458@481104fd`), so its inline code stays `255` on a light screen and `235` on a dark one while the prose beside it follows (`p5-inline-code-table`, `p5-inline-code-table-light`). Both hold in a session whose terminal claims truecolor too (`p5-inline-code-retint-tc`, `p5-inline-code-retint-light-tc`, `p5-inline-code-table-tc`, `p5-inline-code-table-light-tc`): the presenter and the highlighter spell their greys `38;5;N` at every depth, as upstream's builtins are (`theme.zig:75-159@481104fd`), and a report answers such a cell as a 256-colour session does and repaints it in that notation (`a_direct_colour_session_matches_and_repaints_in_the_same_notation`), so an answer's inline code and its code blocks move as upstream's do, and a table keeps what it was drawn in. A tool group's row is the statusline grey with a command's words, strings, numbers and comments in the highlighter's, and upstream does not recolour it: it projects the group again in the new theme (`tool_group_projection.zig:558-640,742-784@481104fd`). In a truecolor session xfx's own greys on that row are `38;2`, so an indexed cell there is the highlighter's and moves as upstream's does (`p5-toolgroup-retint-tc`, `p5-toolgroup-retint-light-tc`). At 256 colours a comment drawn light (`243`, a grey no other role of the row holds) goes to `245` on a dark screen as upstream's does, but **a cell holding the statusline grey of the mode in force does not** (`p5-toolgroup-retint`, `p5-toolgroup-retint-light`, `p5-toolstop-retint`, `p5-toolstop-retint-light`): a string on a light screen (`241`, the light statusline grey) goes to `245` on a dark one where upstream's goes to `250`, and a comment on a dark screen (`245`, the dark statusline grey) goes to `241` on a light one where upstream's goes to `243`. A cell holds it whether it was drawn in that mode or a switch put it there, so a string drawn dark (`241` once light) and a comment drawn light (`245` once dark) join it on the next switch. The colour cannot tell such a cell from the statusline, and xfx keeps no group to draw again once its block has closed; the unit TG-REDRAW owns it. A cancelled call's own line is not projected again: upstream draws that entry's bytes (`tool_group_projection.zig:649-664@481104fd`), which a switch rewrites through its builtin token table at either depth (`store.zig:3784-3800`), and xfx answers that row by the same four pairs, so its argument (`245` on either screen when drawn) goes to `247` on a light screen and back, and a comment drawn light in it stays `243` (`p5-toolstop-retint`, `-light`, `-tc`, `-light-tc`). **Divergence, in notation only, decided 2026-11-07 to follow upstream (the unit TC-NOTATION):** in that session xfx spells its own greys -- the divider, the hint row, the card's rail, its notices, a turn's summary, a tool group's statusline grey, a stopped call's own line (its marker, `Cancelled` and its argument) -- `38;2;L;L;L`, the colours upstream writes as `38;5;N` on xterm's greyscale ramp, so that a terminal theme which remaps indices 232-255 cannot repaint them; upstream's truecolor claim reaches only a selected theme's diff markers and the Ultra mark (`render.zig:92-104,395@481104fd`), and its capture with `COLORTERM=truecolor` is byte-identical to its 256-colour one. A pair taken there differs in those cells' spelling and in nothing else (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/inline-code-truecolor/`). **Hyperlinks**: a link in an answer -- an inline `[text](target)`, a bare URL, an autolink, a URL in a code span -- in the document, in the full transcript and in a resumed session's replay, and an image badge, is an OSC 8 hyperlink spelt as upstream's (`ESC ] 8 ; id=fx-N ; <uri> ESC \` and `ESC ] 8 ; ; ESC \`, ids numbered from 1 per process as upstream's, the badge's with no id), and every link is closed on the row it opened on. A target is linked only when it is inert -- no control character of any kind, at most 2083 bytes, and in the `http`, `https`, `file` or `mailto` scheme -- and any other one is drawn as the link's text; upstream links whatever target an inline link names (`isValidLinkUrl`, `inline_render.zig:917-921`), so `[x](README.md)`, a `javascript:` and an `ftp:` target are links there and text here. Every other output path is unchanged: `ask`, `status`, `doctor`, the JSON and JSONL renderers and the line shell are plain text, and the only control sequence they emit is the erase pair `/clear` writes. |

### Full-screen TUI: a write the terminal took only part of

What the `full-screen TUI` row's partial-write sentence means, path by path, in preview-channel builds
only. `.prd/03-tui-port.md` item 21 keeps the evidence behind each reason, and none of it claims that a
terminal's own parser resynchronized on the fragment it was left holding.

| Write | When the terminal takes only part of it | Why |
|---|---|---|
| The band's frame on the normal buffer | Recovered in the same call: one fixed cleanup, then the band rebuilt whole | The band's rows are its own and the whole frame is in hand; nothing it writes enters native scrollback. |
| A repaint of the alternate plane a question already owns | Recovered in the same call: the same cleanup, then the surface repainted whole; the next write on that plane is forced to be a real one | No scroll, no plane transition and no native scrollback, and the surface shares its screen with nothing, so the whole of what it meant is in hand. |
| Taking the alternate plane, or giving it back | Contained; the session ends | After a torn `1049h` or `1049l` which buffer the terminal is showing -- and so who owns the screen -- is unknown, and no rebuild is right on both. |
| The update that carries the document's rows -- an append, a carry, a recolour or a collapse -- before the first of them | Closed by the same cleanup; the rows and the band are owed again, and the next frame is forced to be a real one | Only `?2026h ?25l` reached the terminal, so no cell moved; upstream retries a frame whose document append was not reached (`interrupted` is false, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:298-300` at `c1db919`). |
| The same update, inside its rows | Closed by the same cleanup; the session ends. A terminal that refuses the cleanup too is still holding the update open, and the exit's restore closes it: every restore leads with `?2026l` | Each row scrolls, and a row already carried into native scrollback cannot be taken back; upstream writes its cleanup and gives up the same way (`recoverPartialFrame`, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:648-689` at `c1db919`, `DocumentAppendInterrupted`), and its restores lead with the same close (`src/core/app/app_lifecycle.zig:41`, `:44`). |
| The same update, inside the band after its rows | The rows are kept; the band is recovered as its own frame is | The rows reached the terminal whole, and what tore is the band's frame, whose whole content is in hand. |
| A reset's scrollback handoff (`/clear`, `/new`, `/reset`) | Contained; the session ends | It scrolls, and a row already carried into native scrollback cannot be taken back. |
| The theme query | Contained; the session ends | Not a repaint: a prefix of it never completed the query and it moves no cell, so there is nothing to rebuild -- only a second asking, which would be a replay. |
| A program status report (OSC 7501) | Contained; the session ends | Not a repaint: it moves no cell, and a prefix of it is a string sequence the terminal is still reading; what would end it is the rest of a report, which would be a replay. |
| The recolour of the visible document rows, torn inside the update that carries it | Closed by the same cleanup; the session ends | The recoloured cells are in hand, but the rows they sit on can hold what the terminal had before xfx ran, which the band's own record of the screen does not, so an erase-and-rebuild would erase the user's document; offscreen native scrollback is never recoloured at all. |

## Embedding surfaces

| Surface | Kind | Status | Notes and upstream evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| CLI binary | embedding | implemented | The only supported entry point. |
| WASM core | embedding | deferred | `src/wasm_core_main.zig`, `src/wasm_term_main.zig`. |
| N-API module | embedding | deferred | `src/napi_core_main.zig`, `sdk/NAPI.md`. |
| Rust library crate | embedding | partial | The crate is public so tests can drive it, but its API is not stable and it is not published. |

## Upstream command aliases

The `command` rows above name them in their notes; this is the rule they
follow. It is kept at the end of the file so that it moves no row a line
citation points at (`scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml`).

**An upstream alias of a command is not a command of its own, and it does not
get a row of its own.** It is named in the notes of the row of the command it
spells, as `Alias:` and the backticked name (`Aliases:` and a comma-separated
list for more than one), and it shares that row's status. Every one today is on
a `deferred` row, so it is deferred with it: not a subcommand, not a clap alias,
not in help, and refused exactly as a name xfx has never heard of is.
`scripts/check-no-stubs.sh` holds those names to the rules a deferred row's own
name is held to, and to one row per surface, and refuses an `Alias:` on any
other command row; `tests/parity.rs` refuses one on any other row at all. An
alias xfx answered would be a clap alias, which neither the script nor the
parser's name list can see, so `tests/parity.rs` also runs each name through
the built binary and compares its refusal with an unknown name's. There are
two: `update` on `upgrade` (new in v0.0.16) and `balance` on `credits`.
